# Foreman — system map

Not loaded every session (`~/.claude/rules/foreman.md` points here). Read it when you're unsure where something lives or how the parts interact. `fm doctor` checks the file map below against disk.

**Contents:** [What it is](#1-what-foreman-is) · [Prompting](#2-how-to-prompt-it) · [File map](#3-file-map) · [Interaction diagram](#4-interaction-diagram) · [Lifecycle](#5-lifecycle-walkthrough-a-fix-line-to-an-archived-task) · [Precedence](#6-source-of-truth-and-precedence) · [Integrations](#7-integrations) · [BUILD_PROMPT](#8-relationship-to-build_promptmd-and-the-repo) · [Operations](#9-operations) · [Limitations](#10-known-limitations) · [Deviations](#11-deviations-from-build_promptmd)

<p align="center"><img src="site/assets/diagrams/loop.svg" width="880" alt="The Foreman loop: capture, expand, ground, plan, execute, verify, reflect, record, then the next queued task"></p>
<p align="center"><img src="site/assets/diagrams/architecture.svg" width="880" alt="How the pieces fit: agents call Foreman's hooks and the fm CLI, which keep state on disk; every view reads fm's JSON"></p>

## 1. What Foreman is

Foreman is a local discipline layer for coding agents: a small state CLI `fm` and one hook dispatcher, packaged as a Claude Code plugin (hooks, skills, always-on rules, read-only agents) and wired into Codex, Gemini CLI and opencode by `fm agents install`, with `fm code` running the same loop on any model. It runs every request, however terse, through one loop:

**capture → expand → ground → plan → execute → verify → reflect → record**

It keeps one central, current record per project (what's active, what's queued, what was decided, what was learned), refuses "done" without recorded evidence, captures new ideas instead of chasing them, resumes at the exact step after `/compact`, `/clear` or a new session, blocks dangerous commands in bypass mode, audits finished work through independent lenses before it counts as done, asks for consent in plain chat (never by making you run a command), brainstorms open-ended requests, and keeps working through the queue ("drive", optionally in full autonomy) until it's empty or it needs you. It's cheap: ~62 always-on instruction lines and ~1.3k tokens of skill/agent descriptions; hooks run at p50 ≈ 29 ms, p95 ≤ 33 ms (no `traceback` or `dataclasses` on the hook path: `fmcore.record` stands in for `@dataclass`, tracebacks load only on errors; `tests/test_core.py` keeps it that way).

## 2. How to prompt it

A plain one-line request gets the full treatment: Claude classifies it ("Treating this as FIX, tier S."), writes a brief, plans to the tier, executes, verifies with evidence and records.

```
fix the login timeout on slow wifi                       ← one-liner: classified and planned
FIX: login times out after 30s on slow networks          ← tagged
FEATURE: export report as CSV @src/reports #T-0012       ← @scope, depends on T-0012
CLEAN!: collapse the three date helpers into one         ← ! urgent (preempts)
PERF?: dashboard first paint takes 4s                    ← ? explore: options + recommendation, no code yet
SECURITY: review the upload endpoint
CONTEXT: Django app; don't touch migrations              ← applies to every item in the block
NEVER: push to main                                      ← CONSTRAINT / MUST / NEVER
DONE-WHEN: all tests pass and the CSV opens in Excel     ← block acceptance
SKIP: mobile layout                                      ← non-goal
```
Tags: CLEAN (REFACTOR, TIDY) · PERFORMANCE (PERF) · SECURITY (SEC) · FIX (BUG) · FEATURE (FEAT, CAPABILITY, CAP, ADD) · RESEARCH (SPIKE, INVESTIGATE) · CONTEXT (NOTE) · CONSTRAINT (MUST, NEVER) · DONE-WHEN (ACCEPT) · SKIP (OUT).

Order: BASELINE → RESEARCH → CLEAN → PERFORMANCE → SECURITY → FIX → FEATURE → FINAL VERIFY → REFLECT. Dependencies override it; a baseline-breaking FIX and critical security findings are hoisted.

Override words: `NOW:` (checkpoint, switch, offer to resume) · `PAUSE` / stop / hold on (checkpoint; drive pauses) · `RESUME` · `STATUS` · `FULL AUTO` / `STANDARD AUTONOMY` (whole message) · "that's for the current task" (your last message was a steer, not new work). Anything new while a task is active is captured, not started.

Plain words work too: Claude runs every command itself ("is foreman ok?" → doctor, "clean up" → tidy, "this repo is sensitive" → `fm sensitive on`). Open-ended ("super improve it"), exhaustive ("limitless", "every possible …") and broad ("a ton of …") requests go to `/foreman:brainstorm` (eight lenses, capability map and approaches among them; every idea cluster grounded and shown to you); a repo-wide cleanup goes to the `clean/repo-sweep.md` playbook. When Claude needs your consent for a guard category it runs `fm ask`, and Claude Code shows you its own permission prompt saying exactly what would be granted; approving grants it (only an approved prompt for that very call can), refusing refuses it, and nothing you type in chat cancels it. For ongoing work on Foreman itself, `fm ask ID core --standing` asks once for a standing yes that covers Foreman's own code, rules and evals for every later task (settings, state and the guard file still ask; like trust, it covers a finding that names one file by its real path, a relative shell write included, never a tree write or a target not known before it runs: T-0180); `fm standing` shows it and `fm standing off` revokes it. `/fm-trust on`, typed by you (the mod checks the prompt came from you), also lets Claude edit the guard and Claude Code settings until `/fm-trust off` or `fm trust off`; `fm trust` shows it, and Claude can never turn it on. Other questions come as an AskUserQuestion prompt. In full autonomy nothing is asked mid-run; what only you can grant is asked at the end.

Slash commands (Claude can start all but capture, tidy, doctor, design-extract, genesis and initiative itself): `/foreman:intake` · `/foreman:brainstorm` · `/foreman:next` · `/foreman:resume` · `/foreman:status` · `/foreman:capture <text>` · `/foreman:tidy` · `/foreman:doctor` · `/foreman:reflect` · `/foreman:improve` · `/foreman:playbooks` · `/foreman:debug-test` · `/foreman:release` · `/foreman:research` · `/foreman:refactor` · `/foreman:performance` · `/foreman:security-audit` · `/foreman:write-docs` · `/foreman:directions <sentence>` · `/foreman:ui-design` · `/foreman:design-extract [ui root | seed]` · `/foreman:build` · `/foreman:initiative` (user-only: map, find, prove and fix a repo's proven findings, stopping at a receipt, T-2123) · `/foreman:genesis` (user-only: drives `fm genesis step` from gen-0 to the report).

CLI (on the Bash tool PATH): `fm init|state|queue|next|resume|watch|ui [--json --follow]|projects [--json --follow]|session start|list|tail [--follow]|send|stop|rm|agents|claude list [--all CMD]|finish|prove [--run CMD|--hunks|--vacuous|--same CMD]|audit|log|done|block|drop [--done-in ID]|defer [--until DATE]|packet ID [--out PATH]|focus|checkpoint|sentinel|log|ask|decide [--kind --help`.

The procedure is enforced by the harness, not recalled: each task's stage (captured → planning → ready → executing → verifying → auditing → closing) is derived from its brief; `fm next` and every prompt, session start and Stop note name the one next required action and its procedure (`fmcore.SKILL_FOR`: the skill or playbook that fits, named once a session per hint, T-1697); `fm focus` refuses a brief that isn't planned for its tier; file edits inside a Foreman project are refused while no task is active (`fm task new … --ac … --step … --focus` starts a small one in one command).

### plugin/lib modules

One line per module, so two changes to different modules never touch the same line (T-1713).

<details>
<summary>every module, one line each</summary>

- `fmcore` (state, briefs, queue, intake, ledger, locks, audits; T-2015: `Brief.parse` keeps the text from the first `## ` unsplit until `sections` is read, and `ledger_events(since=…)` walks back from the file's end in 64 KB chunks to the first chunk of older stamps, so a hook's first load of 1199 briefs takes 32 ms, down from 74, and today's events take 2.8 ms, down from 30)
- `fmcli`
- `fmpy` (T-0368: below Python 3.12.7, fm and the statusline re-exec and the hook dispatcher runs a child under a supported interpreter saved in `state/python`, found once, re-searched by `fm doctor`; a crashing child leaves the hook to run itself, so the guard never fails open)
- `fmguard`
- `fmhooks` (incl. chat approvals)
- `fmtidy`
- `fmdoctor` (T-2281: `footprint()`/`record_footprint()`/`check_footprint(project=)` — the lean ratchet WARNs per metric (rules, descriptions, largest SKILL chars) more than 2% over the last `fm ship` record unless a later decision names it; the fixed caps still FAIL; also `fm wiring`, one screen of what is wired; checks the status views match the briefs, `--repair` regenerates them; with no `claude` on PATH the Claude Code-only checks — plugin validate, statusline, deny rules, rules symlink — pass as `NO_CC`, "no Claude Code here", T-2174; steer checks, T-2167: in a Genesis tower project a rule cited from a user steer with no `[check: …]` is a FAIL, so fm check stops a commit that leaves one unchecked; `pending T-NNNN` passes here while that task builds the enforcement; `--full` runs the checks)
- `fmwatch` (`fm ui --demo`: a canned view from `ui-demo.json` with every key a real one has, T-0962)
- `fmsetup`
- `fmideas` (tool-less brainstorm children with a coverage map and holes per round, `--falsify` kill tests, `--crossbreed`, reframing and provocation lenses, and per-lens keep rates in `fm taste` from each brainstorm's `ideas.json`, T-0684; and `fm oracle`: behaviour examples from a task's spec alone)
- `fmtaste` (T-1537: `fm looks`, the taste ledger in state/taste/looks.json: the twenty looks ranked by what the user kept, reverted, called "not like that" or picked in a 12-pair calibration, and learned only from local files the user names, read-only and capped; kept and reverted are recorded only when the model runs fm looks keep/revert)
- `fmmandate` (T-1400, the standing mandate): `fm mandate set "<request>" --until YYYY-MM-DD [--usd N]` keeps a sweeping request as one record in the project meta (its text, its `fm clauses`, the end, a recorded spend bound; `fm contract` enforces spend); `fm mandate` shows it, `off` clears it. While it stands, what `fm scout` (and so `fm night`) captures says it was found under it; once it ends, scout captures one RESEARCH decision keyed by when it was set (renew or let it lapse) and runs no probe until it's renewed or cleared. T-2186: session start shows a standing mandate's text as quoted data (an ended one: renewing it is the user's call, never the session's; `fm mandate off` clears it), and the brainstorm skill sets one for a sweeping request meant to keep standing, with the end the user named (none named: none set). T-2135, `fm goal [show|set|item|link]`: the mandate grown into a goal ledger. `set TEXT --until D | --no-end [--usd N] [--replace]` (one goal; `--replace` archives it to meta goal_history, the last 20; an ended goal is the user's to renew, so set, item and link refuse it); `item TEXT --done-when CMD [--source S]` (the check passes the guard as a stored command, T-0901); `link ITEM TASK` or `fm task new … --goal ITEM` (stamps `goal: N`; a task builds one item). `show` lists drift and exits 1 on any: open non-inbox tasks made since the goal with no item, items with no live task, items whose tasks all closed while the check fails (each guard-checked, 300 s each, 900 s a show). `fm mandate` on a goal shows it read-only (no checks, exit 0); a plain mandate prints as before. `fm mandate set`/`off` archive a goal with items. Dates print as the local day typed (`day()`). T-2137, `fm goal verdict [--json]`: `finished` (exit 0) only when every item has a done task and none open (dropped ones ignored), every done-when passes now (drift's guarded checks), every goal clause shares 2 words (1 if it has one) with an item's text, check or task titles and fm check's last `check_run` is green on this tree; else `stopped: <causes>` (exit 1); none stands: `no goal`, exit 2. T-2136, `fm goal plan [--apply --done-when CMD] [--json]`: proposes the contents list tagged user (one per clause), standard (tests, MASTER/README/CHANGELOG, site, pane, each surface, only what this repo has) and model (one haiku child, kept only when it names an existing path, found surface or fm verb, else "proposals (not added)"; none on a sensitive project), plus a checklist of the top 5 past misses (items added after a `goal_verdict` finished event, tasks reopened within 7 days of closing); `--apply` adds the non-duplicate ones. It stores meta `goal_verdict` {verdict, causes, at, tree, root, set_at} (dropped when the goal is set, replaced or cleared); Stop runs no check or git, reads it, and blocks a done claim once per cause set, goal and session when it says stopped for this checkout's standing goal (under a day old), counted toward DRIVE_MAX. T-2139: `goal_line(p)` (meta and briefs only, no checks) is the one line a standing goal with items prints in SessionStart (instead of the mandate text), the PreCompact checkpoint and `fm resume`: its quoted text, items done/total, the next item with its first open task; `fm run` puts that item's tasks first among the tasks it already accepts. T-2141: the same `goal_line` rides in fm code's `_context_note` (inside the run's data envelope), `fm export agents`' `## Goal` section with the stored verdict (omitted when none stands or the project is sensitive) and the MCP `goal` tool (in-process JSON of the goal, item states, line and stored `goal_verdict`; no check runs); `fmmandate.fresh_verdict(p)` (the goal stands, `set_at` matches, this checkout, under a day old) is the one test of a stored verdict for those, the pane and the Stop gate; the view model's `signals` carry `goal stopped: <first cause>` from it; the site's Planning page has the Goal to finish block.
- Upstream watch (T-1391, `fmscout`): a stale skill source whose remote HEAD (`git ls-remote`) is still its pinned commit isn't fetched; one that moved and changed its items is one RESEARCH finding listing what was added, removed and renamed (`index_delta`, a rename keeps its path) with its licence class; nothing installs. `probe_cc_changelog` reads Claude Code's CHANGELOG from the indexed anthropic-claude-code checkout and makes a finding for each bullet past `CC_TESTED` whose backticked names appear nowhere in Foreman's own text.
- Literacy and prompt variants (T-1403): `fm code runners --probe MODEL` scores a model profile 0-10 on `PROBES` (four JSON-only shapes, two data-in-an-envelope, two "unknown" over invented, an exact word, a sum; exact graders, no judge model); `fm code bench --variants base,evidence` runs the seeds once per prompt variant (`FM_PROMPT_VARIANT` adds `VARIANTS[name]` to the plan and act prompts) and reports each one's closed count, each run recorded with its `variant`. base stays the default until the numbers say otherwise.
- Offers (T-1397): `fm code` prints one `skill: NAME matches (class, ~N tokens)` note at a new run's start when one indexed skill from `fm lib`'s sources clearly matches the request (`skill_offer`: a name word, four points, a lead over the next); nothing is installed or loaded. `fm plugins suggest [--json]` lists up to three marketplace plugins not installed that fit the project's stack (`fmplugins.suggest`: the best `find` hit per detected card, the card and its evidence as the reason).
- `fmdojo` (T-1608: `fm dojo [--rounds N] [--seed S] [--quiz]`, a drill for people: commands from the guard's labelled corpus, about half refused; allow or refuse, then the guard's live verdict (`fmreplay.verdicts`, no grants) and the case's why; off a terminal or with `--json`, the drill as a study sheet with the answers)
- `fmdrill` (T-1669: `fm drill [--seed N] [--faults 3-6]`), a scripted day (status, hooks, task log, evidence, checkpoint, Stop) in a temp home with faults planted mid-day (torn ledger and journal, stale lock, broken lane, corrupt state JSON, an unreadable file); each must be found by fsck or verify-ledger or healed by the day, nothing may print a traceback, and the day's task must survive the repair; the report names the seed to replay; the home is kept only on a failure. Corrupt state and unreadable files end any verb with exit 4 naming the file, and hooks say so in one line instead of tripping the breaker.
- `fmoffice` (T-1406: `fm check office FILE`, a stdlib asserter for .xlsx/.xlsm and .docx deliverables usable as a verify command: no error cells (#REF!, #DIV/0!, …) or broken Word fields, `--cell 'Sheet1!B7=42'`, `--sheets N`, `--contains TEXT`; cached values only; members over 64 MB unpacked and any DTD refused (expat with the doctype handler raising))
- `fmimg` (T-1630: `fm img info|diff`, a stdlib PNG codec, 8-bit and below with all five filters, no Adam7 or 16-bit, and a changed-pixel count and box; `diff --tol N` counts a pixel within N on every channel as unchanged, T-1684)
- `fmshot` (T-1636: `fm shot URL|FILE...`, a viewport shot, frames in real time and a poster frame (the most painted) per page that moves, from a headless Chromium found, never installed, driven over --remote-debugging-pipe; `--frozen` is fm smoke's frozen profile; shots in state `shots/` or a non-link `--out`; no browser: exit 0 and why; T-1637: `--diff BASE` compares each viewport shot with BASE/<page>/viewport.png and passes within the baseline's noise floor, the most its own frames differ from its viewport shot (no frames: unmeasured; over 5% of the page: a --frozen warning), else fails naming the changed box; a font that visible text uses and that fell back per document.fonts, or fonts that couldn't be checked, fails it too; with --diff and no browser it exits 1; T-1642: `load(b, url)` opens a page, answering held requests while it settles, for fm shot and fm critique; `safe()` replaces every Cc/Cf/Co/Cs character)
- `fmcritique` (T-1642: `fm critique URL|FILE`, a computed-style census of the visible elements, every colour, font family, font size, radius, shadow and spacing value with counts, taken through fmshot's browser under fm shot's frozen profile by one fixed JS function, or read from a census .json, and critiqued against DESIGN.md's Tokens table: values no token names, near-duplicates under ΔE_OK 0.02 or within 7%, outliers used once or twice; fm flow's URL policy (files and localhost unless `--any-url`, every request checked); exit 1 on findings, 2 and "not run" with no browser or an unreadable `--design`; `--why SELECTOR` prints the element's matched rules winner first, with their stylesheets, and the winning declaration per property from CSS.getMatchedStylesForNode, capped; page text stripped of Cc/Cf/Co characters and capped; T-1644: `--bingo` scores nine tells (generic font, violet accent, Tailwind greys, one radius, one shadow, one gap, a flat or sprawling type scale, near-duplicate drift) on a 3x3 card off the census, exit 1 on a full row, column or diagonal; `--marks FILE` takes a screenshot with pencil-red strokes (r ≥ 170, g and b ≤ 100) shot again at its own size and diffed red-only, 16px cells joined 8-connected, or a JSON list of boxes, and captures one FIX per element under a mark (selector and text by one fixed JS function, quoted as page data, deduped on a hash of URL, box and note, scoped to a local file); `--brief [ID]` looks for the brief's quoted phrases (criteria less their verify commands) in the page's text and accessible names, exit 1 naming the missing; bad input exits 2 before any browser)
- `fmflow` (T-1639: `fm flow FILE`, a journey of goto/click/type/expect/wait steps run through fmshot.Browser as a verify command; selectors and text as JSON data to one fixed page function; goto and every request the page makes (Fetch domain) held to files in the flow's folder and localhost unless `--any-url`; exit 1 names the failed step, 2 with no browser)
- `fmraster` (T-1631: an integer rasteriser, edge functions, a 1/z z-buffer and flat shading, PNG through fmimg; no floats, so a frame's pixels match on every machine)
- `fmstories` (T-1640: `fm stories CHART`, a component's state matrix: a JSON chart of named states, each reached by fm flow steps (a relative goto keeps its ?query), shot through fmshot.Browser under fm flow's URL rule and request blocking, into one page of cells with inline PNGs and a CSP that loads nothing else; no browser: the states listed and why, exit 2 as fm flow's; capped states, steps and sizes)
- `fmscene` (T-1633: scene.json validated, `fm scene compile --to three`; T-1634: `--to component`, the same module plus an `<fm-scene>` element; T-1635: `fm scene cells FILE [--cols N] [--turn DEG]`, the scene as 24-bit half-block terminal cells; T-1847: `fm scene import FILE [--out PATH]`, three.js JSON (Object3D.toJSON) to a checked scene.json, nested transforms composed, what it can't carry named; T-1848: `fm scene metaballs [--balls N] [--grid G] [--seed S] [--out PATH]`, a seeded closed blob by marching tetrahedra; T-1851: `fm scene terrain [--days N] [--out PATH]`, the repo's churn as a ridge per busiest top-level path; T-1849: `fm scene airlock [--days N]`, the guard's blocks per category and its let-through tool calls as boxes either side of a gate; T-1850: `fm scene bench`, fm code bench's last 12 runs as a box per seed, green closed, red not, height the seconds; T-1852: `fm scene lookbook [--count N]`, the look cards hung in a 3D gallery, best ranked first, each palette as colour-field bands; T-1846: `fm scene shade FILE --out PNG [--size WxH] [--time S] [--set NAME=V,…]`, fmshade compiles a GLSL ES fragment subset to Python and runs it per pixel, derivatives from 2x2 quads, unset uniforms at preview defaults; T-1873: `fm scene gallery --out PNG`, `gallery()`: each built-in from `_builtin` (cmd_scene's builder) or `SAMPLES[action]` on ValueError/OSError, drawn unturned at 160x100 by `_frame`, tiled 3 wide by `_sheet` (turntable's tiler), a `GALLERY` caption per tile printed in order)
- `fmshade` (T-1846: a GLSL ES fragment-shader subset compiled to Python and run per pixel on the CPU for `fm scene shade`; derivatives from 2x2 quads, per-pixel and per-render step budgets, refusals name the line)
- `fmmission` (`fm mission`: the mission, surfaces, lenses and pack for an open-ended request, T-0375; T-2160: the mission doc ends with "## The user's standing rules (steers)", from `c.standing_rules`)
- `fmgenesis` (`fm genesis [status|init|put NAME|export DIR|check|step|draft [LINE]|spec|select N|seed N|drift N|sweep N|exam] [--file F|-] [--per-kind N] [--agent A] [--arm genesis|plain|oracle|empty] [--split holdout|train|all] [--seed N]`; `--per-kind` outside spec/exam and a draft LINE beside `--file` are usage errors (T-2101), T-2104: `fm genesis drift N` runs `drift(p, n)` (gen-0 §3.7), which checks gen-N's seed.md and mission-*.md: every unfenced `MUST:`/`NEVER:` line (`_constraints`; a code fence toggles, so a brief's intake block keeps its own MUSTs) is one of gen-0's verbatim after whitespace folding, and each file holds the string gen-0 or one of gen-0's constraint lines; only when that is clean, one tool-less cold read (`fmideas.run_child` feature genesis, sonnet, 180 s) of the seed against gen-0's goal line, MUST/NEVER and DONE-WHEN lines (plus the user's later steers in a `<user-steers>` fence: `decisions.md` rows tagged `cites: user steer` or `cites: the user's steer`, `c.plain`ed; a steer may only add to gen-0, T-2114), whose `DRIFT:` lines count only when a seed line (a wrapped bullet joined whole) holds the quote (≥ 8 chars, `c.plain`; drift.md records the seed's line, never the reader's words); fails closed (only a `CLEAN` line with no stray quote passes; a reply naming lines the seed lacks, or neither, is unread), the seed's own `</seed`/`</gen-0` are defanged so it can't close its fence; verdict clean, drifted or unread (a read that didn't run is no pass) into gen-N/drift.md, dated when the check began so an edit during the read reruns it, exit 1 unless clean; T-2184: `check(p)` also returns `reach(p, n)`: each fm verb gen n's missions name (a real subcommand of `fmcli.build_parser()`) must be named by a session path (`_session_text()`: the operating rules, skills and commands whole, plus the string literals fmhooks.py and `c._next_for` can print, `_literals`: comments and docstrings don't count, nor (T-2196) a set display, a matcher such as STATUS_WORDS, or a `cmd_*` body, printed only to a session that already ran that verb), or (T-2186) run by one: a verb whose module (`fmcli.lazy` sets `run.module`) a named verb's module imports, one level read with ast (`_imports`; fmcli and fmcore import everything, so they reach nothing), else a `✗ reach` problem, also in fm genesis check's exit code and the sweep's genesis check row; `scorecard` returns `gaps` = `free_beats(p, agent)`: per run, a free capability arm (`fmexam.AGENTS` but oracle and empty) whose newest line found more held-out defects than the agent's newest line there, printed first as `top gap:` by fm exam scorecard; `seed` opens What this generation is for with `Top gap:` lines from `free_beats(..., "train")` (held-out results never reach a Mission). `sweep(p, n, agent)` (gen-0 §7) needs gen-N/seed.md, then writes gen-N/sweep.md with one ✓/✗ line each for genesis check, steer checks (T-2167: every unsettled user-steer rule has a `[check: …]` and it passes), mission tasks (closed, or blocked with a reason, archived briefs included), tasks (no planned/active/verifying task but the active one running the sweep, no blocked task without a `blocked: <reason>` log line, `_block_reason`), lanes (a worktree branch not an ancestor of HEAD, unless its task is blocked with a reason: queued for the user), docs (`fmdocs.scan`), doctor (`fmdoctor.run_all` FAILs), gates (`fmcli._cached_pass` on this exact tree: read, never rerun), privileges and exam (a graded line for gen N); step stays on drift or sweep until its file says `Verdict: clean`, and reruns drift when the seed or a Mission is newer than drift.md; both log `genesis_drift|sweep` and kick the live site; T-2102: `select(p, n, agent)` compares gen n's `closed.train` ids (fm exam grade lines carry `closed` {train, holdout} and `gen`: `current(p)` at `fm exam run`, stamped in the answers dir's `gen` file so a regrade keeps it; n < 1 is refused; select and seed hold `c.lock(p.dir/genesis-lineage)`, not p.dir, which put holds) with gen n-1's on the newest shared run, each side the intersection over that run's lines: a reopened id or no more closed → revert (seed base = the parent's), +2 or +1 over 2+ lines → keep, +1 on one line → `rerun` (writes nothing), no parent line → baseline; held-out ids are never read; the verdict is a `## gen-n` section in lineage.md (replaced on a rerun, ending `- seed base: gen-K`; T-2171, steer 1 rule 4: a `- provisional:` line names the exam run, agent and graded lines it rests on, until the end-of-run exam confirms or reverses it) plus `fmcli._write_decision`; `check()`'s `lineage` part (`marks()`) names each `## gen-N` section with no provisional, confirmed or reversed line, which fails fm genesis check's exit and the sweep's genesis check row (`_bad(r)` collects every part). `seed(p, n, agent)` (n ≥ 2, never overwrites; the Exam section reads that agent's lines; critique asks lose their `|` lines so none joins the Missions table; a gen-0 base carries no text, since the seed points to gen-0) builds gen-n/seed.md from lineage's gen-(n-1) seed base: a Missions table of `coverage()` `rows` ranked by ideas + empty + bad (≤ 8, ties in file order, the rest named), every empty cell, every not-yet item, gen-(n-1) critiques' "What the next Seed should change", the newest train score per gen, the lineage verdicts, then the base seed's own sections minus the ones seed writes (`MADE`) and table lines; step's select/seed actions run them. T-2078: the Genesis tower's home, `p.dir/research/genesis`; T-2191 (steer 7): `intent()` reads `plugin/templates/standing-intent.md`, the user's standing intent (one source, nine numbered clauses; `intent_clauses()` parses them once as [head, rest]): `_charter` fences it as `<standing-intent>` after `<user-steers>` (`_fence` escapes the tag), READ_CHILD asks what the user would say and fails a line a clause rules out, DRIFT_CHILD counts such a Seed line as drift, `seed()` and spec.md carry a `## Standing intent` section (in MADE, never carried twice), the export's `intent` field (the parsed clauses) fills the site's rules, `draft()` fills `{{intent}}` under the DONE-WHEN line, `_oracle` carries it and `_misses` adds `standing intent: no clause '…'` per clause whose whole line (whitespace-normalised) the draft lacks (closed only; given drops it, no writer is handed it); `drift(p, n)` from gen 2 fails a seed without a `## Standing intent` section holding the file verbatim. T-2099: `draft(p, line)` fills `plugin/templates/genesis.md` from `facts(p)` (name, languages by tracked-file count, top-level docs, README head defanged, `checks` gates, the last 3 ledger corrections) and one tool-less `run_child("genesis")` that names only context lines and §2 capabilities with exam kinds (no child → the fixed 9-capability FALLBACK); the line's own MUST/NEVER/CONTEXT/DONE-WHEN lines go in verbatim, open briefs' quoted MUST/NEVER lines carry over unless `cover()` finds them already said (a non-user brief's marked `unconfirmed`, ≤12); `cover(ref, draft)` lists each ref MUST/NEVER line no same-tag draft line shares 60% of its 4+-letter words with, then each numbered section missing or under half its title words; `spec(p, per_kind)` maps each §2 line to exam kinds (its `Exam: a, b.` suffix, else KIND_WORDS), runs `fmexam.gen` in the project's languages and files spec.md (rows: kinds, scenarios, held out; unmapped capabilities named); T-2095: `step(p)` names the tower's one next action from its files alone (gen-0 → spec.md when §2 maps to an exam kind (T-2100) → newest seed's rows in order: mission doc, its `Task:` ids closed, else `intake` until one id per intake brief line, critique → a `## gen-N` lineage section → gen-N/drift.md → gen-N/sweep.md → the next seed, or report.md after two seeds with no rows), read-only so a kill anywhere resumes; kind `fatal` (exit 1) for more than 8 seed rows, a seed with no rows that doesn't say "no Missions this generation" (T-2119: a bullet list or a header-only table isn't a dry generation), or a `Task:` id that isn't a task, `blocked` for a blocked brief, `done` once report.md is filed; also `next` in `status` and the export, and `fm next`'s idle path names it while a tower is open; T-2093: `check` (`check(p)`, also in `status` as `check` and in the export) enforces the gen-1 standing rules: `coverage()` wants one `## <area>` per row with all `STAGES` × `SURFACES` cells as `- <stage> × <surface>: F: <path> | I: <idea> (<file>) | N/A: <reason>`, F/I paths existing inside `p.root`, no templated cell (T-2159: `_twins` flags a cell that reads the same as another slot's in its surface column (the area or stage swapped in) or as another surface's in its own slot (the surface swapped in), same words or 4+ matched at a difflib ratio of 0.6 or more after `_words` drops the trailing (file) and the area's own name; both twins are flagged, except that of two F cells only the later copy is (one shipped path may serve two areas, counted once); a flagged cell isn't filled, adds to `templated` and names its twin; a non-F cell under 3 words besides the area's name is too short; empty-cell problems list last), and a row for each gen-0 §2 capability (`_caps`); `not_yet()` wants each item's `- blocker:`, 2+ `;`-separated `- tried:` approaches, `- unblocks-with:` and an `- added:`/`- retried: gen-N` for the newest seeded generation, and `## Removed` lines only for harm or a duplicate; exit 1 on any problem; `init` writes gen-0.md as the bytes given, chmod 444, refused when present or when it holds a secret; `put` files only names matching `SHAPE` (gap-map, lineage, catalogue, report, not-yet, coverage .md; gen-N/seed.md, gen-N/mission-X.md, gen-N/critique-X.md, N from 1), redacted; it refuses the stage gates' own records (`OWNED`): exam.jsonl, which only `exam_line` appends (fm exam grade, fm genesis exam), and gen-N/drift.md and sweep.md, which only `fm genesis drift N` and `sweep N` write (through `_file`), so a hand-written line or a forged `Verdict: clean` can't pass the scorecard, `select` or a drift/sweep stage (T-2155); T-2166: every other put (by hand, `spec`, `select`'s lineage.md, `seed`) first runs `_read`: one cold Haiku child (`genesis-read`, `READ_CHILD`) sees `_charter` (gen-0's goal, MUST/NEVER/DONE-WHEN and the steer decisions; `READ_CHILD` reads DONE-WHEN as the whole run's finish line, so a file that says what is still open passes and one claiming done without evidence fails), `_exam_tail` (the newest graded exam.jsonl line per run and agent, up to 20: found, false alarms, fixed, held out, scenarios fully closed or closed on the given score with their held-out count) the findings of earlier failed reads of the same file (T-2205: `<previous-reads>` from `READS`, p.dir/genesis-reads.json, the newest `READ_KEEP` 40 per name, cleared on a pass; T-2245: sections split past SECTION_FLOOR logical lines or SECTION_CHARS (3000), and only a section under SECTION_TINY (300) characters joins the read before it, up to SECTION_GROUP (1500); T-2243: past SECTION_FLOOR logical lines with 2+ sections, `_read` reads per section group (`_parts`, `_groups`: short sections merged, at most 12 reads a put), keyed `name#<hash>` (never the heading text), with an outline of the other sections' headings and first lines; a group's pass hash (reader, charter, exam tail, outline, body) skips an unchanged one, stored only when nothing was carried; findings pool from the whole-file and every name# entry, and orphan keys are pruned on a pass; T-2163: `select` writes gen-N/select.md (owned: put refuses it) and `step` passes select only on its `Gen: N` line, not on a lineage heading or a ledger event; T-2236: `_named` splits a quote on a literal \n; every non-empty piece must name a line (the 8-character floor applies to the longest), the union keys acceptance, and its 4th element, the last piece's lines, is what a finding pins to and what DRIFT counts; T-2231: `_logical` joins a wrapped paragraph's next line (headings, table rows, fences and blockquotes stay apart); T-2229: `_named` returns each quote as matched, and a stray one is printed in its own words; T-2228: a previous read's finding follows its line to the closest current one (`_successor`, difflib, 80% alike: distinct coverage rows run to 77%) and ends once none is, one finding per line, the re-key saved, so the reader isn't shown lines the file no longer holds; quotes match with backticks, asterisks, backslashes and curly quotes dropped and an ellipsis cut (`_norm`); T-2207: `ACCEPTED`, p.dir/genesis-accepted.json (state: the guard refuses direct writes), per name a key of READ_CHILD, the charter (gen-0, steers, standing intent) and the exam tail, the lines the last read left unflagged (`seen`) and those two reads in a row left unflagged (`lines`), each hashed with its nearest heading (`_context`): a FAIL on an accepted line prints as advisory (`~`), doesn't block and ends its acceptance, so a second fail blocks; a changed or moved line, an earlier fail still in previous-reads, a quote that also matches an unaccepted line, a new steer, reader prompt or exam line all block as before; a long file converges while every line still gets a whole-file read; `genesis_read` events carry `carried` and `carried_lines`; `READ_CHILD` re-checks each, fails an unmet one again, doesn't repeat a met one, fails a line doing what an earlier read asked only on a gen-0, steer or intent breach or metric gaming, and still looks for new problems; only findings on a line the file holds are kept, one per line with its newest why, and the file is reloaded before the write so a concurrent put of another file keeps its own) and the `c.redact`ed file, each `_fence`d; only a plain `CLEAN` with no `FAIL:` files it, a `FAIL:` line the file holds (`_named`, shared with drift) refuses with that line and the reader's `WHY:` (`c.plain`, 160 chars), and a stray quote (its `WHY:` still shown as `? a line … doesn't hold — reader: …`), no verdict, a read that didn't run or no gen-0 refuses too; the OWNED records aren't read (fm's graders write them), and the steers come from `fm decide --cites` rows the session itself can write, so the read is a check on the writer, not a guard (same trust as T-2114); nothing is written and a `genesis_read` event logs the verdict; `--file -` reads stdin; status: gen0, gap_map, lineage, not_yet, coverage, exam_lines, generations [{n, seed, missions, critiques}]. T-2080: `export` writes each doc (gen-0 plus the SHAPE .md files, home shown as ~, `c.redact`) under DIR plus `genesis.json`: capabilities from gen-0 §2 (`CAP`), each capability's gaps with their `(N)` tiers from gap-map's `- Gaps:` bullet (`_gaps`, `_tiered`), per generation the seed's mission table (`ROW`: n, name, caps, gaps, depends), each mission's written/critiqued state and the tasks its doc names (`_task`: steps, done, current step), the seed's standing rules, the last 200 exam lines, coverage cells (`- area × surface: F|I|N/A: …`), the not-yet count and the docs. `site/genesis/` (index.html, genesis.css, genesis.js, no libraries) draws it: the tower, capability cards, ambition scale, exam table, rules and docs, each opening a drawer (deep links `#doc=`, `#cap=`, `#mission=`, `#task=`); T-2090: `live on [--task ID]` checks `ls-remote origin main` (refused before spending: the grant is kept), spends one publish grant (`fmrelease._grant`/`_named_grant`, `_spend`) and sets meta `genesis_live` {remote, since, task}; already on, it returns the state and spends nothing; `live off` clears it. By design one grant opens publishing until `live off` (data-only pushes to main, path-checked), not one push; `kick(p)` (after put/init and in the Stop hook after `sweep_detached`) touches `p.dir/genesis-publish.pending` and spawns a detached `fm genesis publish --settle` unless one already waits (a marker older than `LIVE_EVERY`+600 s is a dead waiter's, ignored; no spawn under `FOREMAN_NO_BACKGROUND`); `--settle` sleeps `_wait(p)` (the rest of `LIVE_EVERY`, 300 s, since `genesis-publish.last`'s mtime, touched when a publish reaches the network), then removes the marker, so a change inside the window goes out when it ends and a change after that schedules the next; `publish` (one at a time via a `genesis-publish` lock, 120 s wait under --settle; waits out fm ship's test run) exports to a temp dir, compares genesis.json minus updated/rev with the last hash, then fetches main into the private ref `refs/foreman/genesis-main` (never FETCH_HEAD, which another fetch can rewrite) and compares there in a scratch worktree, commits only site/genesis, refuses a commit whose diff from that base touches anything outside site/genesis, and pushes `sha:refs/heads/main` when they differ; a failure is recorded `failed: …` (`c.redact`, `c.plain`) and the next kick retries; status `live` {…, last, pushed, hash}. Site data reaches main only this way: never commit site/genesis data on a branch fm ship cherry-picks from; T-2105: `step` returns kind `stopped` when lineage.md's last three generations all start `- reverted` (the rewrites stopped closing scenarios), and `fm next` then says "queue is empty; Genesis stopped at gen N" instead of driving it; a new gen-N+1/seed.md written by hand (`fm genesis put`) lifts it, and `fm genesis seed` refuses while it holds (T-2110); the export adds each generation's drift and sweep verdict, which the site's level 4 card and drawer show) T-2112: `fm genesis exam` grades #13 itself: `exam(p, arm, split, per_kind, seed)` draws `fmexam.draw(seed, GEN_KINDS, LANGS, per_kind)` (`GEN_KINDS` line, none, sweep: mission-genesis.md's G-line, G-none, G-sweep; held out by default), and `_ask(s)` derives from the scenario id its request (`REQUEST`: "make this great" plus one planted `PLANT` MUST and NEVER for line, nothing for none, "super improve this" for sweep) and the defect kind `fmexam.render` plants in its repo. Each draft runs in a throwaway `c.Project` over that repo with an empty state dir, so this project's briefs and gen-0 never reach the writer; `_misses(chk, text, kind, defect)` is `cover` against gen-0 plus the planted lines, a §2 capability whose `_kinds` (the shared 'Exam:' suffix or `KIND_WORDS` reader `spec` uses) holds the defect, a `CONTEXT:` line naming what it assumed (none; `draft` now adds `CONTEXT: No goal was given, so this assumes: …` when the line has no goal) and no §2 capability without a kind (sweep). Arms: genesis (`draft`), plain (one `fmideas.run_child("genesis-plain", PLAIN, …)` told only the format), oracle (`_oracle`: every line the checks read) and empty, the scoring's bounds. Drafts and their misses land under `fmexam.root(p)/genesis-s<seed>/<arm>/`; one `exam_line` (the append `fmexam.grade` shares, then `kick`) files agent `draft-<arm>` with miss counts and the run's genesis spend. `closed` is strict; `given` drops the misses in `cover(ref, text)` (gen-0's own lines and headings, which no writer is shown), so arms compare on what they were handed; a draft whose source is fallback (genesis) or error (plain) counts in `errors` and closes neither; the none check wants a `CONTEXT:` line saying no goal (or request, or nothing) was given and what it assumes; an arm outside `ARMS` raises; one stderr line per scenario shows progress; `fmexam.summary` prints "(N on what it was given)" and the cost as ~$ (matched by feature, project and time).
