- `fmexam` (`fm exam [status|gen|run|grade|check|scorecard] [RUN] [--seed N] [--per-kind N] [--lang L,..] [--kind K,..] [--split train|holdout|all] [--agent oracle|empty|finders|foreman|plain|genesis] [--author self|child] [--model M] [--limit N] [--budget USD] [--request TEXT]`, T-2079: a seeded exam under `p.dir/exam/<run>`; each scenario is a one-commit git repo in python/js/ts/ruby/rust/c with one planted kind (bug, vuln, untested, slow, docs, flaky, dep) or a request shape (vague, ambiguous, missing-tool, none), its key.json and hidden checks beside the repo, never in it; `holdout()` keeps (n+1)//3 per kind; `check` is the exam of the exam (each hidden check fails on the planted repo and passes on the clean render, the clean suite passes); `run` calls an agent then `grade`, which scores found/false alarms/fixed/tests-first/readings/missing-tool per split and appends a line to the Genesis exam.jsonl (then `fmgenesis.kick`); `fm genesis` shows the last line via `summary()`; the finders arm is Foreman's deterministic finders (fmdocs.scan, fmsecrets) with no session; `--author child` has a tool-less child (`fmideas.run_child`) write the bug/vuln plants from the module and tests alone, rendered through the template's harness under a `child` test key and kept only when `_check_one` passes them, else listed in run.json `dropped`; the `foreman` and `plain` arms are claude sessions via fmbench (`_seed` + plugin dir, or FOREMAN_QUIET + `_bare_guard`) in a temp copy of the repo outside p.dir (the guard blocks edits there), one at a time, kinds interleaved, `--limit` defaulting to 3, `fmbudget.check`/`record` under `exam-arm`, the reply's last findings JSON parsed by `_reply` and its cost summed into the grade; T-2192: sessions run with `keep`, and a reply with no parsed findings object (`_answer`, which `_reply` shares, so broken JSON counts as none; a session that fixed things, then ended on prose) gets one resumed turn (`AGAIN`, same for every arm; a scenario can spend up to twice `--budget`), both turns' cost summed, the answer keeping `reasked` and `said` (the first reply's redacted last 400 characters), the grade counting `reasked` (the exam line's `N re-asked`); a session error counts under `errors`; `_run` strips `fmbench.CREDENTIALS` from the env of the model-written code it runs; `grade` refuses unknown agents, scores only answered scenarios, and the line carries the `split` and `kinds` it covered and the `author`s) T-2112: session arm `genesis` (G-tower) seeds Foreman like `foreman` and asks for one Genesis generation on the request (`GENESIS`: draft, init, then `fm genesis step` until select); `--request` (a session agent only; any other raises) replaces each session's scenario request (default for genesis: "make this great", set in `run_agent` and recorded too), is saved as `answers/<agent>/request` and lands on the grade's line as `request`. T-2120: the README states one edge contract every language's clean app and planted variants keep (page n or size < 1 empty, clamp lo wins when lo > hi, label s itself when wider and the C one takes a capacity, dedupe any value, total of none 0, query the SQL quoting), each clause asserted by the visible and clean suites; the README's build command keeps `&&` unquoted. An unkeyed finding whose `test` (a file inside the arm's work tree, at most 10 a scenario) fails on a copy of hidden/clean and passes on the arm's tree was an `exam_defect`; T-2170: the arm wrote both the change and the test, so it stays a false alarm, counted apart as `proved_by_arm` (the exam line, the genesis cold read's exam tail and the site's exam table: `N false alarms (K with the arm's own proof)`) and listed in `proved_by_arm_findings` for reading (old lines' `exam_defect_findings` meant defects); `exam_defects` stays 0 on new lines (the clean suite already asserts each contract clause, so an outside-confirmed one would be a generator bug). T-2126: capability #1's kinds: `none` (no request; the child plants one bug, the template adds an untested function, never the planted one, and a docs slip), `clean` (no request, nothing planted; any finding is a false alarm) and `rank` (no request; a vuln, an untested function and a docs slip, key `rank` tiers `[[vuln], [untested, docs]]`); `_in_order` gives `rank_hit` only when every keyed defect is found and each tier is first named before the next, and `_closed` needs `rank_keyed == rank_hit`; keys without `rank` grade as before. T-2140: capability #4's kinds (`GOALS`): `goal` (the request is a vague goal, `REQUESTS['goal']`, never the child's report; under `--author child`, T-2149, the tool-less child writes the whole key: `_author_key` asks for 4 to 6 items (`AUTHOR_KEY`), each a code gap (a function shipped without some handling, plus the test that catches it: a run check `checks/codeN`, its overlay `planted/codeN`), a missing test (its visible test removed: a static check on test files) or a README line with its regex (a static check on README.md), at least one of each; a reply under 4 items, two code or two test items on one function, or a regex that is bad, repeats a group (catastrophic backtracking), misses its own line or matches another README item's is dropped with its reason, and `_check_one` drops a regex the planted README already matches; `_keyed` renders it and resume ticks every third item met; the template's items keep the self-authored goal kinds) and `resume` (the same goal with the `DONE` items, vuln and docs, already met in the repo and ticked in its PLAN.md, the open ones unticked; the request is "continue"); a done item's check must pass on the planted repo (`_check_one`) and counts as `broken` when the arm's tree fails it; on a goal kind findings neither score nor alarm, and `false_done` counts a done claim with an item unmet or broken; `_closed` needs no broken item; `summary` names false done and broken. Genesis maps a §2 capability that names a goal or resuming to the goal and resume kinds, and its fallback spec gives them to Goal to finish. Initiative words map to `none`, false-alarm or clean-repo words to `clean` (Calibrated judgment's fallback kind) and ranking words to `rank` (T-2131). T-2097: `grade` and `fm genesis exam` file the held-out ids they graded (`answered: {holdout}`) beside `closed`; `fm exam scorecard [--agent A] [--bar 0.8]` (`fmgenesis.scorecard`) is gen-0 §8's scorecard: per §2 capability (`_caps`, `_kinds`) the newest graded result of each held-out scenario of its kinds from that agent's lines (default `foreman`; lines without `answered` are skipped, regrade them); T-2204: only held-out scenarios an outside author wrote count (grade records them as `answered.child`; a template-written result is counted apart as `selfmade` per row and overall, shown on the Genesis line, fm exam scorecard and the site's exam rows, and never erases a child result for the same id; a child id of a kind CHILD_KINDS lacks is ignored), a capability none of whose kinds is in fmexam.CHILD_KINDS is `no outside author` (T-2215: #13 has one through `fm genesis exam --author child [--model M] [--lang L,..]`, where a child writes what the drafts read (the project's README in its own words, keeping every function name and command, and G-line's MUST/NEVER pair: AUTHOR/LINES, `_vet`), kept per run and scenario under `authored/ID.json` so every arm grades the same input (`_author_project`, re-vetted on load); the planted defect stays Foreman's, which no draft reads; a failed child call keeps the template, left out of answered.child; r and the summary count the child-written ones, spend is the project's, and the line carries the author's model (a Haiku author is a smoke signal)) and one with only some is `partial` (its `why` names the rest), never pass; dep's author prompt says its function's own tests fail (KEEP); CHILD_KINDS gains slow and dep (the one-function plant: a big-input test that times out, a lazy import of a package that isn't installed); T-2211: and untested, clean, docs and flaky, each its own reply shape (`SHAPED` prompts, `_author_shaped` validates, `_shaped` renders): a new function with its test held back (untested) or kept (clean: novel code, no defect), exported in JS/TS and declared in C's app.h; a false README line with its correction and must/never regexes that tell them apart and miss the README as it is (docs); a test body that passes by chance with its fixed body and a never regex, added beside the function's own test and the clean suite run twice (flaky); child regexes may hold no repeated group and at most two open-ended repeats (`_regex`), C gets a declaration only from a plain signature and Rust a `pub`; a malformed reply drops its scenario with the reason; T-2217: and vague, ambiguous and missing-tool (`ASKS`): the child writes the request, the case-insensitive regexes a good answer matches (exactly 2 readings, neither matching the request; 1-2 missing, which must name what the request names) and an example answer that matches them all, which the oracle says; no regex may match the module, its tests, its README or a filler answer (FILLER); `_guard` refuses a regex that matches the empty string, counts ? among open-ended repeats and catches overflow, and the template renders the code (vague keeps its slow plant); every exam kind now has an outside author, and a capability with a kind no child-authored result covers yet reads `partial`; T-2225: so does one with a language of `fmexam.LANGS` it has no graded result for among the languages spec.md's header drew (all when none; ids are kind-lang-hash, `_lang` skips odd ones; a `--lang python` run is `partial`), the row's `langs` lists those covered and its `why` names the rest and `fm exam gen --lang …`; T-2216: three more kinds give gen-0 #2, #9 and #11 an exam (KIND_WORDS maps them by anchored words): `exists` (a request for what a function already does: graded on naming it as already there, and `broken` when the answer's app defines more functions than the clean one by the language's DEFS pattern; a finding on it is no false alarm), `parts` (three defects named in one request: a proxy, PROXY says on #9's row that delegation isn't visible to the grader and the long-horizon tier decides) and `trigger` (tests to run on a schedule: a static check over TRIGGER_FILES only (CI workflows, timer and service units, crontabs) for a schedule plus a test command; the clean render holds .github/workflows/nightly.yml; `_static` skips links and non-regular files); each child-authored (exists: the child picks the function and writes an example; trigger: the request, no tool named; parts: three plants rendered through `_keyed`), a capability naming genesis scored on `draft-genesis` lines (GEN_KINDS); state `pass` (≥ 3 graded and rate ≥ bar), `under`, `too few` or `no exam kind`; the rate is over graded scenarios, not every held-out one; a bar outside (0, 1] is a usage error and an agent with no graded lines is named; exit 1 unless all pass. T-2158 (steer 1, Haiku never decides): `run_agent` stamps a session arm's model in its answers dir (`model`, `c.plain`, ≤ 60 chars; `default` with no `--model`) and `grade` copies it to the line; `fm exam grade RUN --agent A --model M` fills in answers from before the stamp or stamped `default` (the configured model), refuses to rewrite a named one and refuses a non-session agent; `summary` names the model; `fmgenesis.smoke(x)` is a line whose model names haiku, `_exam` gives an unstamped line the stamp a later line of its run carries; `scorecard` skips smoke lines and returns and prints their count (`smoke`), `select`'s provisional line names each line's model (`: a smoke signal`); `free_beats` still reads them (a gap is a building signal) and labels a gap `(from a Haiku smoke run)`; select still counts them by steer 1 rule 4 (provisional), the done gate never; `_exam_tail` (cold reads) names a row's model (`: a smoke signal`) and the site's exam table tags a Haiku row `Haiku smoke, not counted`. T-2179 (gen-0: no spend cap on proving): a session arm gets no `--max-budget-usd` unless `fm exam run --budget USD` names one (`fmbench._session` omits it on `None`, and the over-cap check skips); the timeout stops a runaway and `fmbudget.check` (the user's own `fm budget` cap) still guards the run. Test-first grading (T-2203) puts back only the defect's lines (`putback`: per clean-to-planted changed region it anchors on the unchanged lines before and after it, each found once and in order in the arm's file, and swaps whatever the arm has between them for the planted lines, so a differently worded fix still applies); a region at the file's edge anchors on the edge; a missing, non-unique or out-of-order anchor credits no tf-first, and only a red that isn't a load error counts (`_caught`). T-2233: each graded line carries `recall` {lang: {L: [found, keyed]}, kind: {K: [found, keyed]}}, overall and in `holdout`/`train`, and `summary` prints both axes.
- `fmfind` (`fm find [PATH] [--rank] [--capture N] [--json]`, T-2121: deterministic, language-agnostic finders over a tree (python/js/ts/ruby/rust/c), no model calls; exit 1 on findings, 2 when the scan itself fails, each a candidate until a failing test proves it. Walks `git ls-files` (os.walk fallback when git lists nothing, as in a folder the parent repo ignores), skipping hidden, vendored, venv, build and fixture dirs, files over 1 MB and binaries; generated files (`fmmap.is_generated`) are read for secrets only. Safe on an untrusted tree: regular files only (no symlinks, FIFOs or devices), git run with fsmonitor and hooks off, names decoded byte-safe, lines over 2,000 chars treated as data, malformed manifests skipped; the repo's code never runs. Finders: untested (public functions no test reaches: test words, then a fixpoint through each function's own body, nested defs included, so a CLI test that runs a `go` verb reaches `cmd_go`; a module is flagged only when its name is unsaid and none of its public functions is reached), docs (backticked `name(` spans in prose, flagged only when most spans name defined code, so a card about another library stays quiet; plus `fmdocs.scan`), sql (a parameter concatenated or interpolated into SQL), slow (a list built, then a membership test in a loop over a parameter), flaky (unseeded randomness in tests, outside strings), deps (imports a manifest doesn't declare; guarded and in-string imports skipped), secrets (`fmsecrets`, test paths skipped). `--rank` (T-2125) scores each lead severity × reach × its finder's precision (reach is 1 plus up to 0.5 for the other files naming the symbol, so it never lifts slow or docs past a vuln) and sorts worst first; `--capture N` (N ≥ 1, inside a project, else exit 2) files up to N new leads as discovered tasks with meta `finder` and `find_key` (finder, file, symbol, message and twin index, so two leaks in one function are two tasks; a dropped or reverted key is never refiled, a done one that comes back is; tasks from a tree outside the project carry confirm). `precision(p)` is (Σw·real + 5) / (Σw + 10) over closed tasks tagged finder, each weighted 0.5 ** (age / HALF_LIFE 180 days) so a mute lifts as its drops age: dropped is a false alarm, done is real unless fmoutcomes saw it reverted. A finder under FLOOR 0.3 is muted and a score under BAR 0.1 held back: listed last, never filed, and not counted toward exit 1. T-2132: score also x effort `max(0.5, 1 - span/320)` of the lead's enclosing function (1 with none) x `PROOF` 1.25 while fm prove's proof still holds (`record` writes `find-proofs.json` {find_key: `proof_hash` of the function's own source} in the project state dir; an edit elsewhere keeps it, a change to that function drops it), a proven lead's score stays 2% under every unproven lead of a higher severity kind in the run (its own unlifted score, if higher, stands); no lift for a file with no text; trailing blank rows aren't hashed. Shared by fm find --capture and scout's `probe_find`: `keyed(found, root, home)` yields each lead's find_key and `settled(p)` the keys never filed again (open, dropped, or fixed then reverted). `fm exam run --agent finders` runs it ranked as an exam arm and must stay at zero false alarms on every clean render. Ceilings: only column-0 public functions; dispatch by computed string reads as untested; inline Rust tests count the whole file as tested; top-level code is attributed to the def above it) T-2184 (steer 2026-10-30 #3: on held-out run s31-006 the foreman arm found 6/18 where fm find alone found 16/18): session start (startup or clear) with nothing active, queued or paused, in an unattended run or a repo Foreman hasn't worked in (`c.worked_in`: no closed brief, nothing archived), adds `fmhooks._leads_note`: fm find's ranked leads, held ones left out, at most `LEADS` (8), each row a JSON string of `c.plain`ed, shortened repo text (data, not instructions), each a candidate to prove by kind (T-2192: a bug or vuln with a failing test, an untested one with a test that calls it, a docs one by making docs and code agree, each reported under its own symbol; s31-006's three misses were untested leads shown and dropped); past `LEADS_FILES` (400 in the listing fm find reads: `git ls-files -co --exclude-standard`, fsmonitor and hooks off; fm find took 3.5 s on Foreman's 2,010 files) or a scan past `LEADS_SECONDS` (1.5 s, a daemon thread) the `fm find --rank` command instead; fm next with an empty queue in such a repo names `fm adopt` (the baseline) and `fm find --rank`, as does the leads pointer there (T-2186), and the operating rules say an unfamiliar repo or no request starts there. T-2237: no host package lookup (`_dists` gone; `PY_ALIAS` maps import names to distributions); Python string lines come from tokenize (STRING and FSTRING/TSTRING spans), with the quote counter as the fallback for a tokenize error or a line over 2000 characters. T-2235: tracked dot-dirs are read (untracked ones skipped, from a second `ls-files -o` listing); a file whose realpath isn't root/rel (a symlinked parent) is skipped; `capture` keeps at most `OPEN_CAP` (5) finder tasks open (`open_finders`, counting fm find's, fm prove's and the scout's) and defangs the title and raw whole, evidence included, as fmscout's capture does; `filed_line` says when the cap stopped a filing. T-2238: `fm find --hotspots PATH [--json]` / `fmfind.hotspots(root, n)` rank tracked regular files by how many of the last 500 non-merge commits with a whole-word fix/bug/crash/regress/broken subject (or a revert quoting one) touched them (git via `_git`: fsmonitor, hooks and gpg programs off, 60 s timeout, None on failure so the CLI exits 2; `--relative -z` so a subdirectory or odd names work; renames followed to the current name; not with --rank/--capture), the input for the bug rung. T-2241: `--capture` (and fm prove's) saves each finder's flagged lines per file to state `find-flags.json` (newest 2000 files); a closed FIX's changed lines (git diff from `task_base`, own files, no tests or docs) with no flag within 3 lines become `blindspots.jsonl` records (best effort at `task_finish`), `fm find --blindspots` lists them and `--capture 1` files at most one "add a rule, tool or check" task a day (`blindspot-day`). T-2239: `--ladder` is the tool rung (`ladder(root, p, sandbox=False)`, `TOOLS`: ruff, go vet, gosec): argv is the registry's, fixed in code; a manifest file (regular, never a symlink) only selects an entry. It runs only when `fmprove.trusted(root)` or the caller passes `sandbox=True` (an argument: no tree file or env var can set it; nothing passes it yet, an exam arm may); else it prints "tool rung skipped: not a trusted tree". Each run: shell=False, abs path from a PATH cut to absolute entries outside the tree, a stripped env (no credentials, FOREMAN_/CLAUDE), cwd the tree, 120 s timeout (group killed), 8 MB per stream (polled; the run is killed past it, no rlimit: it would hit the tool's own compiler); SARIF/JSON/line-regex output becomes `tool:NAME` findings (paths kept only inside the tree, 500 a tool), so T-2125's per-finder precision learns them; a selected tool off PATH files one `capability-miss: NAME` task via `fmeco.capture_once` (the T-2270 key). The ladder also runs a tree-sitter rung over Foreman's own `.scm` rules in `plugin/lib/ladder_rules/<lang>/`, behind an optional `tree_sitter` import: it parses tracked, size-capped, non-symlink files (no repo code runs, so no trust is needed), reports `ts:RULE` findings, and files `capability-miss: tree-sitter` when the binding is absent (T-2240).
- `fmlong` (`fm exam long gen|run|grade [RUN] [--from REPO] [--n N] [--agent oracle|empty|foreman|plain] [--faults kill,pause,compaction,misleading]`, T-2258: the long-horizon exam tier. `gen` replays past `(T-NNNN)` task changes from a trusted local repo (`fmprove` trust, rechecked at grade): each goal is the base commit with no later history, hidden checks are the test ids the change added (red at base with its test files laid over, green at target; 1..5 kept) and a regression sample that passes at base and at target. Tests run in an isolated runner (`python -I`, stdlib first, script outside the tree, process group killed on timeout; a runner failure drops the goal, never reads as all-red). `grade` counts every scenario in the denominator (unanswered = not done), and lays the window's test files plus changed same-folder support files over the answer. Zero-token arms oracle and empty, `model` null (T-2262: `foreman`/`plain` run a headless claude a goal in a temp copy like `fmexam._session_arm`, the model stamped at run and read at grade; `--faults` plants `kill` (first turn capped at `KILL_T` min, resumed by its `--session-id`), `pause` (stop at a checkpoint, `PAUSE_GAP` s, resume), `compaction` (a 20k auto-compact window, compactions counted) and `misleading` (an always-red decoy test) and logs each to `exam/long/RUN/faults/AGENT/ID.json` for the metrics slice; `--limit` defaults to 3 goals, `--timeout` minutes a turn; on Foreman's own history the plain arm can read `plugin/` in its work tree: no Foreman tooling, but not code-blind), one `tier: long` line via `fmgenesis.exam_line`. T-2263: each session goal also stores `faults/AGENT/ID.run.json` (known cost + `cost_unknown`, last reply's `claims_done`, wall secs, guard blocks from the session's own events, ended_ok); `grade` adds per goal and per run `rate`, `false_done` (said done, hidden checks red), `resumes_needed` (a failed planned resume, or an end in error: a person would step in) and `unfinished` (ended with no done claim and no error: stopped short, no intervention; each None when the run didn't store the flag), `hours_to_done` (fastest true-done goal's session time: the tree is checked once, at the end), `guard_blocks`, `cost`, `cost_unknown`, and `unknown` counts; a metric the run can't compute is `None`, never 0 (`reverts` always None: the final tree keeps no history). `fmgenesis.long_row` (in `scorecard()["long"]`, `fm exam scorecard`, `fm genesis status`, genesis.json `long`) is the deciding row: Foreman's vs plain's newest long line on the goals both answered, `ahead|level|behind` by true-done, a Haiku line gives `smoke`; `behind` fails `scorecard.ok`. Ceiling: nested fixture dirs aren't laid over)
- `fmprove` (`fm prove [PATH] [--limit N] [--yes-run] [--capture N] [--only ID] [--json]`, T-2122): fm find's candidates become proof tickets or leads, best first up to `--limit` (10), within `DEADLINE` (900 s; the rest stay leads). T-2242: `--bugs [N]` (3, at most 10; a trusted tree, never a sensitive project) adds `bug_candidates`: one tool-less haiku child per hottest file (fmfind.hotspots, else the largest source files) names up to 5 `LINE: what is wrong` lines, kept only when strictly shaped and inside a function, and each is proven by `_written` or stays a lead. Bug candidates go first in the proving order (`--limit` grows by their count); their keys omit `says` (the model's wording changes every run, so settled and refutations still match); a proven one's test is saved in state (`prove-tests/<proof_id>.<ext>` + `.json`, `_keep`) and its task's criterion is `--only bug-ID`, which `_recheck` reruns on a fresh copy (red open, green fixed, missing or crashing never fixed; exit 1). Repo code runs only on a throwaway copy (`_copy`: symlinks, sockets, FIFOs and .git/node_modules/target/venvs left out; a failed copy removes its folder; `_sweep` clears copies a killed run left over a day old) with `_env` (fmbench.CREDENTIALS and FOREMAN_/CLAUDE_/DBUS_/XDG_/GPG_/PYTHONPATH/NODE_OPTIONS/RUBYOPT dropped, HOME the temp folder: environment isolation only, the code still runs as the user with the network), `RUN_T` 300 s a run, and only in a tree `trusted()` (T-2202): one the user ran `--yes-run` in (the guard's USER_ONLY_FM, so a session can't; `remember` keeps the tree's realpath in state_dir()/`prove-trusted.json`, which is state-direct to anything but fm; a failed write is reported and the run goes on). Anything in the tree, an author email included, is only a claim, and so is any environment variable (no Foreman-home exception: FOREMAN_HOME is a session's to set). The yes is per path, not per version of the code, and governs Foreman's initiative, not a session set on running the code (it could run the tests directly). Untrusted, `prove(run=False)` still proves the cold kinds and gives each untested candidate `NO_RUN` as its lead. `_pick` takes the first suite (fm map's test gates, then the test files run as scripts by python3/node/ruby) that is green untouched and runs a control (a function the tests name that fm find didn't flag). untested: `_instrument` makes the candidate and the control create marker files as their first statement (py via ast, re-parsed; rb the next line, never an endless def; js/ts/rs after the body's `{`, refused before a `;` or a destructured arrow parameter; c unsupported), and the candidate is proven when the suite stays green, the control's marker appears and the candidate's doesn't, so a swallowed error can't fake a proof; its marker appearing is a lead naming that a test runs it. docs/dep/secret: re-found on a fresh copy by the same deterministic check (the tree holds it, not its surroundings). vuln/bug/slow/flaky: leads until a failing test shows them (model-written proofs: T-2199). Repo text in the output is quoted; exit 1 when one is proven. fm find itself never runs repo code; fm next's idle line, the session-start leads note and the rules name `fm prove`. T-2123: `--capture N` files up to N proven tickets as planned S tasks through `fmfind.capture(proof=)` (keyed like fm find --capture, so neither files one twice); each criterion is `fm prove <tree, relative to the checkout> --only <proof_id>` (sha1 of the find key, hex: no repo text reaches a shell), exit 0 only once the finding is gone from fm find or shown fixed (a test runs it; not re-found cold), 1 for any other lead (a red suite, no run allowed, the deadline), so only a shown fix is green; a lane re-proves its own copy on the yes the user gave the main checkout's tree (`_lane_trusted`); provable kinds (COLD + untested) take --limit slots first; nothing proven writes research/prove-receipt.md with found, held and tried counts; the user-only /foreman:initiative skill chains fm map, fm find --rank, fm prove --capture and fm run. T-2199: when run is allowed, flaky leads are proven by 6 fresh-copy reruns that both pass and fail with a failure line naming the test (`_flaky`); vuln, slow and bug leads by one haiku child's test (`_written`, written only into the copy, credentials stripped, 20000-character cap) that's red 3/3 on an FM_RED assertion and changes under both a raise-stub (a runtime-built sentinel; a stubbed file that doesn't parse stays a lead) and a benign-return stub; a sensitive project gets no child test. T-2237: each suite run's timeout is `_t(end)` (the seconds left, floored at 1, at most 300); a COLD verdict's evidence says it's a re-find by the same check. T-2235: `--yes-run` is remembered only after the call's arguments validate.
- `fmdemo` (`fm demo`: the real fm CLI and guard fix a seeded bug in a throwaway repo, no model calls, nothing left behind; `--pace SECONDS` pauses per stage and types the commands out, colour on a terminal or FORCE_COLOR, T-0902; doubles as a smoke test, T-0870)
- `fmplaybooks` (`fm check playbooks [ratchet]`, T-1505: the playbook library's lint — index coverage and dead index lines, SKILL.md's budget, shared titles, 5-word-shingle Jaccard over 0.6, a Use-when line, a numbered procedure, `fm lib show` names, python blocks run with `python3 -I` in an empty folder under memory, process and file-size limits unless marked no-run (trusted like the repo's tests: hygiene, not a sandbox), a token ratchet in `skills/playbooks/ratchet.json` that fails past 10% + 50 tokens of growth — and a reach report from events.jsonl's Read events; a lane runs `python3 plugin/bin/fm check playbooks` from its worktree)
- Playbooks from this repo's own work (T-1523, `fmplaybooks`): `fm playbooks draft ID` (`draft`) turns a done task's brief into a draft in the library's shape (Use-when line; its steps numbered, each with the last `exit 0` run that proved it after a dash; criteria as checks; lessons and plan gaps as pitfalls; files touched); `fm playbooks mine` (`mine`) lists each file changed in 5+ of the last 400 non-merge commits (sweeps of 30+ files skipped) with the files beside it in 60%+ of them; `fm playbooks counter` (`counter`) lists commits git reverted and tasks blocked with a reason, newest first; a name that matches no playbook gets up to three close ones (`_near`, difflib or substring: "did you mean fix/alpha?", T-2009) from `fm playbooks show` and `eval`; `fm playbooks show` puts "Look here first" above a playbook when up to three of its numbered lines share two or more words with the active task's failed runs and plan gaps (`look_first`, JSON `look_first`).
- Learning from playbooks (T-1525, `fmplaybooks`): `fm playbooks score NAME --task ID` (`score`, the dojo) marks each numbered step followed when the task's steps, evidence, approach and log share two or more of its telling words with it (words at most an eighth of the library's playbooks use); `fm playbooks kata NAME [--answer FILE]` lists the playbook's held-out briefs (`plugin/evals/playbooks/<stage>/<name>.json`) as practice and scores an answer against each one's checks (`passes`); `fm playbooks lesson [RANGE]` (`lesson`, `lesson_text`; one commit means its own change) lists up to 12 commits, the folders changed, two lessons from each task its commits name, and the review checklists the files call for (`REVIEW_BY_EXT`). The site's Playbooks page (`site/pages.py` `_field_guide`) lists every playbook by stage with its Use-when line, under tiles counting each stage.
- Shader sketchbook (T-1784, `fmscene`): each shader look card has a `## Sketch` line, its preset in the last backticks (`uName=1 uColour=0.1,0.2,0.3`, `sweep` for a uniform no clock drives); `sketch` parses it, `uniforms` reads the GLSL's float and vector uniforms, `sketch_cards` reads every card, and `sketchbook_html` lays one canvas per card with the shader as a non-executed script element and the card's description as a fallback shown until the shader runs; `SKETCH_JS` compiles each with a full-screen-triangle vertex shader, draws one still frame, and animates only visible canvases unless the reader asks for reduced motion. `fm scene sketchbook [--out FILE]` writes the page; the site's Shaders page (`site/pages.py` `_sketchbook`) is the same fragment.
- 3D checks offline (T-1530, `fmscene`): an animation may target `camera` (position only; no mesh may be named camera), and `pose` re-aims the camera at `camera.target` every frame, so a camera path replays as the demo; `fm scene compile --hud` (`compile_three(hud=True)`, `HUD`) calls `hud(renderer, fig)` after each render: an overlay with frame rate, draw calls, triangles, geometries and textures, the same numbers on `globalThis.__fmScene`; `fm scene check FILE` validates and counts (`faces`: three.js's triangles, a sphere's empty pole faces dropped); `fm scene turntable FILE --out PNG [--frames N]` (`triangles`, `turntable`) puts the scene through fmraster from N turns about +Y (T * R * S, look-at, depth negated and two vertices swapped so front faces stay front) into one contact sheet, no browser or GPU; `fm scene shader PATH` (`shader_problems`, `shader_paths`) checks each glsl code block of a shader card or GLSL file has a main, compiles under glslangValidator when it's on PATH (stage from gl_Position), and declares exactly the uniforms the card's ## Uniforms table lists. `fm scene check X.gltf|X.glb` (`gltf_problems`) checks a glTF offline: the GLB header and chunks, asset.version 2.x, every index in range, each accessor inside its view, each view inside its buffer, each buffer as long as it says (BIN chunk, base64 data URI, or a file beside it; a URI leaving the folder is a problem, never read). `fm scene soak FILE [--seconds N] [--budget-ms MS]` (`soak`, `soak_problems`) serves the `--hud` build, the page `SOAK_PAGE` and a three.js build (`find_three`: FOREMAN_THREE, else node_modules/three/build above the scene or cwd; r170+'s three.core.js copied too) from 127.0.0.1, mounts it in fmshot's headless Chrome (SwiftShader; the page, never the module, drops failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat), samples `__fmScene` once a second, calls `dispose()`, and fails on geometries or textures that grew, a p95 render time over budget, a scene that never drew, or one not back at its poster; with no browser or three.js it says why and exits 0.
- Divergence (T-1536, `fmtaste`): `fm looks tournament [LOOK…] [--n 2|4|8] [--answers a,b,…]` (`bracket`) seeds a single-elimination bracket with the named looks or the best-ranked (1 v last, 2 v second-to-last), replays the cumulative answers, prints the matches left and the command to continue, and at the champion records one `tournament` event whose matches score like a calibration's (winner picked +1, loser passed -1); `fm looks opposite LOOK` (`opposite`) ranks the other looks by palette distance (nearest colours both ways over the RGB diagonal) plus tag distance; `fm looks book [LOOK…] [--n N] --out FILE` (`book`) writes a script-free lookbook page of titles, lines, swatches and tags. The directions skill's step 4 says when to diverge (three lanes, a tournament, opposite day, a lookbook, `references/idioms.md`: five historical idioms of one layout); `fm design diverge [N] [WHAT] [--out DIR]` (`spread`, `variant`) writes N variant briefs in looks far apart (the best-ranked, then farthest-point by `distance`, the measure opposite ranks by) and `fm design diverge pick LOOK` keeps the winner; and `/foreman:looks` asks tournament matches through AskUserQuestion. A blind multi-runner taste panel was left out (decided: paid model runs to answer what the user's own picks answer).
- Taste follow-ups (T-1672, `fmtaste`): `fm looks roll` draws the look of the day (weight e^(score/3), seeded by the date, so the same all day while the ledger holds); `fm ui`'s `look` field and the pane's idle row show it once the ledger has taste. `fm looks bisect` splits the ledger at the signal where the leading look changed most (4 signals at least). `fm looks seance` lists the departed (score below 0) with their last words and the nearest look that lives on. `fm looks history [PATH]` reads DESIGN.md's git history (200 versions at most, 60 s; a stint cut by that window is dropped; a keep said by hand during a stint counts once) into stints: a look named ≥7 days is kept, replaced sooner is reverted, each stint recorded once (source FILE@sha), so a rerun records nothing. Decision: DESIGN.md is read by default as Foreman's own record; another path only when named.
- Design sprint (T-1538, `fmdesign`): `fm design sprint [ROOT]` (`sprint`, `sprint_text`, `SPRINT`) checks five stages in order and stops at the first that fails, naming its next step (`SPRINT_NEXT`): brief (DESIGN.md's ## Point of view, with no `VAGUE` word), direction (## Direction names `audacity safe|bold|wild`), tokens (a ## Tokens table with a row), build (UI files under ROOT, `lint` clean), critique (`check_gates` has gates and every must passes); exit 0 only when all five pass. `fm design vague TEXT` (`vague`) lists each sweeping word with the concrete question to ask. `fm task receipt` adds `swatches` (`fmdesign.swatches`): hex colours and transition/animation/duration/delay durations on the + lines of the UI files' diff since the task's base (a new untracked file: all of it), 12 of each at most, shown as one Swatches line. Left out (decided): nudge keys on a live dev server, a gamut-cube colour picker, a nightly design residency and a weekly design PR.
- Site build and stories (T-1541): `fmcodetui.snapshot` draws at t=0, so `site/build.py` is byte-reproducible (a test builds twice and compares every file); `build.case_study_html` turns each `EXAMPLES` run into `stories/<name>.html` (its card, then each verify, step, changed and closing event at its time since the start), linked from the card; `pages._wall` adds a cover per release to the Releases page (hue from the version, bar from its tests over the latest's); `fm task card ID [--out FILE]` (`title_card`, `write_card`, `HAND`) writes a proved task's 1200x630 SVG title card (hand-lettered face stack, nothing embedded or fetched), and `fm task finish` writes it to the project's `cards/<id>.svg` when the close is proved red then green. T-1785: a playbook index line naming a folder (`references/ui/examples/`) covers every file under it; `pages.gallery()` reads each UI playbook's `Example: `references/ui/…html`` line into (name, title, use, path); `build()` copies each to `gallery/NAME.html` (a missing file stops the build) and the `gallery` feature page links them; `test_site_gallery` keeps every example passing `fmnew.check` and `outside_loads`.
- Plugin parity (T-1822, memory cc-plugin-parity): `tests/test_plugin_parity.py` maps each `COMMAND_LABELS` command to its Claude Code route (`PLUGIN_ROUTES`) or an app-only reason (`APP_ONLY`) and resolves every route against fm's subparsers, `plugin/skills|commands` and a few CC keys.
- fm code app extras (T-1546, `fmcodetui`): F (`photo`, `App.take_photo`; the loop keeps `App.size`) writes the frame on screen to `foreman_home()/photos/frame-<time>.svg` and, with rsvg-convert (`_png`), its PNG; `snapshot` writes a PNG for a path ending .png. `fm code tui --chaos SECONDS [--seed N]` (`chaos`, `_chaos_event`, `HOSTILE`, `CHAOS_COMMANDS`) builds a headless App on an empty temp root with every source stubbed, then floods it with hostile events, presses only read-only commands (text in the prompt and search; Enter there becomes Esc), resizes from 1x1 to 220x70 and renders a frame after each; a crash reports its step, mode, size, error and trace with the seed, exit 1. Left out (decided): the isometric layout builder, golden frames against a terminal-emulator oracle, springy draggable panels, a wear-in UI.
- Receipts and review (T-1547, `fmcli`): `fm task receipt ID --svg FILE` (`_blueprint`) draws the receipt as a blueprint sheet (grid, files in reading order with +/− bars, UI marks, the swatches it added, before/after, a title block; everything escaped); `--sign KEY` (`_sign_receipt`, `RECEIPT_NS` foreman-receipt) writes the receipt text beside the SVG (or the project's receipts/<id>.txt), signs it with `ssh-keygen -Y sign`, writes an allowed-signers file from the key's .pub and the repo's user.email, and prints the `ssh-keygen -Y verify` line. `fm audit prep` adds `_past_nits`: ledger `finding` events on the same files (by their file, or a path their text starts with; rejected ones and this task's left out), newest first, 12 at most, as the brief's pre-review checklist. Left out (decided): a process cast, a client packet, a receipts GitHub Action, a cold-reader model test.
- Session and release care (T-1549): `fmideas.user_voice` keeps each quote once (by its words, whatever its prefix); `fmrelease.bad_hour` reads the ledger's `check_run` events by local hour and, with 20 runs in all and 5 in the hour, flags one whose gates failed 40%+ and at least twice the overall rate: `fm release` (dry runs too) warns, never stops; `fmsettings.defaults()` and `changed_defaults()` compare the schema with `plugin/defaults.json`, which `fm release`'s changelog step diffs into "Default changed:" lines under the new version and rewrites (the commit step adds it); `fm settings changed [SNAPSHOT]` marks each change yours (your files set it) or not; `fm digest --say` (`fmcost.say`) speaks it with the first of say, spd-say, espeak-ng or espeak. Left out (decided): a teleprompter pane, a near-miss gallery, per-dependency decision cards.
- Scaffolding and dev loop (T-1554): `fm new NAME DIR` into a folder that doesn't exist yet (not a panel) runs `fmnew.born`: git init, fm init, `fm check add "fm new check FILE"`, a first commit ("Born from fm new NAME") and fm check, each step reported (JSON `born`, `green`); an existing folder only gets the file. `fm dev [--once] [--interval S]` (`fmnew.cmd_dev`) polls the mtimes of git's tracked files and runs `fm check --affected` on a change; --once runs it now and exits with its code.
- `fmcodemod` (`fm codemod rename OLD NEW | sub PATTERN REPL [PATH…] [--apply]`, T-1554: an identifier-boundary rename or a regex over git-tracked UTF-8 text files of 1 MB at most (binary, non-UTF-8 and symlinked files left alone), previewed as a unified diff (40 lines a file) and written whole only with --apply; the deprecation playbook's first step points renames here)
- `fmextras` (`fm extras zine|vase [--events FILE] | cursor X,Y X,Y … [--out FILE]`, T-1556: `load` reads a recorded run's JSON lines, `digest` what it asked, planned, checked, did and changed; `zine` imposes eight `pages` on one letter sheet (`IMPOSE`: the top row upside down, the cover bottom right, the cut dashed); `vase` turns `profile` (a ring an event: a failing check pinches, a passing one swells, smoothed) into a closed ASCII STL wound outward; `cursor` eases an arrow along arcs with a ripple at each stop (SMIL); `Breakout` is the game fm code's `B` opens while `fmcodetui.checks_running`, closing when they stop and handing back every key it doesn't play)
- `fmmarks` (`fm marks icons|empty|wordmark NAME|fonts [--out] [--stroke W] [--subset FONT --text TEXT]`, T-1555: `ICONS` (stroked paths on one 24 grid, 2 in from each edge) written with one shared attribute set and a `sprite` of `<symbol>`s; `EMPTY` states drawn from those paths at 3x with the stroke scaled back; `wordmark` escapes the name and takes its hue from it; `shelf` marks `PAIRINGS` against `installed()` (fc-list, None when missing); `subset` runs fontTools' pyftsubset when importable, else says the recipe; stdlib only)
- Build what's missing (T-2278): `fm new tool NAME [DIR]` (`fmnew._scaffold`, `templates/scaffold/*.tmpl`) writes `tools/NAME.py` (argparse, `--help`, exit 0/1/2, first lines `# fm new tool`) and `tests/test_NAME.py`, which fails until the tool works; `fm new skill NAME [DIR]` writes `.claude/skills/NAME/SKILL.md` with a "Use whenever" description of at most 125 characters. Names are validated, all files are checked absent (symlinks too) and created O_EXCL, never overwritten (no `--force`); `fm new check` runs a tool's `--help` (exit 0) and a bad option (exit 2), and checks a `SKILL.md`'s frontmatter; T-2270's capability-miss note ends "or build it: fm new tool NAME".
- `fmnew` (`fm new [list] | NAME [DIR] [--force] | check PATH... [--run]`, T-1519: strict-by-default templates — `web` (`templates/web/index.html`: tokens, light and dark, local faces with a voice, a CSP refusing other hosts), `scene` (the playbook's own `ui/templates/scene.html`) and `panel` (`templates/tui/panel.py`, landing in fm code's `tui/` folder); a copy is created O_EXCL, so nothing there or appearing meanwhile is replaced without `--force`, which replaces a symlink itself rather than writing through it; `fm new check` runs design lint on a page, and a panel check runs the file: only one fm code would load (yours, writable by no one else) and one under a `.git` outside fm code's `tui/` only with `--run`; it runs in a child through `fmplaybooks._run` (limits, `CHECK_SECONDS`, a process-group kill) whose verdict counts only on a line with a per-run token, so sys.exit, os._exit, a swallowed exception or a hang never pass: setup against a recorder, handlers on field-less events, each panel at four sizes before and after them — at most h lines of at most w columns, theme styles only, under `PANEL_BUDGET` over `PANEL_STRIKES` frames)
- `fmdesign` (T-1645: `fm design check [ROOT]` runs the gates DESIGN.md's `## Gates` opts into, advisory by default and blocking when marked; `trial` (T-1669) runs advisory and logs each new would-block in the project's design-trials.jsonl (a rerun on the same code adds none), `--wrong GATE [--why]` marks the latest one wrong (a self-report, like editing DESIGN.md), and it blocks once 10 are logged with under 10% of the last 20 marked wrong (demoting by the same rule); outside a Foreman project it reads as advisory; the brief gate finds each Knobs colour, quoted type family and duration, and each Direction `- must:` line, in the UI files; the a11y gate (T-1646) proves the role tokens' contrast pairs (shadcn's --foreground/--background names too; blocking with no pair checked fails), takes the lint's reduced-motion, flash and img-alt findings, and wants a :focus or :focus-visible style that shows (outline, box-shadow, border, background, colour or text-decoration with a visible value, or a Tailwind focus: variant) wherever there are interactive elements; the debt gate (`- debt: advisory 40`) counts untokened colours, durations and radii per file through the lint's parse, passes at or under the budget (none given: 0) and lists the top files; `fm design lint PATHS [--json]`: text rules over .css/.scss/.html/.jsx/.tsx/.vue/.svelte for the tells of generated design — a default-face font stack (also next/font, Google Fonts links and Tailwind font-sans), the purple-to-blue gradient, one 6-16 px radius, one grey shadow (CSS, SCSS variables and Tailwind classes, @apply included; runtime-built values never count as equal) — and craft slips — img without alt, motion with no prefers-reduced-motion path, animated layout properties, flashes over 3 a second, Math.random in render, literal durations beside duration tokens; run-wide rules look across every file linted, `design-lint: allow RULE why` silences one line, exit 1 on findings; T-1874: it then names the `## Tokens` rows of the repo's DESIGN.md (or `--tokens FILE`) no linted file uses (`unused_tokens`; a Tailwind theme name counts as used by its class), report only, silent without a table; a worker of fm code may run it, T-1506; T-1507: tokens beside it, OKLCH maths by hand — `fm design palette SEED [--format css|dtcg|tailwind]` makes the type-and-colour roles in light and dark from one colour, each text role moved in lightness until its pairs pass (body text 7:1 in dark), export on stdout and proof on stderr; `fm design contrast FILE [fg/bg[:need[,dark]]]` proves a token file's pairs (`var()`, `light-dark()`, .dark/[data-theme]/prefers-color-scheme blocks) on the 8-bit hex with WCAG 2.x luminance, exit 1 on a failure, 2 on a token it can't read, and runs without fm as `python3 fmdesign.py contrast`; `fm design extract PATHS [--out FILE|-]` writes the :root/html/:host/@theme custom properties and literal tailwind.config colours as a table, with each role pair's contrast, into DESIGN.md's `## Tokens` section (repo root, or docs/DESIGN.md), replacing only that section; `fm design tokens DESIGN.md --to css|app|pane [--light|--dark]`, T-1651: that table (columns by its header's names) compiled to `:root` custom properties (dark values under prefers-color-scheme: dark and `.dark, [data-theme="dark"]`; a value with an open bracket or quote, `; { } < \ @`, `url(` or a comment falls back to its hex, else is named), a tui.toml `theme` + `[colors]` fragment for fm code (roles from token names — bg/surface, text/ink, muted (a texty name first), primary/accent/brand, success, danger/destructive, warning, border, raised/panel/card; a `-foreground` name is text, never its role — onto the preset's styles; the preset paper or night by the screen colour's lightness unless --light/--dark; checked with fmsettings' own load and faint check: a style the app would skip is left out and named in a comment, as is a preset style too faint on the new screen) or the pane's `C` palette as JSON (the mod doesn't load it yet); workers may run palette, contrast and tokens, not extract; `fm design roll [--seed N] [--apply|--undo]`, T-1648, in `fmroll`: a seeded pick of one look card, its palette roles, type pair (the seed picks each class's lead face, never a generic one like Inter) and motion together, printed, or written as DESIGN.md's `## Look` (card name beside the seed) with the bytes before the first roll kept in the project's state (a JSON record and a plain `.orig` copy), which --undo restores only while the file is what the last roll wrote; a symlinked DESIGN.md is refused; T-1652: `read_design` also reads a W3C design-tokens or Figma variables `.json` (`json_tokens`: light/dark groups or a mode named dark are the themes, aliases resolved, depth-capped), `tokens --to brand --out DIR` writes favicon.svg and a 1200x630 card.svg from the accent, bg and text roles, `--to md [--out FILE]` writes the tokens as a `## Tokens` section, `infer PATHS` proposes repeated untokened colours, durations and radii as rows (and names the literals a token already holds), and `xray PAGE [--tokens F] [--out PNG]` outlines each painted element green on-token or red off in a headless shot, exit 1 on any off; `plugins/foreman-design` is the design skills as an optional marketplace plugin, symlinks into plugin/skills)
- `fmmotion` (`fm motion check [PATH]`, T-1624: the Motion Score, motion.toml's (or a .json's) `[[motion]]` entries or `[motion.NAME]` tables — name, duration in ms or a DESIGN.md token, easing, properties, reduced none (rest state, never plays)/fade/instant — resolved and checked, exit 1 on problems, 2 on an unreadable score; `fm motion compile [PATH] --to css|waapi|three|lab [--out FILE]`, T-1625: a clean score as CSS vars + @keyframes, WAAPI options + play() or three.js tween params with ease functions, the same ms and easing in each, reduced forms kept; `fm motion verify [PATH] [--samples FILE]`, T-1627: values sampled from fm smoke's web URL under its frozen profile (`smoke_web.cjs`'s `sampleMotion` seeks each paused `motion-NAME` animation through one iteration) or read from a file, held to the score's duration and curve (the three target's ease(p), run in node) within 2% of the travel; each drift names motion, property, ms, expected, sampled and tolerance (exit 1); colours, rotations and mismatched values are noted, not checked; no browser or no URL: says so and checks the score only; fmmotion.sample is where fm shot would take over, T-1716)
- `fmsmoke` + `smoke_web.cjs` (`fm smoke`: the web UI crawled in the project's own headless Playwright at desktop and phone width, every tab clicked; stuck loading placeholders, alerts, console and page errors, failed or 4xx/5xx requests, sideways overflow and overlapping fixed controls; `fm smoke set web <url>` makes it a gate; fm doctor warns about a web UI with none, T-0417; T-1509: first the start page's visual-qa frame matrix, 360/768/1440 × light/dark + a reduced-motion stillness pass, on the app's own clock, frames capped at 8000 px, its findings (`frame_findings`) notes unless strict (`--strict`, or `fm smoke set strict on`, kept as meta `smoke_strict` beside `smoke`, whose keys fm night reads as targets); `--profile` torture|empty|offline|slow3g|deuteranopia|protanopia|tritanopia|achromatopsia|frozen|motion|all crawls under a hostile condition instead, exit 0 on findings unless strict, logged as `smoke_profile` so fm outcomes doesn't read it as a regression, offline/slow3g running out of time a note; screenshots under `<project state>/smoke/<commit>[-dirty-<diff hash>]/<default|profile>/` named after each view (Unicode letters kept), a run holds the commit folder's lock (`fmcore.lock`, 300 s) and replaces its own subfolder, five commits kept; a Playwright with no browser is skipped with the reason, never installed; the crawl runs in its own process group, SIGTERM then SIGKILL past 240 s)
- `fmbudget` (`fm budget`: one spend ledger for child runs and subagents, caps that refuse overspend, halved at high usage)
- `fmevolve` (`fm evolve`: one bench-gated generation revising or ablating one instruction file on an evolve branch)
- `fmbench` (`fm bench build [--judged]|run|list|show|compare|gate|models|duel|versions|court|soak`: finished tasks replayed as fail-to-pass or judged cases with a candidate plugin, against a rival plugin or an earlier Foreman, the user's steers as cases, a shadow-user soak)
- `fmresearch` (`fm research ask`: web researchers per sub-question, quoted claims checked against their pages)
- `fmserve` (`fm serve` / `fm run`; `--fit` small tasks first, `--failover AGENT` opt-in handoff on a usage limit, T-0751)
- `fmhome` (T-1665: `fm home export [--out FILE]` / `fm home import FILE`: an allowlist both ways (per project tasks/, archive/, research/ markdown, decisions.md, ledger.jsonl, meta.json cut to `META_KEYS`; tui.toml), redacted, credential-named JSON values blanked, sensitive projects left out, sha256 manifest; import is user-only (`USER_ONLY_FM`), checks every header (paths, links, sizes, header records) and hash first, strips grants and run marks (`fmsync._from_mirror`, plain text first), demotes active briefs to planned with `confirm: true` and no lease marks, drops forgeable ledger events (check_run, evidence, ledger_damage_ack), type-checks meta and sets next_id past every imported id, takes tui.toml only beside its own state, and moves the projects folder in with one rename, into an empty home only)
- `fmsettingsserve` (T-1659: `fm settings serve`: the settings schema as a page on 127.0.0.1, random port; the printed URL's token works once and becomes an HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie; Host and a POST's Origin checked, bodies capped, CSP default-src 'none'; each change through `fmsettings.set_key`, guard included; stops on Ctrl-C, SIGTERM or IDLE)
- `fmpage` + `page.html` (`fm page [--catalogue NAME] [--url URL] [--out PATH]`: the active task and step, queue, inbox and blocked counts, last closes and optionally a research catalogue's clusters (`research/NAME.json` or `research/NAME/catalogue.json`) as one self-contained HTML page for a claude.ai Artifact; ids, titles, tiers, marks and counts only, every string redacted and the write refused, naming the field, while one still holds an absolute or home path; it never publishes, it prints the Artifact line (capabilities db and user, the link recorded by `--url`) and that Build/Veto picks are read back with ArtifactData from the collection `picks`, T-0786; `fm page --catalogue NAME --picks FILE|-` stores them, `{id: add|veto}` checked against the catalogue's ids, in state `picks.json` and prints a summary, T-0962; `fm page looks [--out PATH]` + `looks.html`: one living specimen per look card, in the card's palette and type pair, one file that loads nothing from another host, T-1649; `fm page city [--out PATH]` + `city.html`: the repo as a 3D city at dusk from fm map's sources, each tracked text file a building as tall as its lines (the 4000 biggest, reads capped at 256 MB in all) with windows lit by its commits in 180 days ("history unavailable" when git log fails), a district per folder (one holding over 35% of the files opens into its subfolders), raw WebGL2 inlined over an isometric SVG poster on the scene template's survival contract (reduced motion and no WebGL2 keep the poster with no context made; one 4.5 s pass that the first drag or key ends, then no frames until input), named by the biggest districts and most changed files, T-1653; `fm page graph [--out PATH]` + `graph.html`: the task graph in 3D on the same contract, every open task and every task a depends_on links (the newest 1500) in layers by the longest chain it waits on (Kahn's order, a cycle broken at its lowest id and said), depends_on as arcs, status as colour, tier as height, named by its counts, active work and longest chain, every task and what it waits on in the text twin; a second run refreshes it from fm state, T-1654; `fm page graph --cells [--cols N]` prints that graph in the terminal instead, boxes coloured from graph.html's tokens drawn by `fm scene cells`, then a key of status counts, writing no page, T-1655; test_showpieces' `OneFile` and `OneFileBrowser` hold every showpiece to one set of checks)
- `fmplugins` (`fm plugins`: marketplace search, conflict check)
- `fmfixes` (T-1032: known fixes, `fm fix list|add|rm`: a failed Bash command and the next command on the same program that passed are kept as one fix, in the project's fixes.md (fm sync mirrors it into .foreman/, so clones and teammates get it through git) and this machine's shared/fixes.md; the next failure with that signature, in any project, gets it in PostToolUseFailure's context ahead of the old-task pointer, and a command that failed before is flagged in PreToolUse once a session; counts stay local and a fix that misses twice more than it worked stops being offered; a failure met 3 times with no fix captures one task; nothing here runs a fix) T-1865: a learned fix (it has `failed`) matches whole only; a signature with under two words of 3+ letters is never offered.
- Capability miss (T-2270): the PostToolUseFailure hook (main thread only) reads "command not found: X", "No module named X" or "Cannot find module X" (a plain name, `[A-Za-z0-9_.+-]{1,64}`, else ignored), notes `fm plugins find X` or build a script with its test once per session (ledger event `capability_miss`), and a second session missing X captures one FEATURE task (source self, keyed, once ever). No network or plugin search in the hook.
- `fmrecall` (`fm recall` and `--ask`, `fm explain`, `fm task show --story`, Related at focus, failure memory; recall weighted by activation — files being touched, failures recurring — T-0663; `fm dream`: the day's repeated failures as tripwire candidates, a free night job, T-0665; `fm recall --magnets`: files where fixes keep landing, T-0613; `fm recall --repos`: prior art from other opted-in projects, T-0618; `fm recall --explain`: where a question's names are defined, T-0659; `fm clauses`: every clause of a request matched to a task, T-0595; `fm bisect --run CMD [--good REF]` in fmcli: the first commit where a check started failing, shared with `fm sentinel --bisect`, T-0612)
- `fmmap` (`fm map`, `fm impact`; `fm map --cold`: hot files no session has read, a free night job, T-0660; the map carries the repo's top stack cards for the session line)
- `fmlib` (T-0933: `fm lib` — the stack library: card parsing and lint, stack detection from manifests (package.json, pyproject, Cargo.toml, go.mod, Gemfile, composer, pom/gradle, pubspec, mix.exs, csproj, Package.swift), files and extensions, suggest, search with installed plugins' skills, show by section, card reads logged as `lib` events; T-0945 `fm lib source add|defaults|update|rm|list`: third-party skill repos as shallow markdown-only checkouts in state/lib-sources with hooks, LFS, filters, submodules and symlinks off, indexed per commit into index.json and searched as source:name; T-1396: a symlink, a skill that breaks the Agent Skills spec (fmplugins.spec_problems: name 1-64 lowercase letters, digits and single hyphens equal to its folder, description 1-1024; fm doctor checks Foreman's own the same way), hidden characters, an over-broad 'use for every task' description or a repeated name is kept in index.json with its reason under quarantine and held out of search)
- `fminstr` (`fm instruments`: the zero-token instruments `fm sym`, `fm fail`, `fm logs`, `fm data`, `fm trace` from one typed registry, T-0701)

Left out: per-domain proof kinds (data, infra, perf).
- `fmoutcomes` (`fm outcomes`: each finished task's fate — reverted, fixed later, held — and the track record by type and tier shown in `fm digest` and at `fm focus`, T-0616/T-0641; stated confidence scored per bucket with a Brier score, T-0619; `fm outcomes --atlas`: files and languages where work didn't hold, with a caution at focus, T-0620; `fm recall --lessons`, `fm bench hygiene` and blocked tasks reopened by `fm tidy` build on the same records, T-0617/T-0656/T-0657)
- `fmreverify` (T-1663: the re-verify queue in reverify.json; `fm sentinel` first scans the commits since its last scan (50 at most, merges skipped), queues the passing criteria checks of each task closed before a commit that touches its scope (5 tasks per commit, not the task's own commits: its Foreman-Task trailer, else its id in the subject; 100 entries kept), runs them ahead of the recent tasks' checks and captures a FIX naming the task (redacted output and subject, a hash of the command in the title) when one fails; entries with side effects, refused by the guard or for tasks no longer done are dropped unrun and counted; the first scan, and one where the last head isn't an ancestor of HEAD (a rewrite or branch switch), only records HEAD; a git failure leaves the watermark)

- `fmeco` (`fm sweep`, `fm adopt`, `fm inbox gh`, `fm canary`, `fm machine`, version skew: Foreman across projects, machines and the tools around it, T-0673)

- `fmoutside` (`fm landscape`, `fm deps`: the outside view; `fm deps --calls`: installed versions and import sites, local only, T-0594)

- `fmscout` (`fm scout`, T-0950: Foreman finds its own work; T-2178: `probe_genesis` starts Genesis itself, once, under full autonomy with an unexpired standing goal or mandate (the user's own text) in a non-sensitive project: gen-0 drafted model-free from that text, sealed in the tower folder, a `costly` decision row; otherwise it stays T-2105's offer that waits on the user's yes)
- `fmorders` (`fm orders`: standing orders that capture a task on a schedule or when a file changes, T-0452)
- `fmcost` (`fm burden`: Foreman's own cost per session and version from the transcripts — bookkeeping-only turns, guard refusals, refused fm calls, injected context — T-0712; `fm cost [--by-model|--sessions]`: spend per task, type and model; per session, context per turn and cache busts, T-0703)
- `fmforesight` (the session-start sweep, T-1810: every project's Stop hook spawns a detached `fm cost --sweep` at most every 10 min (a stamp in the project's state); it notes a changed model, window or CC version from the statusline snapshot in `state/foresight.json` — the burn scan reruns at once — and every 30 min per repo (one call shared by its clones and worktrees; a private repo's 404 retried daily) reads the default branch's GitHub workflow runs over the public API into `state/ci/<owner>__<repo>.json` (github.com origins; never under `FOREMAN_NO_NET` or in a sensitive repo), capturing one FIX per red sha; session start reads only those caches and adds the blocked pile and an overdue tidy, each named with its action, a bad cache costing only its own line; T-2117: the usage burn, the blocked pile, an overdue tidy and the week's most-hit guard rule share one `Watch:` line, each a short item pointing at the verb with the detail (fm digest, fm tidy, fm risk), and the Queue line names 3)
- `fmambient` (`fm check ambient on`: the affected tests run detached after each edit and only a flip reaches the model, T-0705; never beside a full `fm check`: an edit while `c.gates_live(p, here=True)` starts no run, T-1965, and a full check first waits up to 5 minutes for a running one, T-1964; a full check also waits up to 10 minutes for another live full check on this tree and clears `gates_running` only when the pid is its own, T-1971; a full check whose tree changed while it ran starts one run afterward via `fmambient.after_edit`, T-1973; nor beside `fm ship`'s test run: an edit while meta `ship_testing` is live starts none, T-1984; every such wait goes through `c.wait_while`, which says what it waits for and, when the limit runs out, that it goes ahead beside the other run, T-2010)
- `fmdebug` (`fm suspects`, `fm whyred`, `fm record`: a red run's ranked suspects, its minimal failing hunks against the task's start tree, and the locals where its exceptions unwound; the failure hook adds the top 3 suspects, T-0706)
- `fmgraph` (`fm graph blast|pack|build`: one local sqlite graph of code, tests, tasks and commits with time on its edges; a change's blast radius and a task's ranked read-set by personalized PageRank, `--as-of` for backtests; graphify's graph.json is its code layer when present, T-0707)
- `fmbus` (`fm bus`, `fm lease`, `fm conductor`: messages between sessions on this machine delivered by the hooks (next tool call; a stopping session is held for its mail; `--wake` leaves it to the session's `fm bus watch` monitor when its heartbeat `state/bus/watch-<sid>` is under 10 s old, else types into its tmux pane, T-0777), function-level edit leases taken by each edit and enforced in PreToolUse, and the live-session list with one steer for all, T-0708; `fm conductor remote add NAME user@host` adds a machine whose sessions show as tiles, fetched over the user's own ssh by a detached refresh and cached, T-0711)
- `fmsplit` (`fm task split`, `fm task capsule`: a task's files partitioned over the work graph into child tasks scoped to each part, a bounded capsule as each child's return, and a parent that closes after its children, T-0709)
- `fmstack` (`fm task finish --commit … --stack`: one commit per step group, per member for a batch host, each checked alone in a scratch worktree; a red one folds into the next, T-0710)
- `fmchange` (`fm spec "<wish>" --module M`: a one-line wish as a red unittest from a tool-less child's assertions, only safe ones kept; `fm rewrite OLD NEW [--apply] [--convert]`: one rename rule, Python by tokens and other code by whole word, every residual spelling named and optionally rewritten by a cheap child, T-0713)
- `fmdocs` (`fm docs`: doc drift against the code)
- `fmreplay` (`fm replay`: the guard's verdicts on real past commands, against a baseline; `--candidate REF|FILE` scores another guard against the working one, loosened verdicts first, T-0712)
- `fmmcp` (`fm mcp`: read-only MCP tools over stdio)
- `fmsession` (`fm session`: detached agent sessions for claude, codex, gemini and opencode, one normalised event stream each)
- `fmclaude` (`fm claude`: every Claude Code session on the device from its own store — conversation, images, subagents, scratchpad — and `send` to continue one)

- `fmmodels` (T-1211: fm code's model layer — one urllib client for OpenAI-compatible chat completions and Anthropic Messages; `profiles()`, `resolve(name)` (models.json's named or default profile, else the first local server answering `/v1/models` in 0.3 s: Ollama, LM Studio, llama.cpp, vLLM, else the first cloud whose key is set: Anthropic, OpenAI, OpenRouter, Groq, Gemini), `complete()` (one `stop` vocabulary: end, tool_calls, length; 3 tries on 429/5xx, Retry-After capped at 20 s, no redirects, the key never over plain http to a non-loopback host and scrubbed from every `ModelError`), `extract_json()`; profiles in `$FOREMAN_HOME/models.json`)
- `fmcode` (T-1212: `fm code`, Foreman's loop run by the harness on any fmmodels profile — a validated PLAN slot, the task made with `fm task new --focus`, one action at a time per step, edits/writes/runs through `hooks/hook PreToolUse`, verify as `fm task evidence --ac N --run`, `fm task finish`; journal `code/<task>.jsonl` in the project's state) — all protected core | Claude | on call | stdlib only
- `fmcodetui` (T-1283: `fm code tui`, fm code's terminal UI, drawn only from its `--json` event stream; never edits, verifies or closes)
- `fmcodeterm` (T-1300: the terminal under `fm code tui` without curses: ANSI in the colours the terminal shows, only changed cells after the first frame)
- `fmcodebench` (T-1215: `fm code bench`, a model or worker CLI graded as a Foreman worker on seeded tasks, each in its own throwaway repo and home)
- `fmlandlock` (T-1607: an fm code worker's writes confined with Landlock through ctypes, beneath its writable set)
- `fmsettings` (T-1496: one schema for every setting the fm code app's tui.toml and the foreman-ui mod's userConfig read)
  - Extras (T-1658): every set, import, macro, words change and undo appends to `state/settings/changelog.jsonl` (`_log`, kept to `CHANGELOG_MAX`; `fm settings log [KEY]`); an undo of a key undone 3+ times names what set it (`undo_hints`); T-1876: the log's text ends with `_macro_tips`, each key a one-key "set" wrote 3+ times whose last value no macro holds, as the `fm settings macro` line that saves it. `macro NAME --set K=V …` saves to `macros.json`, `macro NAME` runs it as one undo step (`apply`). `duel A B` draws two tui.toml files on one run. `replay [--set K=V] [--events F] [--runs N]` plays the sample run, this project's last runs and a stress burst at `REPLAY_SIZES`, and with `--set` the same on the changed file, nothing written (`_plan_app`, not `_app_change`, so no guard). `words TEXT [--apply]` asks a tool-less claude -p (`fmideas.run_child`) for settings; each is checked, and a change undone twice is never proposed.
  - Control Room extras (T-1661): `layout_moved(lay, c, i, dc, di)` is the one panel move (columns and heights together; None at an edge; a column's only panel refused) for the app's layout editor and the served page's grid (`fmsettingsserve._grid`, ← ↑ ↓ → forms posting to `/move`, one `set_apps` write, one undo). `detent(e, v, n)` turns a number one notch: a closed range on a 1-2-5 grid at about a twentieth of the span, an open one up the 1-2-5 ladder; the page draws a closed range as `<input type=range>` with a tick `<datalist>`, an open one as a stepped number, both with − + turn buttons (`turn=±1` on `/set`), and `[`/`]` turn the selected number in the app's settings screen. `newcomer(goals, real)` / `fm settings newcomer [--goals F] [--real]` plays `plugin/evals/settings/newcomer.json` through an in-process App on the defaults in a temp sandbox (FOREMAN_HOME, FOREMAN_STATE, Claude Code's settings; `fmcore.write_atomic` refuses any path outside it; only the settings and layout screens): a step whose `see` text isn't on screen is where it got stuck (exit 1); `--real` has a tool-less claude -p reply with the keys from the screen alone. T-1876: a goal not reached carries `macro`, the first saved macro setting its `done` values (`_reaches`, read before the sandbox), said as "fm settings macro NAME sets it".
  - Scopes and pins (T-1611): `fmcodetui.layered(cfg, root, run)` puts this project's settings (`project_settings_path`: tui.toml-shaped, `tui.projects/SLUG.toml` beside tui.toml, core to the guard as tui.toml is, never in the repo), this run's (`App.run_settings`, memory) and the repo's `.foreman/policy.toml` `[pin]` table (`policy_path`) over tui.toml's config, each through `_apply`; `PIN_FREE` (keys, motion) is never pinned, `LAYER_ONLY` tables (profile, rule, ext) stay tui.toml's; `cfg["scopes"]` names the narrowest scope per setting. The app layers at start, reload, outside edits and previews (`App._layer`); s in the settings screen cycles `set_scope`, `:set … --scope run|project|everywhere` overrides it, a pinned setting refuses a :set, and rows carry their scope. `fm settings set KEY VALUE --scope project` (`fmsettings._set_project`) writes the project's file.
- `fmauditrun` (T-1186: `fm audit run ID`, the audit lenses as headless read-only reviewers over the brief `fm audit prep` writes)
- `fmaway` (T-1155: `fm away`, the summary on return after a long gap)
- `fmbacktest` (T-1058: `fm backtest`, where a changed rule, known fix or card would have fired on recorded sessions)
- `fmci` (T-1052: `fm ci-watch`, the branch's latest GitHub Actions run polled through gh after a push, or GitHub's public API when gh is missing or logged out, T-1939)
- `fmcontract` (T-1158: `fm contract`, how long and how much an unattended run may take, and its receipt)
- `fmdeps` (T-1049: a package new to the project in an npm/pip/cargo install gets a typosquat, registry and age look; notes only)
- `fmdraft` (T-1062: `fm draft`, a headless claude drafts the top queued S task in a throwaway worktree while nobody waits)
- `fmedit` (T-1089: `fm edit` and `fm sub`, many exact replacements in one call)
- `fmintegrations` (T-1551: Foreman for other tools, read-only: `fm timeline` as Perfetto/Chrome trace or OTLP JSON, and more)
- `fmloop` (T-1057: `fm loop`, an empty queue starts an improvement round instead of ending the run)
- `fmmutate` (T-1066: mutation tests for the guard, one ast mutant per rule site in fmguard.py)
- `fmoracle` (T-1553: the guard's oracle, a line run in a throwaway bwrap sandbox that records each command it would run)
- `fmpr` (T-1087: `fm pr threads` turns a PR's unresolved comments into steps; `fm pr train` merges approved green PRs in order)
- `fmrelease` (T-1188: `fm release VERSION`, the release ritual as named, resumable steps; Foreman's own repo only; T-1943: `fm ship`; T-2054/T-2212: with no commits named it replays the branch's first-parent steps since the last ship when origin main hasn't touched the files they change, so a lane merged after a conflict ships; T-2255: a red scratch-tree test, whose output streams while a 1 MB tail is kept, captures the urgent baseline-red task `fm check` writes (`fmcli._baseline`), named on the error line, so its fix teaches `fm impact`; nothing is pushed)
- `fmrisk` (T-1080: the daily risk budget: destructive blocks, failed closes and reverts spend points; past the cap autonomy steps down)
- `fmroll` (T-1648: `fm design roll`, a seeded, coherent pick of one look card's palette, type, motion and texture)
- `fmrollback` (T-1085: a snapshot of every project's record before a new version runs, and `fm rollback` to restore it)
- `fmtrials` (T-1059: a self-change as a pre-registered trial, graded by the digest on ledger event rates before and after)
- `fmwhich` (T-1699: `fm which TASK`, the skills, playbooks and commands that fit a task, each with the call that loads it)

</details>
