## 4. Interaction diagram

```mermaid
flowchart TD
    U[Your prompt] --> UPS[UserPromptSubmit hook<br/>intake tags, override words,<br/>PAUSE/RESUME → meta]
    UPS -->|factual note ≤ 400 chars| C[Claude + rules/foreman.md + skills]
    C -->|fm intake / capture / task / focus / evidence| FM[fm CLI<br/>atomic writes + lock + ledger]
    FM --> ST[(state/projects/slug<br/>briefs · STATE · INBOX · ledger)]
    C -->|Bash / Write / Edit| PTU[PreToolUse hook<br/>guard: deny + how to authorize<br/>scope note]
    PTU -->|allowed| T[Tool runs]
    T --> POST[PostToolUse: sync for writes, async otherwise<br/>write validators block<br/>touched files → ledger<br/>timeline → events.jsonl]
    C -->|turn ends| STOP[Stop hook<br/>evidence gate: block once per step<br/>drive: continue while work remains,<br/>while background work runs: once, work on what doesn't need it, then wait,<br/>end the turn once when this session's dev-mods changed]
    STOP -->|block reason| C
    TC[TaskCompleted hook] -->|refuse step without evidence| C
    PC[PreCompact hook] -->|fm checkpoint --auto| ST
    SS[SessionStart hook<br/>startup · resume · clear · compact] -->|reads| ST
    SS -->|state summary ≤ 2000 chars| C
    ST --> SL[statusline · foreman-ui band/pane · fm watch]
```

## 5. Lifecycle walkthrough (a `FIX:` line to an archived task)

1. You type `FIX: login times out on slow wifi`. **UserPromptSubmit** (`plugin/hooks/hook` → `fmhooks.user_prompt_submit`) injects "Message has 1 intake item (FIX). No active task." and updates `meta.json` (session heartbeat).
2. Claude follows `rules/foreman.md` → `/foreman:intake`: `fm intake` creates `tasks/T-0012-login-times-out-on-slow-wifi.md` (status captured) from `templates/brief.md`, appends `intake` to `ledger.jsonl`, and regenerates `STATE.md`, `INBOX.md`, `state.line`, `badge.txt`.
3. R1–R4: `fm task new … --from T-0012` (planned), `fm task set --section …` (T-1886: a captured task that gets criteria and steps from set, ac add or step add turns planned, unless set names a status), `fm task ac add` (and `fm task ac ID edit N --verify CMD|--text TEXT` to fix one; T-0985: a criterion locks once a run of its check has evidence, but a run that never reached the check — exit 127, or a missing file or module the command itself names — is marked `(never ran: …)` and doesn't lock it, so a wrong verify path is edited instead of the task being dropped and redone), `fm task step add` (T-1959: `fm task step ID edit N "<text>"` and `rm N`, renumbering the rest; refused once evidence sits at or after that step, since evidence is keyed by step number); grounding reads the code; a tier-M plan compares two approaches. Adjacent ideas go to `fm capture --source followup` (new briefs).
4. `fm log baseline …`, then `fm focus T-0012` (status active). **PreToolUse** runs the guard on every Bash/Write/Edit (`lib/fmguard.py`), notes out-of-scope edits and adds a stack card's pitfalls to a file's first edit (T-0949); a one-line note it already gave since the session's last start or compaction is dropped (`_said_once`, `sessions/<sid>.said`, T-1258), and `test_context_tax` replays a session against per-event byte budgets; a separate, never-blocking Read entry answers the first full Read of a 600+ line file with its outline (T-0724); **PostToolUse** records `touched` files and the timeline (`state/events.jsonl`), and validates each write (T-0947). The reply badge `[T-0012 FIX · 2/4 · 14:02]` comes from **MessageDisplay** (screen only).
5. Each step: `fm task step T-0012 done N --evidence "<cmd>" "<result>"` (refused without evidence). Steers go in with `fm task log`. If Claude claims "done" without evidence, the **Stop** hook blocks once (`gate.json`); if work remains, **drive** continues it.
5b. Resuming: `claude --continue` with drive on, full autonomy and open work starts its own first turn (SessionStart `initialUserMessage`). A driven turn that changed this session's hot-reloaded mods (`~/.claude/dev-mods/<session>/`) ends on purpose, since Claude Code reloads a mod only when a turn really ends; the Stop hook records `resume_after_reload` in meta and the reloaded foreman-ui submits "Continue the Foreman drive" once (`fm ui --json` carries the record for 10 minutes; the next prompt clears it).
6. `/compact` mid-task: **PreCompact** writes the auto resume block into the brief; **SessionStart(compact)** re-injects the focus and resume notes.
7. Audits (`references/audit.md`): tier M → `intent` plus the riskiest other lens, each a `foreman:fm-reviewer` run with its own context slice; findings are reproduced, fixed test-first or captured, then `fm task audit T-0012 <lens> …`. `fm task ac T-0012 check N --evidence …`, `fm task done T-0012` (refused unless every step and criterion has evidence and the tier's audits postdate the last edit). `/foreman:reflect`: `fm decide …` → `decisions.md`; learnings → auto memory; Foreman ideas → `fm capture --self`.
8. 14 days later `fm tidy --apply` moves the brief to `archive/YYYY-MM/`, rolls old ledger months into `archive/ledger-YYYY-MM.jsonl`, and records `last_tidy`.

## 6. Source of truth and precedence

| Information | Lives in | Loaded into context |
|---|---|---|
| Foreman operating rules | `~/.claude/rules/foreman.md` (symlink) | every session |
| Your stable preferences | `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` | every session |
| Project conventions and commands | project `CLAUDE.md` | every session in that project |
| Path/topic-specific rules | `.claude/rules/*.md` with `paths:` | when matching files are read |
| Procedures | skills (`plugin/skills/`) | on demand |
| Current focus and next step | `STATE.md` (generated from briefs) | injected at startup/resume/clear/compact |
| Task specs, progress, evidence | `tasks/T-*.md` | on demand |
| Unplanned requests | briefs with status `captured` (`INBOX.md` is a view) | count + titles injected |
| Why things were decided | `decisions.md` | on demand |
| Durable learnings and gotchas | native auto memory (`MEMORY.md` + topic files) | startup window, then on demand |
| History | `ledger.jsonl` | never wholesale; via `fm` |
| Pending consent | `meta.json` `pending_approvals` (written by `fm ask`, decided only by your next prompt typed at an interactive Claude Code; T-1226: a prompt from a `-p`/`--print`/stream-json/SDK process, or a CronCreate/ScheduleWakeup/RemoteTrigger prompt starting with a yes, never decides it) | reported by the prompt hook |
| In-session checklist | the brief's Steps (built-in task tools are off on current models; the TaskCompleted gate is ready if enabled) | via `fm resume` |
| Knowledge graph | none installed | — |

Precedence: your current message > project CLAUDE.md and rules > `rules/foreman.md` > skill defaults. Safety guards are never overridden.

## 7. Integrations

| Plugin / tool | Stage | How |
|---|---|---|
| context7 (MCP) | GROUND | library/API docs during R2 |
| clangd-lsp, rust-analyzer-lsp | GROUND, VERIFY | diagnostics |
| ponytail | CLEAN, all | minimal-code discipline; `/ponytail:ponytail-audit`, `-debt`, `-review` for CLEAN (injects ~5.7k chars at SessionStart and into every subagent) |
| security-guidance | SECURITY | continuous edit warnings + async Stop/commit reviews (non-blocking; coexists with the Stop gate) |
| code-review | FINAL VERIFY (L) | `/code-review`; `foreman:fm-reviewer` for an independent read-only review |
| skill-creator, `claude plugin eval` | REFLECT / improve | author and evaluate skills; eval referee |
| claude-md-management | REFLECT | content revision on demand; mechanical CLAUDE.md lint is owned by `fm tidy` |
| frontend-design | FEATURE (UI) | on demand |
| claude-hud | visibility | rendered inside the Foreman statusline wrapper |
| plugin-dev | build only | disabled after the build |
| ECC | disabled | ~41k always-on tokens, sync hooks on every tool call, competing memory/planning, transcript sent to a model at compaction; useful procedures ported to `/foreman:playbooks` |
| superpowers | disabled | competing orchestrator; debugging, TDD and verification ported into intake references |
| pyright-lsp, typescript-lsp | disabled | language servers not installed (re-enable after installing them) |
| playwright, chrome-devtools-mcp | disabled at user scope | enable per web repo: `claude plugin enable <id> --scope local` |
| design, figma (synced) | turned off locally | not doing design work |
| Grafana/InfluxDB/telegraf | history | `plugin/observability/`; enable once the collector endpoint is known |

`fm next` also names, while a task executes, up to three installed skills that fit its stage (T-0205): enabled plugins', the user's and the project's skills whose name or first description sentence carries the stage's words (UI words when the scope has UI files), ones invoked before in this project first, then Claude Code's own (`/security-review`, `/simplify`); skills for authoring skills or plugins and ones duplicating Foreman's process are left out. The index is cached in the project's state until the plugin registry, settings or a skills folder changes. Re-enable anything with `claude plugin enable <id>`; `plugin/uninstall.sh` lists what Foreman disabled.

## 8. Relationship to BUILD_PROMPT.md and the repo

`BUILD_PROMPT.md` is the origin spec; this repo is how Foreman travels between machines (`install.sh` one-liner). Changes to Foreman itself go through intake as tasks against `~/.claude/foreman/plugin` (Foreman's own project), and MASTER.md is updated in the same commit; `fm doctor` fails if the file map drifts from disk. Self-improvements are bounded and eval-gated (`/foreman:improve`): built in an `improve/<date>` worktree, compared with `claude plugin eval`, and merged only with your approval. The guard blocks agent writes to protected core (all Foreman code in `plugin/lib`, `bin` and `hooks`, the rules, evals, BUILD_PROMPT.md, settings), including writes from interpreter code, and agents can't grant themselves `core`: Claude runs `fm ask <ID> core` and your yes grants it. The same holds for `remote` (starting `fm serve`) and `plugin` (`claude plugin install|enable|disable|uninstall|update`, `claude plugin marketplace add|remove|update`, `claude mcp add|remove`, `claude config set`, `fm plugins install|enable|disable|add-marketplace`, writes under `~/.claude/plugins` (installed plugins' code and registries, including `git pull`/`checkout` there), and `claude --settings|--mcp-config|--plugin-dir` sessions, and creating a skill, agent or command file under `~/.claude` or the project's `.claude/`), and `~/.claude.json` (workspace trust, MCP servers) counts as core; user systemd unit files count as `system`. `confine` (T-0234) is never grantable: in a builder's worktree (its task carries `builder`), every write outside the worktree is refused but scratch, and the project's main checkout never counts as scratch. `confirm` (T-0289) is user-only too: a request `fm second session` found in an old transcript is worked only after `fm ask ID confirm` and the user's yes — not `approved=true`, `explore=false`, batching or full autonomy.

## 9. Operations

The notes below are grouped; each group folds. `fm outline MASTER.md` lists the groups.

| Group | What's in it | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| [Dev loop and install](#dev-loop-and-install) | Edit, reload, test; the shell, time travel, MCP, plugin workflows, integrations, install and health checks, plugins, Claude Code and portability notes | 15 |
| [Tasks, batches and backlog](#tasks-batches-and-backlog) | Batches, capture nudges, inbox and backlog tools, waits, many ids at once, tours, away and todo, queue preview and ranking | 15 |
| [Lanes and builders](#lanes-and-builders) | Linked worktrees, defect classes, merge-prone files, stalled and dropped builders, claims, racing builders, cut-off lane repair | 8 |
| [Evidence, gates and verification](#evidence-gates-and-verification) | Evidence, gate runs and reuse, affected tests, close gates, hunk proofs, audits, second reads, test claims, print-mode holds, recurring failures | 19 |
| [Guard and security](#guard-and-security) | The guard's reads, scripts, oracle, mutation and ratchets; credentials, stored commands, install checks, approvals, outbound, sandbox | 19 |
| [Hooks, notes and steering](#hooks-notes-and-steering) | What the hooks say and when: steering as data, session map, tripwires, write validators, outside text, breakers, postures | 9 |
| [Recall, lessons and self-improvement](#recall-lessons-and-self-improvement) | Recall, lessons, taste, trials, drafts, friction, evolve, scout, backtests, repeated work, forge, outside view | 21 |

The dev loop:

```mermaid
flowchart LR
  A[edit plugin/] --> B["/reload-plugins"] --> C[run the tests] --> D[fm check] --> E[fm ship]
```

### Dev loop and install

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Dev loop: edit `plugin/` → `/reload-plugins` (the local marketplace loads in place). Tests: `python3 plugin/tests/run.py` (one test class per core, ~20 s; `-k NAME` to narrow, `test_x -k NAME` for only the matching tests of module test_x, T-1994; plain `python3 -m unittest discover -s plugin/tests -t plugin/tests` still works). Hook latency: `python3 plugin/tests/bench_hooks.py`. Validation: `claude plugin validate --strict plugin`. Live scenarios: `python3 plugin/tests/e2e/scenarios.py`; install round trip: `python3 plugin/tests/e2e/roundtrip.py`; evals: see `plugin/evals/README.md`.
- Time travel (T-0898): `fm at TARGET -- CMD` runs CMD in a throwaway worktree of a task's start snapshot (uncommitted work included) or any commit, branch or tag (`fmcli._run_at`, as `fm bisect` and `fm task prove` use), prints the result and exits with CMD's code; the working tree is never touched. The guard reads the command inside `fm at` and `fm quiet` as if typed (`fmguard._fm_trailing`).
- Shell (T-0917, T-0924): bare `fm` in a project prints the status line and `fm next` (help elsewhere); `fm complete -- WORDS` prints what can come next from the live argparse tree (verbs, a verb's subcommands, options) and open task ids where a positional goes; `fm complete --shell bash|zsh` prints the hook to `eval` in the shell's rc.
- Plugin workflows (T-0787, `plugin/workflows/*.js`, run as `/foreman:<meta.name>`): `review` (a general-purpose scope agent writes the diff for a range, branch, task id or the uncommitted change to a mktemp folder; one `foreman:fm-reviewer` per lens; two skeptics per finding, dropped only when both refute; returns confirmed findings and `fm task finding` lines) and `ideas` (one Explore agent per lens reads the code and marks each idea new/partial/exists with file:line evidence; dedup by title words against `seen`; a critic names up to 4 missing angles for up to 2 more rounds; returns the catalogue and an intake block). `test_workflows` checks each file: a pure-literal meta first, name = file name, phase titles declared, no Date.now/Math.random/new Date(), and `node --check` of the body wrapped as the runner runs it.
- MCP server (T-0245, `fmmcp.py`): `fm mcp` speaks MCP over stdio (JSON-RPC 2.0, one message a line, stdlib only) for the project it starts in: `initialize`, `ping`, `tools/list`, `tools/call`; notifications get no answer; a malformed line is a -32700 error, an unknown method -32601, any other failure -32603 — and the server keeps serving. Tools, all read-only, each running the matching fm command so the CLI stays the one implementation: `state`, `next`, `recall {query}`, `research_list`, `research_read {name}` (a plain note name, no paths, no symlink out of the folder), `task_show {id}` (T-NNNN only), `checks` (the last `fm check` run from the ledger); a recall query can't start with `-` (never a flag), and a line over 1 MB is refused without being held. Everything sent is redacted; a sensitive project lists no tools and refuses calls. Registering it — `claude mcp add foreman -- fm mcp` — is the user's step (the guard's plugin category). T-2172: `exam_scorecard` runs `fm exam scorecard` (exit 1, not every capability passing, is an answer); T-2201: `genesis_status` runs `fm genesis status` (the tower's generation, files, coverage, next action and last exam result), so Desktop and other agents see where Genesis stands. T-0946: also `lib_suggest`, `lib_search {query}`, `lib_show {name, section?}` and the instruments `outline {path}`, `impact {path}`, `why {target}`, `sym`, `logs`, `data`, `trace` (their input schemas from `fminstr.TOOLS`, each value capped) and `fail {text}` (the text piped in); every path, links followed, must resolve under the project root; no value starts with `-` or holds a NUL; a call with keys outside its schema is refused; each call times out after `FOREMAN_MCP_TIMEOUT` s (default 120) and gets an empty stdin, never the server's stream. `fm mcp new NAME --wrap 'CMD' [--sub a,b] [--dir DIR] [--force]` fills `plugin/templates/mcp-server/` (server.py, test_server.py: stdlib only, argv only, args matching `[^\x00-][^\x00]*` up to 200 chars, `sub` an enum, a 30 s timeout, a 100 kB output cap) into an empty folder; it refuses shell syntax in CMD, `_NEVER` commands (rm, dd, sudo, shells, xargs, env…), git subcommands outside a read-only list, a bare dispatcher (git, npm, docker…) without `--sub`, and anything `fmguard.check_bash` flags. `fm mcp register claude [DIR]` prints the `claude mcp add` line; codex and gemini get no line until their format is confirmed against current docs (`fm agents install` already wires Foreman's own server there). T-2127: `find {path?}` runs `fm find ./PATH --rank` (a path under the root, prefixed `./` so it is never a flag; exit 1, findings, is an answer, not an error) and returns the first 200 leads with a count of the rest; Foreman Code reaches fm find through its `run` tool (no prompt tokens added), and the band (`fm ui --json` signals) shows an amber "fm find muted (…)" while your drops hold a finder under `fmfind.FLOOR` (kept two minutes in `find-muted.json`, like the learned line, so a poll skips the archive and git log); an allowed exit 1 with nothing on stdout is a crash, so the tool reports an error. The site's Initiative page covers fm find and scout.
- Integrations (T-1551, `fmintegrations.py`; stdlib, read-only, never a port): `fm timeline [ID] [--otlp] [--out PATH]` exports a task's run (its ledger events, the active task by default) as Perfetto / Chrome trace JSON (`traceEvents`: the task and its steps as `X` spans on track 1, each `check_run` result as an `X` span laid end to end on its session's track and ending at the event, every event an `i` mark with its data re-redacted; non-finite numbers become null) or, with --otlp, OTLP JSON (the task the root span, steps and checks its children, events its span events, a failed check status 2; ids are hashes of project and task, so a second export matches). A sensitive project's export keeps kinds and timings, no words. `fm desk [--out FILE] [--size WxH] [--every S]` (T-1615: `desk_svg`, `desk_html`, `_desk_write`) draws the glance as a black-and-white card (800x480 by default, scaled) for an e-paper panel or an old phone: SVG, .png through rsvg-convert, or .html that reloads itself, written whole, once or every S seconds (10 or more) into a synced folder; no port is opened. The site's charts (`site/build.py` hbars and pairs `grow-x`, columns `grow-y`, line `draw` with `pathLength="1"`) draw once when seen: `site.js` arms them after its reduced-motion check, `style.css` moves them only under prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference. `fm glance` prints one ASCII JSON line (v, project, task, step, counts, paused, drive, autonomy, and waybar's text/tooltip/class); outside a project it prints `"project": null` and creates nothing. `fm lsp` is an LSP stub over stdio (Content-Length frames, a frame over 32 MB ends it): initialize, shutdown, exit (code 0 only after shutdown), full sync; didOpen/didChange/didSave(text)/didClose publish the document's proof gaps: a criterion with no verify command (warning) or no `(ac N)` evidence (info, warning once ticked), a step with no evidence (info, warning once ticked). Desk card and animated SVG charts: not built.
- Health: `fm doctor` (`--full` also runs `install.sh --no-plugins`; T-0954: an `invisible text` check WARNs with file:line when the user's CLAUDE.md or rules, or Foreman's rules, skills or agents hold zero-width, bidi or tag characters — hidden instructions; a ZWJ inside an emoji sequence is fine — and `self-docs` FAILs on a README, MASTER, rules or skill naming an `fm` command the parser lacks), `fm tidy` then `fm tidy --apply`, `fm watch` (curses; `--once` for text) in a tmux split.
- State location: `state/`, unless `~/.claude` is read-only (Bash sandbox, `claude plugin eval`, a container): then state moves once to `$XDG_STATE_HOME/foreman` (or `/tmp/foreman-state-<uid>`, cleared at reboot) and a marker there keeps every process following it. `fm doctor` warns while it's in use; `fm doctor --restore-state` moves it back once `state/` is writable. The guard treats every fallback location as state, and a marker in a directory other users can write is ignored.
- Commands: `fm help` (or bare `fm`) lists every command once, in tiers with the everyday ones first (each command's `add(..., tier=(title, order))` registration fills `HELP_TIERS`, T-1144; a command without a tier fails to register); `fm <command> -h` gives its options. T-1519: `fm help WORDS` answers "can Foreman do X?" from that same help (`fmrecall.can`: the commands whose name, help and options' help hold at least half the question's non-asking words, rarest first, a command the question names lifted; each line ≤ 100 columns, a long help cut to the clause holding the question's words (`fmrecall.gist`); exit 1 with a plain no when none do; `--all` with words, or a question of only asking words, is refused saying so). T-1699: `fm which TASK` (`fmwhich`) ranks the skills (`plugin/skills/*/SKILL.md` names and descriptions, read at run time; a user-only one listed as "you type /foreman:X"), the playbooks' headers (title and Use-when lines, 600 characters) and the commands' help (less Foreman's own release tools) against the task's words (`fmrecall.words`: idf, a word in the name weighs triple), and prints each fit with the call that loads it; fits holding under half the task's words together are "Nothing matches" (exit 1). The prompt hook says `fm which` once a session for a work request that names no skill and got no skill note. The fence below is runnable: `fm release-check` (`fmdemo.run_fences`) runs each `bash runnable` fence of README.md and MASTER.md as `bash -e` through `fm check playbooks`' runner, with HOME, Foreman's home and state and TMPDIR in its own temporary folder, this checkout's `fm` first on PATH, a `claude` that only fails, stdin closed, its limits, a 120 s timeout and a process-group kill. Runnable fences are trusted like the tests: that is hygiene, not a sandbox (a fence can still reach any file and the network).

```bash runnable
fm help can foreman notify me when a run finishes   # the commands whose help says so, or a plain no
fm new panel tui                                     # into ./tui (with no DIR, fm code's own tui/ folder)
fm new check tui/panel.py                            # it loads, fits every box, in theme styles, inside the budget
fm lib check                                         # every card well-formed; each verified: date real and past
```

- State in the repo (opt-in): `fm sync on` mirrors the project's briefs (archive included), decisions and research into `.foreman/` in the repo, kept current on every fm change, to commit with the work; git is the transport and the conflict detector: fm keeps a hash of what it last wrote to or took from each mirror file, so a file changed by a pull or merge is left alone by exports and taken in by the next fm command, the next session start (a fresh clone turns syncing on unless sync was switched off there) or `fm sync import`. When both sides changed, the local copy wins and theirs goes to `sync-conflicts/`; files with unresolved merge markers wait for git. The ledger, meta, sessions, pending asks, gates and views stay local; brief logs carry the history. Nothing from the mirror counts for this machine's gates: `allow`/`approved` are stripped both ways (a pulled brief keeps the local grants), `[ran]`/`[tree]` marks and audits from elsewhere are neutralized (audits relabelled `imported`), focus is per machine, and symlinks in the mirror are never followed. fm writes `.foreman/` (hand edits are `state-direct`); its markdown is left out of the worktree id (anything else placed there still counts) and out of checkpoint git summaries; new ids skip ids the mirror already has. Exports pass through the secret redactor and imports skip files over 1 MB. `fm sync on` warns when git ignores `.foreman/` (snapshots and task commits then leave it out: `mirror_ignored`) and fails cleanly (sync stays off) when it can't write it. `fm sync status` shows incoming, repo-only and local-only briefs and, when on, the line a teammate runs to join (`fm sync on`, T-0962); `fm sync off [--remove]`.
- Plugins: `fm plugins find <need>` searches the local marketplace indexes (installed/enabled state, always-on estimate; T-1396: a bad entry is skipped with its reason, and a plugin the marketplace's `renames` points at, or whose name, source or sha changed since `fm plugins install`, reads as untrusted, keyed in state/plugin-trust.json); `fm plugins check [ID]` flags enabled plugins with a synchronous Stop hook (competes with Foreman's gate and drive), duplicate MCP servers, or process skills that duplicate Foreman (`fm doctor` warns); `fm plugins install ID` (after your yes to `fm ask TASK plugin --pin ID`; one yes covers one change, and a command bundling several is refused; the yes to install or enable is pinned to that plugin and a hash of its content at the time — the installed copy, else the marketplace entry plus its local copy — so installing another plugin, changed content or a yes over 24 h old is refused with the fm ask to run again; the newest plugin yes on a task replaces an earlier pin; `setup-plugins.sh`, whose installs the guard can't see, refuses a real run inside a Claude Code session, `--dry-run` aside) installs or re-enables it, and a fresh install is recorded so `fm uninstall-user` removes it (only once it really landed in `installed_plugins.json`). Nothing found → `fm plugins add-marketplace <owner/repo | git URL | path>` (same yes), recorded and removed the same way; `fm plugins forget ID` keeps something through uninstall. `check` also reports the curated conflicts (`KNOWN_CONFLICTS`, the same list `setup-plugins.sh` warns about) and resolves enabled plugins with this project's `.claude/settings*.json`.
- Driven by another tool (T-0077): a session started with `FOREMAN_QUIET=1` in its environment (an orchestrator launching `claude`) gets no Foreman context, nudges, drive or brief requirement; only the PreToolUse guard runs, and blocks what it always blocks. No settings change or plugin disable is needed.
- Claude Code integration (2026-10-14 round): a one-off `claude -p` in a Foreman project gets one note and never drives the queue — only `fm run`'s sessions (FOREMAN_DRIVE_TASK) do (T-0775); in full autonomy a turn that ends on an open `fm ask` or a drained queue asks once for a PushNotification (T-0778); the session is named `T-NNNN <title>` via sessionTitle when the focus moves (T-0781); in a sensitive repo the outward tools (Artifact, ArtifactData writes, PushNotification, SendMessage, RemoteTrigger, Claude Docs writes) are refused by a PreToolUse entry of their own (T-0784); `fm install-user` sets `worktree.baseRef: head` so builder lanes start from your branch (T-0803); fm doctor names the features the installed Claude Code is too old for (T-0785); Foreman's `claude -p` children carry `--max-budget-usd` (T-0776); lane briefs pass a per-tier effort (T-0779).
- Compact output (T-0783, opt-in): `fm compact-output on` adds a PostToolUse Bash hook to `~/.claude/settings.json` (backed up; `off` and `fm uninstall-user` remove it; new sessions pick it up) that runs `plugin/hooks/compact`, a stdlib-only script: a Bash output over 6,000 characters reaches the model as its first 40 lines, every other line that reads as a failure (exact duplicates once, at most 40, long lines clipped around the failure word), its last 60 lines and the path of the full text under `state/outputs/<session>/`. Measured on 30 days of this machine's transcripts: 1,090 outputs, 10.75M → 6.61M characters (about 1.04M tokens), no distinct failure line lost (exception names like AssertionError count); ~15-19 ms per call. Off, there is no hook at all.
- Oldest-Python gate (T-1805): `fm check --python VER|oldest` (oldest = `fmpy.NAMES[-1]`, 3.12) finds that interpreter with `fmpy.find((name,), name)` (PATH, ~/.local/bin, uv's; a supported patch only), symlinks it as python3 and python in a temp folder put first on PATH, and runs the usual check; `c.env_id` hashes PATH, so its pass is its own cache entry. Run once before a push to main or a release (the release skill's gate step), not on every fm check (it would double gate time). A version not found is a usage error.

</details>

### Tasks, batches and backlog

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Batches (T-0257): `fm batch ID ID … [--title T]` builds a host brief (type: the members' most common; tier: S for two S, M for three or more or any M, L if any L; criteria and steps prefixed with each member's id; scope and outside dependencies merged; meta `batch`); members get meta `batched_in` (hidden from `rank_inbox` and `order_queue`), close done-in the host when `fm task done` closes it, and are handed back if the host is dropped. Started, active or already-batched items are refused.
- Smarter nudges (T-0262): `fm capture` and `fm task new` say first when an fm command's help already covers the request (`fmrecall.covered`: ≥ 3 shared words and ≥ 60% of the help's; the best two, with the drop command). T-1662: `fm capture` refuses a near-duplicate of an open task of the same type (`fmrecall.near_tasks`: ≥ 60% of the two titles' tokens shared, 2 or more, identifiers like `fmcodetui` or `fmcli.py` kept whole; scopes that never meet, or other names or numbers, are other work), naming it, with exit 5 (`{"refused": "near-duplicate", "of", "title"}` under `--json`) and no brief or attachment written; `--anyway` keeps both. An open task of another type is noted, not refused, and a FEATURE close to done work names that task. Only `fm capture` checks: `fm intake`, `fm task new` and `fm research --from-agent` don't; `fmfixes._escalate` passes `--anyway` (it dedupes by signature). `fm decide … --revisit "after YYYY-MM-DD" | "when PATH changes"` tags the decisions.md row (a path trigger keeps the file's content hash); once it fires, `fm next` carries the decision (and the pane shows it) until a later `fm decide … --revisited WORDS` or `--reverses WORDS` settles it. T-2167: `--check CMD` stores `[check: CMD]` on a rule: only `fm genesis check`, `fm exam scorecard` or `python3 plugin/tests/run.py test_…` (`c.check_argv`: shlex, never a shell, refused when written and again when run, since it runs later outside the PreToolUse guard); `--check "pending T-NNNN"` (`c.CHECK_PENDING`) marks a rule whose enforcement a task is still building; `--cites` naming a steer or the user in other words warns. `c.steer_checks(p)` lists each row cited from a user steer (`c.STEER`) and not settled by a later steer-cited row (a session's own `--reverses` can't retire the user's rule) that has no check, a pending one, or, when run, a failing one with its last output line (each command once, `sys.executable`, cwd the repo, 900 s): fm genesis sweep N's steer checks row and fm doctor's (pending left out). Next's hints (the batch suggestion, installed skills that fit) are counted in the project's `hints.json` where UserPromptSubmit injects them: one shown and not used before the next injection counts as ignored, and past `QUIET_AFTER` (6) it shrinks to its command; running `fm batch`, calling a skill, or a failing `fm check` restores it.
- Review risk (T-0885): every `fm tour` row carries a verdict — review, glance or skim — with its reasons (`fmmap._risk`: a security-sensitive path or added code (`c.sensitive`) +3, runs in CI, on install or brings dependencies +2, over 50/200 changed lines +1/+2, a code file with no test changed beside it +1, deleted code +1, a hot file +1; review at 3+; tests, docs and generated files skim, unless a test file mostly lost lines), and a header naming what to review first (highest score first, 8 named). `fm tour --since REF` tours everything since REF's merge base with HEAD, uncommitted work included: the morning read of a long unattended run, or a branch before merging (`fm tour --since main`). Only code files count as "used" in the reading order.
- Handoff (T-0263): `fm tour ID` lists a task's changed files in reading order — a code file before the code files that name its stem (fm impact's heuristic; tests are never "used", stems under 4 characters and names like index/utils/types are skipped, cycles fall back to most-used first, docs and data last) — with +/- lines, what each uses and the step being worked at its last edit; a finished task with one commit naming `(ID` is read from that commit. `fm pr ID` adds the proof: the red→green command, each audit lens's newest verdict, inconclusive runs, the Assumptions with their tags, and the task's bench replays from the newest saved runs. `fm export agents` writes AGENTS.md for other harnesses (Codex, Cursor, Gemini): how work is done here (with or without fm), the user's last corrections, the project map, recent lessons and decisions, and the playbook index — redacted, under 32 KiB (Codex's default read limit), with a generated-by marker; an AGENTS.md without the marker, a symlink or an unreadable path is kept unless `--force`; playbooks are named by plugin-relative path, and a sensitive project (`fm sensitive on`) exports no corrections, lessons or decisions. T-1043: `fm export cursor` writes `.cursor/rules/foreman.mdc` (frontmatter `alwaysApply: true`) and `fm export gemini` writes GEMINI.md, the same body and the same keep-unless-`--force` rule; every target adds a "Known fixes" section (trusted fixes only, redacted and defanged, at most 15; none in a sensitive project).
- `fm inbox spec FILE` (T-1165, `fmeco.inbox_spec`): each unchecked `- [ ] text` of a markdown checklist (path from the shell's cwd; fenced blocks and empty items skipped) becomes one inbox brief, source `spec`, its text redacted and defanged behind `| ` (title tagged when instruction-like). A Foreman-owned `CONTEXT: spec <file> item <hash>` line dedupes reruns (item text can't forge it). At most 1 MB and 100 items per run, each limit reported.
- Backlog tools (T-1664, `fmeco`; `/foreman:backlog` asks through AskUserQuestion): `fm inbox first` (S items waiting on nothing open, with why), `fm inbox swipe [--apply ID:revive|drop|keep,…]` (the deferred pile, all verdicts checked before any applies), `fm inbox pick` / `fm inbox prefer WINNER LOSER` (close pairs; choices kept as title-word weights in the project's prefer.json, `c.title_tilt` adds at most ±2 to rank_inbox's value), `fm inbox ticket FILE|-` (one brief: type from labels or a traceback, criteria from checkboxes or a criteria list, the first command as a repro step, scope from trace paths inside the repo; text redacted, defanged, quoted, backticks turned to quotes so no verify command rides in). More (T-1866): in a terminal `fm inbox swipe` asks one item at a time (`swipe_loop`: r revive · k keep · d drop · s skip · q stop, each applied as said; a pipe or `--json` gets the list); the fm code app's backlog view (`I`) does the same; `fm inbox pick` skips items waiting on an open one and with probability `--epsilon P` (default 0.1) pairs an item with a farther one of its kind; `fm inbox prefer --reset` deletes prefer.json. `fmcli.spike_note`: an L FEATURE's first focus says spike first unless a step spikes, probes or prototypes. `fm inbox swipe --serve` (T-1858) serves the pile a card at a time on `fm settings serve`'s loopback server (its Handler's `CMD`, `PATHS`, `draw` and `act` are overridden; `fmsettingsserve.run` prints the one-use URL and serves until idle). Undo (T-1871; `swipe_undo`): `swipe_apply(p, spec, undo)` pushes `(id, verdict, revisit)` per answer, a skip pushes `(id, "skip", None)`; `u` in `swipe_loop` or the app's backlog view, or the page's undo button (it names the id it takes back, so a re-posted reload finds the page out of date: 422) pops the newest. A revive or drop goes back to deferred under `mutate` only if the status is still what the answer set and the item is no batch; cards show "N left"; `swipe_loop` returns None for an empty pile ("Nothing is deferred."). `fmsettingsserve.BASE_STYLE` (msg, bad, color-scheme and its dark rules) ends both pages' stylesheets. `fm inbox ticket KEY|jira:KEY|linear:KEY|URL` (T-1859; `ticket_ref`, `fetch_ticket`) fetches a Jira issue (REST v2 from `JIRA_URL`, basic auth with `JIRA_EMAIL` or a bearer `JIRA_API_TOKEN`; a Jira URL must be on `JIRA_URL`'s host) or a Linear one (GraphQL, `LINEAR_API_KEY`) when the argument is no file, and feeds it to the paste pipeline; the key dedupes it. GitHub too (T-1869; `fetch_gh`, `_gh_json` shared with `fm inbox gh`): `gh:OWNER/REPO#N`, `#N` or an issue URL runs `gh issue view` in the root, the key canonicalized from the answer's URL, and an issue `fm inbox gh` captured is that brief (kept as is; `fm inbox gh` in turn skips a `CONTEXT: ticket github` brief). A ticket fetched again with a new text hash rewrites its brief in place while it is captured (text, title, type, criteria, repro, scope) or planned (text and title; the plan stays, logged); started or closed, it gets a new brief. `fm inbox ticket --post ID` (T-1870; `ticket_source`, `post_ticket`, `_tracker` shared with `fetch_ticket`, `_gh` under `_gh_json`) posts "Closed in commit SHA (Foreman ID)." to the source issue (T-1881: the guard reads it as publish, `_check_publish`, so it needs that grant): the source is the Raw request's second line only (the first is the ticket's title, the rest quoted behind `| `), from `fm inbox ticket`'s `CONTEXT: ticket …` or `fm inbox gh`'s `CONTEXT: https://github.com/…/issues/N, `; refused unless done, committed (`closing_sha`: the last `committed SHA` log line), pushed (`git branch -r --contains`) and not yet posted. The log's last `posting the close to` / `posted the close to` / `the post failed` line decides: a claim is written under the project lock before the call, so two runs post once, and only a failure frees a retry (a timeout says the post may have landed). The Jira host is `JIRA_URL` at post time: the brief records only the key. GitHub: `gh issue comment`; Jira: POST `/rest/api/2/issue/KEY/comment`; Linear: the issue's id, then `commentCreate`. The finish names the command for a committed fetched ticket, just before Landed. `fm inbox prefer --why ID` (T-1872; `prefer_why`): an inbox item's `title_tilt` and the learned word weights behind it, one line each, by size then word; refused for an item outside the inbox, a second item or `--reset`. `fm triage` (`cmd_triage`) is `fm inbox` with the source chosen: `swipe` on a TTY or with `--serve`, else `first`.
- Waiting on the outside world (T-1183, `fmcore.until_condition`/`deferral_due`): `fm task block ID WHY --until COND` and `fm task defer ID --until COND` take `installed NAME` (`[\w.+-]{1,64}`, found with shutil.which) or `exists PATH` (inside the project or the home folder, symlinks resolved), besides a date or `when PATH changes`; never a shell command. `fmtidy.unblocked` and `fm next` re-offer the task once it holds; blocking or deferring without `--until` clears an old condition.
- Many ids at once (T-1877, `fmcli.task_ids`/`_task_each`): `fm task drop|defer|unblock` take `T-3..T-7`, `T-3..7` or `T-3,T-9` (≤ `BULK_MAX` 50; a range takes the tasks that exist in it, a listed missing id fails by name), each run as if named alone, failures said per id, "N of M <verb>ed.", exit 1 when any failed; drop's reason is checked once first. `fm task unblock ID [WHY]` sets a blocked task to planned (captured with no criteria), clears its revisit and logs `unblocked: WHY`; anything not blocked is refused.
- The user's uncommitted work stays out of task commits (T-1169): the first `fm focus` of a task saves the files already uncommitted then (from `git diff-tree` of HEAD against the focus snapshot, minus files another Foreman task touched per the ledger, and minus files written in the minute before the focus: T-1177, the task starting, T-0192) to `audits/ID.dirty.json` (≤500 paths); `fm task finish --commit` leaves those out when the task also edited them and names them; `--with FILE` (repeatable; ./ and absolute paths normalised) commits one.
- `fm away [--json]` (T-1155, `fmaway.py`): what closed, blocked, was asked (asks still pending in the ask digest) or was reverted (rollback events, git Revert commits) since the user last spoke before a silence of 2 h or more (the latest `prompt` ledger event whose silence runs to the next prompt or now). SessionStart with source startup or resume adds one "Away: N h …" line naming the newest 6 ids; compaction and clear starts don't; a prompt that ends a silence of 2 h or more mid-session carries the same line in its context (`returned`: the ledger's last 500 lines, a whole-ledger scan only when the previous prompt is further back, and only a silence that ends at this prompt, T-2008); ~35 ms on a 5,000-line ledger (no per-row brief lookups).
- `fm todo [--json]` (T-1157): what only the user can do now, each with what to type, from existing stores only: pending asks (meta `pending_approvals`; past `c.APPROVAL_TTL` they read "expired: ask again"), unanswered `fm decide --ask` tasks, blocked tasks whose block reason names the user (log in, password, credentials, approve…), and costly/outward decisions from the last 7 days not settled by a later reversal (older ones as a count). At most 20 entries; a resolved item drops out.
- Batch edits (T-1089, `fmedit.py`): `fm edit [--dry-run]` parses a stdin spec (JSON list of {file, old, new, all} or `@@ file [all]` / `<<<` / `===` / `>>>` blocks), refuses without an active task, then checks every target before reading: inside the project, inside the task's scope, and `fmguard.check("Write", …)` with the same Ctx the PreToolUse hook builds (allow, standing, trusted), so a batch can't write what an Edit couldn't; all replacements are made in memory and written only when every entry matched (unique unless all), mode kept. `fm sub PATTERN REPL --glob G [--apply] [--max 50]` counts re.subn matches per file and writes them under the same checks. `fmlanes.builders_cap` reads meta `builders` (1-6, default 2) for the builder limit; `fm lane max [N]` sets it. fmhooks' thrash counter says `fm edit` once per session per file at the 3rd edit in a row (BATCH_AT).
- Queue preview (T-0114): `fm queue --preview` lists the queue and the ranked inbox with this project's usual minutes for each type and size (fm ui's `typical`: median focus→done over 3+ closed tasks) and what each will need from the user under the current autonomy: a plan yes (L, or an explore item, in standard), an open `fm ask`, or a core yes its scope implies without a standing one; those come first, together, so they can be answered before a long run. `fm queue --cost` (T-1878, `fmcost.queue_quote`) adds a token quote: each queued task the median of finished tasks of its type and tier (two or more, 90 days of transcripts), summed, beside the last 24 h and the week's usage; tasks with no such history are named; quote only.
- Inbox ranking (T-0111): captured items are listed (fm state, INBOX.md, the dashboard) and picked by `fm next` urgent first, then in the intake type order, then by value per effort: who asked (user 3, discovered or self 2, followup 1), 2 for each item depending on it, up to 2 for waiting two weeks, divided by the tier (S 1, M 2, L 4); an item never precedes a captured item it depends on.
- Across projects, machines and tools (T-0673, `fmeco`; nothing edits another repo or sends anything anywhere): `fm sweep "<fix>" --grep TEXT` asks every other registered project about a fix made here: where the fixed string is (git grep), or without `--grep` what its history recalls, becomes a brief in that project's inbox (source `cross-project`, once; `--dry-run` only says where); sensitive projects are skipped. `fm -p SLUG capture … --source cross-project` is one project asking another, and `fm friction` lists the open requests. `fm adopt` takes in an existing repo once: the map, each gate run (a failure rerun once: passing then is a flake), `fm secrets --history`, the declared dependencies (no registry call), a baseline research note `adopt-baseline`, and captured CLEAN/SECURITY/RESEARCH work for failing and flaky gates, hot files no test covers, credentials and dependencies. `fm inbox gh` reads the repo's open issues through `gh issue list` (read-only) into briefs (source `github`, type from labels, the first fenced command as a repro shown, never run); issue text is untrusted data: control characters removed, instruction-like text marked, quoted, an issue captured once by its URL. Each session start records the Foreman version and machine in the project's meta: `fm projects` shows them with the released task ids (from CHANGELOG.md) the project lacks, and `fm doctor` warns about version skew. Each session start also runs `fm canary --if-changed` detached: a new `claude --version` runs the hook fixtures through the real hook (the guard's block fixture among them) and `fm replay`; a failure is a FIX brief in Foreman's own project. `fm machine` prints this machine's id (hashed OS machine id) and name; once named (`--name`), focus log lines say `focused on NAME`, so briefs fm sync carries show which machine ran what. `fm doctor`'s backup check reads the newest `backups/*.tgz` through to its end (a restore rehearsal): an archive that doesn't open or is cut short fails. `fm doctor`'s claude code features check (T-0785) compares `claude --version` (fmhooks._cc_version) with a table in fmdoctor (`CC_FEATURES`: feature, minimum, plugin files, pattern) and WARNs naming each feature in use that the installed Claude Code is too old for.

</details>

### Lanes and builders

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Defect classes (T-1164, `fmlanes.defect_classes`): every builder brief carries "Defects this project keeps shipping": confirmed `finding` ledger events (`fm task finding ID LENS confirmed TEXT`) from the last 30 days, bucketed by the first matching keyword class of `DEFECT_CLASSES` (hot path, cwd vs root, duplicated output, counting events not actors, wrong signal or stale state, half-done on failure, pattern too loose or narrow, untested branch, unbounded input, untrusted input, race/lock, else other), top 5 with a count and the newest example. Recording reviewer findings with `fm task finding` is what feeds it; since T-1181 a stopped `fm-reviewer`'s findings are logged automatically (`fmlanes.reviewer_findings` parses its `- SEVERITY — path:line — issue` lines under `## lens:` headings, ≤30 per stop, redacted, verdict `reported`, for the active task or the first T-id it names), and the classes count confirmed plus reported findings no `rejected` one matches.
- Merge-prone files (T-1161, `fmlanes.hot_files`): a conflicting `fm lane merge` logs `lane_conflict` (files from `git diff --name-only -z --diff-filter=U`, ≤50) after `git merge --abort`; `fm lane brief` warns under `## Warning` (JSON `warnings`) when the task's scope matches a file other lanes conflicted on twice or more in 30 days — distinct tasks, never the task's own conflicts.
- Dropped builder redo (T-1823): `fm lane rm ID --why REASON` (main thread only) drops a builder's work: a Claude-Code-locked unmerged worktree goes too (the branch is kept), and `audits/ID.dropped.json` holds the reason, the kept branch and the last `merge_refused` enforce reason from the 500-event ledger tail (`_last_refusal`). The next `fm lane brief ID` adds "First attempt (dropped)" with them (the check in a fence). The lane_rm event carries `dropped`.
- Cut-off lane repair (T-2257): a lane whose creation was interrupted (0-byte branch ref, every file empty) fails `fm doctor` (also any 0-byte ref under refs/heads, each ref sized in its own try), and `fm lane rm ID --why` moves the folder, the ref, the worktree admin dir and the branch reflog aside into `state/<project>/lane-wreck/<id>/` (never deleting), removes the stale `locked` file (matched by absolute, relative or emptied `gitdir`), prunes the entry and frees the task; `_ref_state` says ok, missing, empty or error (a git failure is never read as missing).
- Stalled builders (T-1156, `fmlanes.stalled`): a lane whose builder process is live but whose newest signal (any tracked/untracked file mtime, the branch's last commit, the task's last ledger event in the 500-event tail) is older than `FM_STALL_MIN` minutes (default 15) reads "STALLED N min" in `fm lane list` (`stalled_min` in JSON, null otherwise) and `fm next`, with "stop it, then fm lane resume ID --force". Lanes whose last event is `lane_merge`/`hollow_return`, or with commits ahead and a clean tree (returned), are skipped; git calls time out at 10 s and the result is cached 60 s in `stalled.json` (`FM_STALL_CACHE_S`).
- Lanes (T-0134): a linked git worktree of a registered repo is a lane of its project (`Project.lane`): it shares the project's state, its git work (snapshots, diffs, commits) happens in its own files, and it has its own active task: a task focused there records `meta.lane`, focus pauses only that lane's task, and `held_elsewhere`/`lane_view` keep each side's next, queue, inbox and `fm run` off the other's work (state lists it under `lanes`). The edit gate in a lane needs that lane's task. `fm lane new <id>` makes `<repo>.lanes/<id>` on branch `foreman/<id>` and gives the task to it; `fm lane list`; `fm lane rm <id>` refuses uncommitted work and ignored files (the folder would take them), prunes a lane folder deleted by hand (`fm lane list` shows it as missing), keeps an unmerged branch and returns the task to the queue. `fm tidy` lists a closed task's lane and merged `foreman/T-*` branches no lane holds; `--apply` removes them the same way (T-0186). Moving a task between checkouts retakes its start point there; `fm task set status=active` is refused (only `fm focus` starts a task). The shared view files (status.json, STATE.md…) and the `.foreman` mirror are always the main checkout's view (`main_view`); a lane session's own view is `fm ui`, computed live. `fm run --parallel N` (T-0167, at most 3) takes the next runnable task plus queued ones that can't collide with it or each other (S or M, planned, explicit scopes whose literal prefixes don't nest, nothing unfinished they depend on, nothing waiting on the user), runs each in its own lane as a fresh `claude -p` session, all at once, then integrates the finished ones serially: the main checkout must be on a branch with no uncommitted tracked changes, checked again right before the fast-forward (T-0174: the gates can take an hour); the lane is rebased on that branch, gated there (`fm check`, when the project has checks), fast-forwarded into it and removed. Anything else keeps the lane and its branch and says why (a timed-out gate or git included); an unfinished lane with nothing in it is removed so its task returns to the queue, one with work stays for a person or the next run; a lane that can't be made is skipped for the run. An interrupt or error while the sessions run stops every one (each runs in its own process group, which Ctrl-C doesn't reach) and removes each lane nobody worked in. Scopes are compared as normalised paths (a leading `./` or a doubled slash changes nothing; `{` counts as a wildcard). Each lane logs a `run_session` (minutes, lane); a timeout kills the session's process group. A usage-limit hit stops new lanes (one task at a time from then on, which waits it out). A batch of one runs as before, in the main checkout. `fm lane merge ID` (T-0948) re-runs each criterion's verify command in the lane's worktree first (the runner `fm task evidence --run` uses, nothing recorded) and refuses red (the command and its last 15 lines) or a brief with no verify command; `--unverified REASON` waives it from the main thread only, logging a `lane_unverified` ledger event before the merge. The task's new start point (the main checkout's pre-merge commit and files) is saved before `git merge` runs (T-0961, a `lane_merge_base` event), so a merge that dies midway still measures the task from there; a failed, aborted merge puts the old one back. Git runs through one helper, `fmcore._git` (`proc=True` returns the CompletedProcess, a timeout as returncode 124 and a git that can't run as 127 (never git's own 1, "no match"); fmbench's filter-driver lookup fails closed on either, and fmsecrets reports a listing it couldn't make instead of a clean audit): fmlanes, fmsecrets, fmmap and fmbench (`_safe_git`, with the session's filter drivers, hooks and fsmonitor off) call it with their own timeouts; only fmguard keeps its own, it is hook-hot.
- Lane claims (T-1068, `fmbus`): `fm lease take FILE[::SYM]...` refuses the whole claim when any target overlaps another session's live lease on that file (same symbol, or either is `*`), naming the session and its task (`--json`: `{refused: [...]}`); `--warn` stores an advisory claim that never blocks. Leases are keyed per session (take, preclaim, after_edit). `fm lane brief` pre-claims the task's concrete scope files as `lane-<id>` (skipping files another session holds, listed under Claims); they are advisory (T-1138: lanes edit separate worktrees and the merge settles overlaps; a file-wide pre-claim had stalled a parallel builder), while an explicit `fm lease take` still denies another session's edit; lane rm, merge and resume release them.
- Racing builders (T-1069, `fmlanes.race/score/pick`): `fm lane race T` (S/M only, once) clones T's brief as its "b" side under a new id (race_of, a Race section asking for a different approach), since fm focus holds one lane per task, and writes a builder brief for each side. `fm lane race T --score` runs each side's criteria in its own worktree (guard-checked) and counts lines changed since its base; the leader passes the most criteria, then changes the fewest lines. Both sides are judged on the original's checks as they were at race start (race_checks), never on criteria a side edited. A race is checked whole before anything is written (S/M, unworked, planned, verify commands, two free builder slots) and rolled back if a brief can't be written; race sides take no pre-claims. `fm lane pick W` runs the race's checks in W's worktree (refused if one fails), merges W through merge() (criteria, guard), drops the other ("lost the race to W", or the original "done in W"), logs race_pick, then removes both lanes (the loser's unlocked first; a cleanup that can't finish is reported as pending, never fatal); the losing branch is kept.

</details>

### Evidence, gates and verification

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Unaccounted clauses (T-2138): when the final message claims done and `fm clauses` left parts of the last request mapped to no task (meta `clauses.open`, set within the last day), the Stop hook holds the stop once per clause (a clause already named never holds it again; an undated list counts as stale), naming up to five of them: capture each, answer it, or `fm clauses --note N "<why>"`. It runs after the evidence gate, never on a `stop_hook_active` stop, and logs a `stop_gate` event. T-2146: UserPromptSubmit records the list itself when a prompt splits into 3 to 12 clauses (fewer is a plain ask, more a paste; a slash command, a headless worker's prompt and a sensitive project record nothing), and the gate and `fm clauses` re-account it on read, so a task made after the prompt covers its clause; the list belongs to the session that sent it, and a clause with fewer than 2 content words ("Please fix it.") is never recorded, since no task could match it.
- Print mode (T-2192): `claude -p` returns only the session's last message, so `fmhooks.stop` (a wrapper over `_stop`) adds `PRINT_LAST` to every hold when `_headless()` (`sdk-cli` or an fm run worker), early returns too: end with the whole answer restated. s31-006's Rust exam sessions lost their findings JSON to a retro written after a hold.
- Test claims (T-0886): when the final message says the tests, suite, checks or gates pass (or are green), the Stop hook reads the turn's tool calls from the transcript (`fmhooks._turn_calls`: since the user's last prompt, the last 4 MB) and holds the stop once per message when a file tool edited a file after the last test run (or none ran after edits), or that run failed (is_error, or a failure in its last 300 characters; `fm task prove`'s intended red line aside). Test runs are Bash commands naming a runner (pytest, run.py, npm test, go test, cargo test, make test, fm check, fm task evidence/finish/prove --run…). A turn with no edits and no runs isn't judged; edits made through Bash (sed -i, a python heredoc) aren't seen.
- Ablation at close (T-1067): `fm task finish ID --ablate` runs the hunk proof on the linked tests (fm check's affected template) after the close's own checks, prints "N/M hunks proven" naming each hunk no test notices, and never fails or blocks the close (any error prints "ablation skipped: why"). One 600 s budget (FM_ABLATE_BUDGET) covers it all; each run times out at what is left, and hunks it doesn't reach are "not run". No evidence line is recorded; under --json its result is the finish's `ablation` key.
- Hunk mutation proof (T-0271, pass 2): `fm task prove ID --hunks --run CMD [--max 20]` diffs the task's code files (not tests or docs) from its start snapshot at -U0, makes a detached worktree of the current tree (a commit-tree of it), and for each hunk reverts it alone (by its position in the new file, T-0286; new Python files per top-level definition) — in a checkout shipping Foreman's hook (its committed-library fallback), HEAD is set to the task's base while checks run, so a mutation that crashes the hook falls back to pre-change code and fails instead of passing on the unmutated tree (T-0297), runs CMD there, and restores the file; every mutated and restored version gets its own whole-second mtime so mtime+size caches (.pyc, make) can't reuse the other version. The check must pass on the unchanged scratch tree first (ignored files like .venv aren't copied, and a check that fails anyway would "prove" every hunk); after each hunk the scratch tree is reset and cleaned (a deleted file that -R recreated goes again); a timeout counts as not tested. A hunk whose removal still passes is unproven. Only a complete run (every hunk tested and proven, none cut by --max) is passing evidence (`N/N hunks proven`); anything else is an inconclusive line naming what's unproven or untested (never counts as passing); exit 1. The working tree is never touched.
- `fm audit run ID [--lens L …] [--model M] [--timeout S]` (T-1186, `fmauditrun.py`): the audit lenses (the default five, plus `design` when the prepared brief has a design section) as headless `claude -p` reviewers, 3 at a time, each with Read/Grep/Glob only, no MCP, project/local settings, `--max-budget-usd` at the per-command cap (lowered to what a USD contract has left), all reading the frozen brief `fm audit prep` writes. Refused when `fm contract` is over or `fm budget` is spent; stops between batches when the contract ends. Each `## <lens>: ok | changes needed` reply becomes audit evidence; its findings become `finding` events with verdict `reported` (≤20 per lens, redacted); a reply in another shape records nothing and the command exits 1 naming the lens.
- Gate and CLI friction (T-0274, pass 2): `fm check` reads the names of failing tests from each gate that really ran (unittest FAIL/ERROR lines, pytest FAILED, go --- FAIL, cargo … FAILED) into the project's `flakes.json` as pending for (command, tree); the same command passing on the same tree turns them flaky (count, last, command) and fm check says so ("fix the race, don't rerun past it"); a known-flaky test failing again is named as such. The ledger never changes a verdict: T-0047's rule still decides (a gate failing in under 120 s is rerun once, and a pass on the rerun counts unless it keeps flaking) — its first run's failing names go to the ledger too, as flaky. unittest names must be dotted (module.Class.test), so log lines like `ERROR: timeout (30)` aren't tests; damaged entries are dropped one by one, and pending names of removed gates are pruned. An unknown fm subcommand or flag ends its usage error with the nearest real one (difflib over the choices and every subparser's options; a mistyped flag is named first, since it shifts the positionals).
- Second reads (T-0276, pass 3; the review family as one primitive, `fmsecond.py`): `fm second plan ID [--model sonnet]` sends the brief's request, interpretation, assumptions, criteria, non-goals, approach, risks and steps to a tool-less child on another model and saves its ranked objections and `Verdict: proceed | revise | rethink` as the brief's Plan review section (data, not instructions; full autonomy still self-approves after reading it). `fm second debate ID --review NAME` writes `audits/ID.debate.md` embedding a saved review; one foreman:fm-reviewer confirms or refutes each finding from the code, and only CONFIRMED ones count. `fm second session [--if-due]` reads the user's messages in the newest earlier transcript of this project (no tool results, notifications or command wrappers) and the open work, and captures each request a child calls unanswered as a confirm-first (explore) self item, skipping ones Foreman already holds (70% word overlap, re-read under the lock); what leaves for the child is redacted; the day is claimed atomically under the state lock before the child runs (two sessions starting together run one review), and a failed review is logged (`second_session` event); once a day, never in a sensitive project; SessionStart starts it detached on a new session when due and no other session was active in the last 15 minutes. Children run with FOREMAN_NO_BACKGROUND=1. `fm audit prep` names installed review skills per lens (routing.json `lens_words`, then `lens_builtin`) and how to record their findings as that lens. Children use fmideas.child_cmd and fm budget — and, since T-0295, every tool-less child (ideas lenses and research researchers in parallel via `fmideas.run_children`; the oracle, fm second (whose `_child` the bench judge and soak shadow call too), and fm evolve's mutation via `fmideas.run_child`) runs through one of two runners: a scratch folder, one deadline, `FOREMAN_NO_BACKGROUND=1`, spend recorded. One test-file pattern too: `c.TESTISH` (fmbench and fmmap alias it). The four word-overlap rules stay separate on purpose (each compares differently).
- Gates by path (T-0126): `fm check paths N GLOB…` declares the files gate N covers (meta `check_paths`); a plain `fm check` then skips that gate, recorded as a pass that says so, when its newest run passed (same environment, within 12 h) and `git diff` between that run's tree and now touches nothing under its globs. A gate without paths, a failed or stale last run, a pruned tree or `--fresh` runs it. A skip records the time and tree of the real run it rests on, so a chain of skips still expires 12 h after that run and diffs against its tree (a write by another gate counts); `--no-renames` makes a file moved out of the paths count. Here: the e2e roundtrip covers `plugin/**` and `install.sh`, the hook bench `plugin/lib/**`, `plugin/hooks/**`, `plugin/bin/**` and its own script and fixtures, the mod tests `mods/foreman-ui/**` (they mock fm); doctor and the python suite (which also reads the docs) always run. Inputs outside the repo (the roundtrip reads `~/.claude/settings.json`) aren't seen, as with the whole-tree cache.
- Evidence and gates: `fm task evidence ID --step N --run "<cmd>"` runs the command (bash, repo root) and records `exit N · <last two output lines>`, marked ✗ when it failed and `[ran]` because fm ran it; a step or criterion whose newest run failed can't be marked done, and typed evidence can't clear that or forge fm's `[ran]`/`[tree]` marks. `fm check --evidence` records one verdict line for the whole run. Evidence lines carry the worktree id they were recorded at, so `fm next` and the statusline agree with the done gate after evidence-only updates. `fm check` runs the project's gate commands (kept in the project's meta; `fm check add|rm|list`) and exits 1 on any failure; the guard checks `--run` and `fm check add` commands like typed ones. `fm audit prep ID` writes the diff since the commit the task was focused at (untracked files included) to the project's `audits/ID.diff` and prints one fm-reviewer brief per lens from `references/audit.md` (`--note` adds this round's focus to each, and each brief lists up to six CRITICAL/HIGH/MEDIUM headlines that earlier reviews saved for that lens, research files named with it, newest first; T-1501: a diff that touches UI (`fmcore.ui_paths`: markup, styles, components, the foreman-ui pane's hooks, TUI screens, not tests) adds the advisory `design` lens at any tier, an art director, an accessibility reviewer and a first-time user, with the existing screenshots the task names listed for the reviewer to read); `fm research add NAME --from-agent FILE` keeps a subagent's final report from its output file, not the transcript. A request another task did is closed with `fm task drop ID --done-in HOST` (done, linked both ways); only never-started requests qualify, and the host must have evidence of its own, so neither side skips the gates. The statusline's progress line names the current step; `fm doctor` names the latest hook error. Debugging ledger (T-0207): `fm task hypo ID add|mark` keeps hypotheses and probe results in the brief's Hypotheses section; while one is open and none is confirmed, `fm next` points at it before the step. Evidence quality (T-0261): `fm task evidence … --inconclusive` records a run that neither proves nor disproves (`[inconclusive]`); it is kept for the record but no gate, red→green or grade ever counts it, a step with only such runs can't be marked done, and `fm next` asks for a sharper check. `fm task assume ID add "<fact>"` writes `[assumed]` lines into the Assumptions section and `fm task assume ID verify N --run CMD | --evidence HOW` turns any bullet into `[verified: how]` (exit 0) or `[false: how]`; done on an M/L task lists the ones still unverified (a warning, not a gate). `fm surprise "<expected> → <observed>"` logs where the model of the code was wrong (the project's `surprises.jsonl` and the ledger): `fm friction` has a surprises section, `fm recall` returns them as `surprise` hits, and the pane's feed shows them. `--bench CMD [--repeat K]` (T-2284) times CMD K times (default 5) on the task's start tree (a throwaway worktree) and on the current tree and records the before → after medians as run evidence and in Measurements, which satisfies a PERFORMANCE close in place of typed `--numbers`.
- Affected tests per runner (T-1131, T-1135): `fm check affected --for .EXT "<cmd>"` gives a test-file extension its own command (meta affected_by; no command removes it). affected() runs the default template on the tests no --for covers and each --for command once when any of its tests changed, joined with &&. A changed test file no command can run is named (`unrunnable` in JSON) instead of reading as 'No tests linked'. This repo runs .tsx tests with the mod harness.
- Auto-evidence: a Bash command that is exactly an acceptance criterion's verify command (whitespace aside) is recorded by the PostToolUse/PostToolUseFailure hook as that criterion's evidence, with the real exit code and last output lines (redacted) and the `[ran]` mark, so the command needn't run a second time through `fm task evidence --run`. T-1256: the same command piped to `tail`, `head` or `grep` (with any `2>&1`; `_unpiped` strips the filters) counts too, but its exit is the filter's, so only the output decides: the runner's own pass summary (`fmcore.passed_summary`: unittest, pytest, jest, cargo, go, mocha) records a pass, a failure summary a failure, neither nothing. A piped run through a verify's runner verifies the current step the same way.
- Cheaper runs: `fm check` reuses the newest full run when it was on the exact same tree (worktree id), with the same gates, and all passed (`--fresh` reruns); `fm check affected '<cmd with {tests} or {names}>'` sets a template and `fm check --affected` runs only the tests `fm map` links to files changed since the task started (T-2148: plus `broke`, the tests a closed baseline-red task's gate named failing, linked to the files its fix commit changed, its docs only when docs were the whole fix; so an edit to MASTER.md, a hub with no co-change pairs, runs test_docs; the full gates still decide before commit and done). `fm audit prep` writes the review brief to `audits/ID.review.md` and prints its path (`--print` shows it); the reviewer's prompt just points at it. UserPromptSubmit restates the task state (Active, Next, Inbox, autonomy) only when it changed since the last prompt in that session (`state/sessions/<id>.note`, cleared at SessionStart); event notes (intake, overrides, approvals) always go through.
- Verification memory: a FIX task can't be done without red→green proof, a command fm ran that failed and later passed (its regression test), or a "Regression test" section saying why there is none. T-2222: so can't any task that changed a path the repo's `.fmproved` lists (globs, one a line; `fmcli._proved_gaps`, `Brief.proof_gap(force=True)`); Foreman's own lists fmexam.py and fmgenesis.py, where the exam and the scorecard decide done. `fm check` times each gate, reruns a failure once (passing on the rerun is labelled flaky and passes, unless that gate was flaky in 2+ of the last 20 runs, when it fails as a likely race; gates slower than two minutes aren't rerun) and labels a failure pre-existing when that gate also failed in the last run before the current task (`check_run` ledger events). Failure memory: a failed Bash command's telling error line (paths cut, numbers zeroed) is recorded per task in `failures.jsonl`; when a finished task met the same failure, the PostToolUseFailure hook points to it ("came up in T-x (done: …; lesson: …)").
- Recurring failures (T-2272): a failure signature recorded under 3+ distinct tasks, closed or not, captures one root-cause FIX (keyed once ever; a `captured` row in failures.jsonl spares later failures the brief scan); test assertions (the baseline capture's job), tallies, Foreman's own messages and bare builtin exception lines never capture.
- Done gates from the diff (T-0071): `fm gates [TYPE TIER | ID]` lists what `fm task done` checks (T-1955: a task id uses its type and tier) — evidence, audits by tier (plus adversary when a change touches auth, crypto, secrets, exec or deserialization), docs impact and a lesson for M/L, red→green for FIX (`fm task prove ID --run CMD` runs the test on the start tree with only the task's test files, then on the current tree), before/after numbers for PERFORMANCE (`fm task finish --numbers`, T-1956), a test run before the first project-file edit for CLEAN (none when it edits none, T-1982), and a "scope:" reason newer than any out-of-scope edit. Done prints a verification grade (strong/ok/weak) and records Files touched, which feed recall's "start here" and edit-time lesson tripwires (`tripwires.json`). S tasks close with `fm task finish ID --run CMD --audit HOW`. Close warnings (never refusals) open with one `N warnings: kinds` line, then the details, among them debug scaffolding and merge conflict markers in the task's added lines; `fm task finish` ends with `Landed: committed SHA · pushed|not pushed` or `Landed: not committed (N files uncommitted)` (T-0956). `fm sentinel` re-runs the criteria checks recent finished tasks passed.
- Also (T-0071 rounds F–H): a bare "status" prompt is answered by the UserPromptSubmit hook from Foreman's state without the model; edits get one-time notes when they leave Python/JSON unparsable or touch generated/vendored files, and those notes re-arm after compaction; `fm why FILE[:LINE]` traces code to commits, tasks and lessons; `fm pr ID` prints a PR description; `fm audit scan [--base REV]` runs the mechanical pre-audit (debug leftovers, weakened tests, dangling names, missing companion edits, generated files, sensitive code) on any branch; `fm map` adds CI workflow commands and co-change pairs; `fm check --fail-fast`, environment-failure labels, 12-hour cached passes (T-1963: a run during which the tree changed, a writing gate or an edit, keeps the tree it tested, so the changed tree gets no cached pass or evidence; every full run sets meta `gates_running` with its root, and an Edit/Write in that tree meanwhile gets a one-time note), and a zero-test green run counts as a failure.
- Close gates (T-1117): `fm task finish` runs `fmcli._close_affected` (the affected-tests command for the files changed since the task's base, skipping another task's red-first files, and since T-1968 any file only another task touched: `_theirs`, which `_task_files` also leaves out of the scope and sensitive-file checks) and refuses a failure unless the last full `check_run` already listed it (`failing` names per gate). `_baseline` in `cmd_check` captures one urgent FIX per gate and failing set (`baseline: true`), only for a gate that names failing tests, on a tree that didn't change during the run, and with no task mid-work unless the failure predates it (a sentinel run counts only on the tree it started from; an environment failure or a failing set the flake ledger knows is skipped); `_close_affected` reruns once, notes environment failures, and `--skip-affected WHY` waives it on the record and names an open one a green run turned green. `_sentinel` starts a detached full `fm check` after a close when the last full run is older than 3 closes or 60 minutes (`FOREMAN_SENTINEL=off|sync`).
- Survival grading (T-1060): `fmoutcomes.outcomes` adds `survival` per close: lost when a revert, a FIX naming it or a correction (read from the archived ledger months too; a smoke run is too weak a link) lands within `SURVIVE_DAYS` (30) of the close (`lost_at`, `lost_by`), survived once the window passes clean, else settling (`days_left`). `fm outcomes` prints the line and days since a lost task (also in `fm digest`); `--settle` logs one `outcome` event per graded task, once, judged against every ledger file (the pane's History shows the lost ones); `--claims` (by close grade), `--regret` (lost tasks with their Approach, reversed decisions), `--graveyard` (lost and dropped), `--seen` (corrected mid-task or not).

</details>

### Guard and security

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Credential reads and exfiltration (T-1224, `fmguard`): `classify_read` refuses reads of the home folder's credential folders and files (~/.ssh but its public parts, ~/.aws but config, ~/.kube, ~/.gnupg, gcloud, gh, ~/.netrc, ~/.npmrc, ~/.pypirc, ~/.git-credentials, Claude Code's .credentials.json, ~/.docker/config.json) by a reading command's operands (`_read_targets`: tar -C moves relative ones, tar/rsync list options are reads), a < redirect or the Read tool; an unknown name is guessed from the command's words as a write's is. `_sent_files` names what curl/wget attach, and the environment (`_prints_environment`) piped into a sender is refused. `_mode_targets` refuses chmod/chown/chgrp -R or a mode that opens write to others on /, ~ or a system folder (serial dev boxes and tightening allowed). Measured by `site/bench_safety.py` (24 dangerous, 25 everyday).
- Script reading (T-0275): the Bash guard reads what a command runs: script files run by bash/sh/zsh/source/`.`, by their own path, or by python FILE, hidden exec vectors (tar -I, ssh, rsync -e, git -c core.*, vim +, find -exec, env-loaded code such as BASH_ENV, NODE_OPTIONS, PROMPT_COMMAND) and nested scripts (a chain too deep is refused), and judges their commands as if typed. A parse over its time budget falls back to a bounded literal scan (rm of home or root, curl|sh, state writes, user-only fm verbs) rather than refusing (decision 2026-10-16). Verdicts are cached in the state dir by content hash, cwd, depth and guard version, with every nested script's hash (or absence) revalidated on a hit.
- Self-improvement pass 1 (T-0269, from research brainstorm-pass-1): the guard treats `git stash list` and `git stash show` as read-only (every other stash form is still a worktree write), and its review closed git's own channels for every subcommand: `--output[=]FILE` (and format-patch `-o`) is a write target; a command git runs itself — exec variables (GIT_PAGER, PAGER, GIT_EXTERNAL_DIFF, GIT_SSH_COMMAND, editors, askpass), `-c` keys whose value is a command (core.pager/editor/fsmonitor/sshCommand, diff.external, textconv, filters, merge drivers, credential helpers, gpg programs) and a `-c alias.NAME=…` it then calls (expanded with its global options) — is checked like a typed command; `--exec-path=DIR`, GIT_EXEC_PATH, `--config-env` and environment config that set such keys are refused (`system`); value-taking global options (`--config-env`, `--namespace`) are skipped correctly when finding the subcommand; T-0270 closed the rest of git's channels: `git config` setting a command-valued key is checked as that command, an alias or include/hooksPath/protocol.ext.allow is refused (`system`), and `--file`/`-f` is a write target; subcommand options that run a command (rebase -x/--exec, bisect run, submodule foreach, difftool -x/--extcmd, grep -O/--open-files-in-pager, --upload-pack/--receive-pack/--exec, filter-branch's filters) are checked as typed; `--template` and config from elsewhere (GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL/SYSTEM, GIT_TEMPLATE_DIR, GIT_EXEC_PATH, HOME/XDG_CONFIG_HOME on a git command, `-c include.path`) are refused; exec variables are read wherever they are set (anything before the real command, behind wrappers and keywords like timeout/env -u/do, NAME+= included, and export/declare), `clone -c/--config` and `init.templateDir` are covered, abbreviated long options (--exe) are expanded, and GIT_TRACE*/GIT_INDEX_FILE with a path, archive -o, bundle create, worktree add, init, format-patch -o/--output-directory, --separate-git-dir, checkout-index --prefix and submodule add are write targets; `fm next` on an S task whose edits reach three or more non-test files says it has outgrown S and how to re-tier (M brings the plan gate and lens audits; code files only, from the newest 2000 ledger events, since it runs on every prompt); fm ideas children get the context pack first and the lens last, so siblings and later rounds share a prefix the prompt cache can reuse.
- `fm guard mine [--days N] [--json]` (T-1187, `fmreplay.mine`): this project's real guard refusals from its Claude Code transcripts (`projects/<encoded root>` and `<encoded root>-*` for subdirectory and worktree sessions; lines prefiltered before parsing, ≤500 refusals), each with its command (redacted, control characters stripped), the rule, today's default replay verdict, and what the session did next: rewritten-not-refused, rewritten-refused, retried-unchanged or abandoned (parallel sibling calls skipped).
