Text output groups by rule with 5 examples each. A refusal is matched with or without the `<Event> hook error: ` prefix Claude Code records.
- Labelled guard cases (T-1162, `fmreplay.add_case/drift/report_cases`): `fm guard wrong "CMD" --should allow|block [--cwd DIR] [--why TEXT]` appends a case to `plugin/tests/fixtures/guard_cases.jsonl` (once per command+cwd; the opposite label for a stored case and key-shaped literals are refused; cwd stored relative to the repo root). `test_guard_cases` reads that file directly (≥6 cases, no broken lines, no drift); `fm replay --cases [--capture]` reports it (exit 1 on drift or broken lines); `fm night` on Foreman's own repo captures one FIX per drifted case (deduped across archived briefs; an allow-labelled drift says not to loosen the guard without the user's yes). The corpus never changes a verdict: only tests read it. T-1517: `plugin/tests/fixtures/guard_allow.jsonl` is the allow-corpus (everyday commands, all `should: allow`, seeded from the allow labels above); with no `FOREMAN_GUARD_CASES` both files are replayed (`fmreplay._corpus_paths`; cases meet on command and folder, `_where`, not id: the same label once, opposite labels one broken line with both kept) and `fm guard wrong` refuses a label either file contradicts; an allow-corpus that is missing, unreadable or under `ALLOW_MIN` (40) cases is one broken line, so the replay and the safety gate go red. `AllowCorpus` replays it under a temp home and with this checkout as Foreman's home, so a tree-dependent case can't sneak in. Unable (`fmreplay.unable`: the guard raised, so `verdicts` holds `error: …`) is its own result everywhere and never a pass: `drift` gives `got: unable` (`fm replay --cases` counts it), `fm replay --candidate` exits 1 on any, plain `fm replay` lists them, exits 1 and never writes one into the baseline, and the friction digest says the guard is unable to judge rather than "still blocks".
- `fm guard try "CMD" [--cwd DIR] [--tool Edit --path P] [--json]` (T-1146): the hook's own decision for a command or edit as this session would get it (active task, grants, cwd), never run and spending no grant or ask slot (those are spent only in the PreToolUse hook). `fm replay --cmd` answers without grants, so the two can differ.
- Session-review security fixes (T-0291, batch of T-0288/T-0289/T-0290; found by the whole-session review that closed T-0258, each logged as an escape against the task that shipped it): `fm export agents --out` writes only inside the project (real paths, so a symlink out is outside too) — the guard can't see fm's own writes, so fm refuses them; items `fm second session` captures carry `confirm: true`, which `needs_approval` honours in full autonomy too (a child's reading of a transcript isn't the user's ask; `fm task set ID approved=true` after their yes); bench git empties every filter driver configured anywhere (local, worktree, global or system — a replay could have written any of them) on every call — worktree add, hidden-test checkout, the judge's add and diff — while the lfs driver always runs git-lfs's own fixed commands (`git-lfs clean|smudge -- %f`, `git-lfs filter-process`), never the configured values — T-0299: a prefix check had let `git-lfs smudge -- %f; <cmd>` through — so LFS repos still check out content. Review hardening: confirm is a user-only grant (above); `fm export` also refuses targets under `.git/` or `.claude/`; `write_atomic` writes through an exclusive `mkstemp` temp file (a guessable temp name could be a planted symlink) and keeps an existing file's mode.
- Session-review follow-ups (T-0296, batch of T-0292/T-0293/T-0294): docs drift fixed (the CLI line, the fmbench file map, the T-0271 bullet); `fm doctor` adds a `routing` check (routing_problem over skills/routing.json — `c.routing()` returns {} for a broken file, which would silently drop hints and --split groups) and scopes `integrity`: damage in Foreman's own repo or the current project fails, another project's warns; `fm replay --cwd` without `--cmd` and `fm second debate --model` are refused instead of ignored; `fm bench duel` takes only an installed plugin (its id or its folder), since the rival runs in a bypass-mode replay.
- Install checks (T-1049, `fmdeps.py`): PreToolUse parses npm/pip/uv/poetry/pipx/yarn/cargo/go install commands (also inside `bash -c` and `python3.X -m pip`) and adds a note, never a block, for a name one edit from a popular package or first published in the last 30 days. Registry lookups share one 1.2 s budget, skip popular, `@scope/` and private-registry names, re-check redirects as public https, and are off under `FOREMAN_NO_NET=1` or `fm sensitive on`; `fm deps check "<cmd>"` runs it by hand.
- Approvals: `fm ask` raises a permission prompt (PreToolUse `ask`); the `PermissionRequest` hook notes that the dialog was really shown, and PostToolUse of that same tool call grants. A refused prompt is forgotten at your next message. The dialog text has control and bidi characters removed; unknown options or categories are refused; under `fm run` (`claude -p`, nobody to answer) fm ask is refused and the task gets blocked instead. Where a dialog can't be tied to the call, fm ask falls back to a pending request answered by your next reply ("yes…" grants, anything else cancels). A question left in a reply's text is sent back once by the Stop hook to be asked as an AskUserQuestion prompt (or decided and recorded).
- Stored commands (T-0901): everything fm keeps to run later gets the guard's checks twice. When the hook sees it stored (`fmguard._fm_stored`): a verify command in `--ac "x :: CMD"`, `--verify CMD`, `fm task ac … add "x :: CMD"` or an intake `DONE-WHEN: x :: CMD` line, `fm check affected`'s template and `fm notify`'s command; and when fm is about to run it (`fmcli.guard_stored`, the task's grants, standing yes and trust applied as the hook would): `fm task finish` (every command checked before any runs), `fm check` gates and `--repeat`, `fm check --affected` and the ambient runner (a refusal is logged), `fm smoke`'s command check, `fm sentinel`'s rechecks (a refused one is skipped and named), `fm resume`'s last green check, `fm task packet --check`, `fm task prove --vacuous` and `fm at` (`_run_at`), stacked commits' checks, and the notify command. The hook reads an fm call's words as argparse does, options left out (`_fm_words`), so `fm check --fresh add CMD` or `fm notify --test CMD` can't hide CMD; it also reads `fm smoke set cmd`, `fm task assume --check`, `--same`, `--stack-check`, an Acceptance criteria `--text` and `fm record`/`fm whyred` CMD. A notify command's `$1` is read as `rm -rf ~`, so one that runs its message as code (`bash -c "$1"`, `eval`) is refused while `notify-send "$1"` passes. That second check covers what never passed the hook: a brief stored before, one `fm sync` brought in from another clone. `fm quiet -- CMD` and `fm at T -- CMD` are read as typed (T-0898).
- Credentials and commits (T-0132): `fm secrets` names (never prints) credentials in the working tree (tracked and untracked, not ignored), in Claude Code's config (user and project settings `env`, `.mcp.json` and `~/.claude.json` MCP servers: a key format anywhere, or a literal under a secret-sounding name in `env`/`headers`; the file's permissions; whether git tracks or ignores a project file) and, with `--history`, the oldest commit on any branch that added one no longer in the tree; it exits 1 on any finding. Patterns are fmcore's redaction ones plus literals under a secret's name (quoted in code, bare to the line's end in `.env`/YAML/INI-style config); a line marked `pragma: allowlist secret` is skipped; diffs are read with git's prefix, binary and textconv settings overridden. `fm task finish --commit` commits only the task's files (`git commit -- <files>`, so anything staged before stays staged), scans exactly those lines first and refuses (unstaging them) on a match or when git can't show them, adds a `Foreman-Task: T-…` trailer that `fm why` reads, and logs the commit in the brief.
- Worker confinement (T-1607): `fmlandlock` applies Landlock (Linux 5.13+, through ctypes) in an fm code worker's child before exec: writes only beneath `writable_for(root)` (the tree, its git dir and common dir, /tmp, /var/tmp, /dev, ~/.claude, ~/.claude.json, existing tool caches; never the whole home), reads untouched, inherited by everything it starts. `fmcode.worker_confinement` decides; the run notes once whether the worker is confined; `FOREMAN_CONFINE=0` turns it off; no Landlock means unconfined as before, and fm doctor's "worker confinement" line says which.
- Guard trust (T-1606): `fmcore.secret_kinds` names what `redact` would replace; UserPromptSubmit adds one note when the user's message holds one (kind only, never the value). `fm ask` keeps `meta.ask_tally` per session and from the third same-category ask (FATIGUE_ASKS) suggests batching. `fm doctor --wrong "CMD" [--should allow|block] [--why] [--cwd]` stores a labelled case (fmreplay.add_case) and, when the guard disagrees today, captures one FIX tagged `[guard-case ID]`.
- Guard ratchets (T-1605): `test_guard_hardening.Mutation` drops each category the labelled corpora are refused with (an in-process mutant of `fmguard.check`) and needs the corpus to go red; `fixtures/guard_untested.json` lists the categories no case exercises and must equal the measured list (it only shrinks); `gen_evasions.stacked` adds three seeded two-transform stacks per dangerous command (pair order by sha256 of STACK_SEED, command and pair; sh-fed wrappers take POSIX inners only), and no gap may be a dangerous command let through.
- Guard oracle (T-1553): `fmoracle` runs a line in a throwaway bwrap sandbox (read-only root, no network, empty tmpfs home, own /tmp and /run, ulimit -v/-f) whose PATH holds nothing, so bash's command_not_found_handle (readonly, with a readonly cd that keeps the folder by path) records each program the shell would start with its real argv and folder; each line runs with stubs failing then succeeding, and path helpers (mktemp, basename, dirname, realpath, readlink, date) run for real inside. `disagreements` judges every recorded argv alone (plainest quoting) in its folder: a refusal where the whole line passed is a misread; a sandbox that didn't start or a guard that raised is unable. `fm replay --oracle [--cmd TEXT --cwd DIR]` over the labelled cases; no bwrap → says so, runs nothing. Ceiling: programs reached without a PATH lookup (absolute paths, PATH=, command -p, exec, nested bash -c) aren't recorded.
- Guard mutation (T-1066): `fmmutate` ast-parses fmguard.py into rule sites, writes each mutant into a temp copy of the plugin tree (baseline run first, PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE, per-mutant timeout with a process-group kill) and runs `run.py -p 'test_guard*.py'`; `fm bench guard --mutate [--weaken] [--only CAT] [--max N] [--tests CMD]`, `--coverage` for the corpus view (fmreplay).
- Outbound and sandbox (T-1086): `fmhooks._outbound` logs `outbound` {tool, target} for WebFetch/WebSearch/MCP tools and Bash network commands at command position (host only; queries redacted); `fm outbound [ID]` (fmledger.cmd_outbound) counts them per task. `fm sandbox on|off|allow DOMAIN` sets the opt-in sandbox for `fm run`/`fm night` (fmserve: a cached real probe of bwrap+socat or sandbox-exec; settings with the allowed domains and writes for the project, caches and state); `--sandbox`/`--no-sandbox` per run.
- Read approvals (T-0974): `fm approve-reads on|off` sets `approve_reads` in the project's meta (on is self-authorize: the guard refuses it from Claude). `fmhooks.permission_request` then answers Claude Code's dialog with `hookSpecificOutput.decision = {behavior: allow}` when `fmguard.read_only(cmd)` holds: every part an allowlisted reader (`_READS`, or a reading git subcommand in `_GIT_READS`) without its writing, exec or never-ending flags; redirects only to /dev/null; no backtick, `$`, process substitution, newline, background, assignment, subshell or path-named program. Logged as `read_approved`.

</details>

### Hooks, notes and steering

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Steering as data (T-0264): a correction that says never/don't/stop X (UserPromptSubmit) also keeps X's key words in the project's `vetoes.json` (newest 30, one per set of words); PreToolUse adds a note quoting the user when a Bash command, or an edit (its path plus the edit verbs), carries every key word — a note, not a block — and the pane shows the newest one (✋). Each clause is read on its own ("No, I don't think so, never deploy on fridays" keeps deploy/fridays; opinions like "don't think" are skipped), curly apostrophes count, and `fm vetoes` lists them (`rm N` drops one that no longer holds). A veto that should stop, not note, becomes a user rule (T-0971, hookify's idea): `fm rule add PATTERN [--on bash|path] [--block|--warn] [--why T]` keeps a Python regex in the project's `rules.json` (compiled at add time, at most `RULE_MAX` 300 characters; `--from-veto N` builds one from veto N's key words, its why the user's words); PreToolUse, after the guard, searches the first `RULE_SCAN` 4,000 characters of a Bash command (`bash`) or an edited file's absolute path (`path`), all rules within `RULE_BUDGET` 0.25 s (SIGALRM; a rule it couldn't finish refuses the call, fail closed): a block denies with the why (in quiet sessions too), a warn is a note. A rule is a text match, not a parse: quoting or a variable gets past one, so the guard's categories stay the safety net. `fm rule rm N` is the user's: the guard refuses it from Claude as self-authorize. The routing tables — stage words and UI words for installed-skill hints, Claude Code's built-in skills per stage, `fm audit --split`'s review groups — live in `plugin/skills/routing.json` (read by `c.routing()`, which returns `{}` unless `c.routing_problem` finds every table well-shaped: lists of short strings, plain built-in skill names, non-empty review groups); `fm evolve --target plugin/skills/routing.json` tunes them like any instruction file, and a revision that isn't a JSON object with the same top-level keys is never benched (an ablation writes `{}`).
- Session map (T-0215): SessionStart adds `fmmap.compact` — one ≤ 500-char line from the project's cached `map.json`; a missing or stale map starts a detached `fm map` for the next session; dropped first when the context exceeds its 2,000-char budget.
- Stale-fact tripwire (T-0113): `fm resume`, the session-start context and a re-focus name the paths and backticked code names (with an underscore or inner capital) a brief cites that existed where the task started (`task_base`: `git cat-file -e`, `git grep -w`) and are gone now, so a resumed task re-checks its plan; files it means to create never existed there and aren't flagged. Up to 20 paths and 8 names, 10 s per git call.
- Write validators (T-0947): each Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit in a Foreman project is validated by the PostToolUse hook, which runs synchronously for those tools (async for the rest) so it can answer `decision: block` with the problem; Claude, or the subagent that wrote it, must fix the file first. Built-ins run in the hook's process: a stack card (`skills/library/cards/<area>/<name>.md`) gets `fmlib.lint_card` (fm lib check's rules for one card), `*.json` must parse (JSON-with-comments files such as tsconfig aside), `*.py` must compile, and a `SKILL.md` or an agent definition (`agents/*.md` in a plugin or `.claude`) needs `name` and `description` in its frontmatter. The project's own: `fm check onwrite GLOB 'CMD {file}'` (meta `onwrite`; GLOB alone removes it, no words lists them; the command passes the guard when stored) runs in the repo root on writes whose repo-relative path matches, at most 5 s each; a failure blocks with its last 8 lines, a timeout only adds a note. Each block is an `enforce` ledger event.
- Session signals (T-0957): a Stop with an active or queued task that nothing kept going (no drive nudge) is an `idle_stop` ledger event, counted over 7 days by fm doctor's `idle stops` row (-v); a UserPromptSubmit whose `permission_mode` is `plan` holds the drive like a plan-only prompt; each user prompt is a `prompt` ledger event (redacted, at most 2000 chars, with the permission mode; none in a sensitive project); the first SessionStart after Foreman's version changes on this machine (`state/seen-version`) shows that version's top-level CHANGELOG bullets once as a systemMessage (a fresh install records the version silently).
- Outside text (T-0972): the sync PostToolUseValidate entry also matches WebFetch, WebSearch and `mcp__.*`; a result holding instruction-like text (ignore/disregard previous instructions, `<system>` or `<|im_start|>` tags, "you are now in X mode", "new instructions:", a note to the AI, "do not tell the user", curl/wget piped to a shell) gets one zero-token note that it is data, not the user's request, and an `injection_flag` ledger event; the first 200 KB are scanned. Bash output isn't scanned (a sync hook on every Bash call costs latency). A foreground stdin reader nothing feeds (T-1129: `cat` with no file or `-`, `tee`, python with no script, -c or -m, a shell with no script or -c; no pipe, heredoc, here-string or `<`; compound commands skipped) is denied before it runs: the Bash tool's stdin never closes, so it would hang until the timeout. Replayed on 30,507 recorded commands: 22 denied, all real hangs. A `pgrep -f`/`pkill -f` in command position whose pattern matches the command's own text (T-1839) is denied too, foreground or background: the shell running that text holds the pattern, so a wait on it never ends (a background watcher idled a session for hours) and a kill hits itself. `-x`, a bracketed letter (`'[f]m check'`) and a pipe into `grep -v pgrep|bash|while` pass. Replayed on 78,307 recorded commands: 224 denied, all self-matches. Command-shaped lines in those results (with a URL, pipe, redirect, flag value or path; the string leaves of a dict joined) are kept in `state/sessions/<sid>.taint.json` under a flock (T-1053): a Bash command holding one asks once per line per session; headless or under full autonomy it is denied every time, naming `fm task block`. The store survives compaction; a store error lets the command go on (an ask, never a guard).
- Test first at edit time (T-0969): the PreToolUse note chain's last note (`fmhooks._test_first_note`). The first Write/Edit of a non-test source file (`c.CODE`, not `c.TESTISH`, as fm check --affected tells tests apart) in a FIX or FEATURE task decides once per task (marker `:test-first` in `cards.json`, set before any git call): no failing run in the evidence (`` ` → ✗ exit ``) and no test file changed since `c.task_base` (`fmmap.changed`) gives one line to write the failing test and record it with fm task evidence. Docs, config and test-file edits don't spend the once; CLEAN, PERF, RESEARCH and other types get nothing; never a block, zero tokens.
- Postures and rivals (T-0963): `fm posture lite|full` sets `posture` in the project's meta; in a lite project (the user's to set: the guard refuses `fm posture lite` from Claude as self-authorize) `fmhooks.run` keeps PreToolUse and TaskCompleted (UNBREAKABLE) and returns before every event but PreToolUse (as `FOREMAN_QUIET` does, but per project and with the brief rule kept), and `fm posture` alone prints it. `fm compact-output shadow` installs the compact hook with `--shadow`: it prints nothing (the output passes untouched), writes no full-text file and appends `{ts, cmd_family, saved_chars}` to `state/outputs/shadow.jsonl`; `fm compact-output` then prints the would-have-cut total and the top three command families. One mode at a time: on, shadow or off. `fmcore.lint_verify` warns on a verify command that reads Foreman's state (`.foreman/`, `state/tasks` or `state/briefs`, `state/projects/<slug>/tasks`). `fm bench duel --vs none` runs the cases on Foreman, then with no plugin loaded (no `--plugin-dir`; the guard alone as a `--settings` PreToolUse hook with `FOREMAN_QUIET=1`, since replays run in bypass mode; fm not advertised (it may still be on PATH, pointed at the replay's own state), no seeded project), and gates Foreman against that bare reference. SubagentStop logs `hollow_return` when an `fm-builder` or `fm-reviewer` ends on a last message under 80 characters (`HOLLOW_CHARS`); it can't block (async), so `fm next` names today's with the transcript path.
- Hook circuit breaker (T-0087): a hook event (not PreToolUse) whose handler fails 3 times in a row is paused for 10 minutes (`state/logs/breaker.json`): skipped, nothing more logged; the band shows a warning row, the pane's hooks line says `paused: <event>`, `fm doctor` fails its hook-errors check, and hooks.log says when it runs again. After the pause one more failure pauses it again; a success clears it. The gates are never paused: the guard keeps failing closed and TaskCompleted keeps refusing unevidenced work. The breaker is shared by every project (the handlers are), and a damaged `breaker.json` is ignored entry by entry; without foreman-ui a pause shows only in `fm doctor` and hooks.log.

</details>

### Recall, lessons and self-improvement

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Knowledge out (T-0958): `fm recall --export-rules` writes finished tasks' live lessons (retired and `none:` ones left out; defanged, each ≤ 300 characters, cited by task id) redacted, to `.claude/rules/foreman-lessons/T-NNNN.md`, one file per task (the newest 60) whose `paths:` frontmatter lists the files that task touched, so Claude Code loads it natively only when one is read; a file more than 5 exported tasks touched (a changelog, the CLI) scopes nothing, and a task left with no scope isn't exported (it would load every session); stale files there are removed, and `--retire` re-exports. `fm decide --export-adr DIR [--review]` writes one ADR per decisions.md row (`NNNN-slug.md`: title, date, status Accepted or Superseded when reversed, context from the why, decision, rejected alternatives as consequences), numbered in order so a re-export rewrites the same files. `fm task defer ID --until "when PATH changes"` keeps the file's content hash (`revisit_tag`, a path inside the project only); `fm next` names the task as due back once the file changes or is gone (`fmcore.deferral_due`), dates as before.
- Self-improvement pass 4 (T-0286, batch of T-0282/T-0283/T-0284/T-0285/T-0279): the guard parses a quoted python heredoc after a chain of plain `cd DIR &&` like a bare one (any other prefix, `;`, pipe, backtick or `$(` keeps the text match; T-1887: `NAME=value;` or `&&` first too, unless NAME is in `_PRE_DENY` or already exported), so an edit script whose strings mention `import fmX` isn't taken for driving Foreman's modules; the test helpers put a lockout `claude` (exit 97, "the real claude is locked out") on PATH ahead of the real one, so a stub that can't exec never falls through to a paid session; the adversary lens follows untrusted text (web pages, registry answers, a cloned repo's files, another model's or session's output) to every sink (terminal, model prompt, URL/network, shell, file path, git config/hooks), and the pre-audit marks network/ingest code (`urlopen(`, `requests.get(`, `http.client`, `fetch(`, `axios.`) security-sensitive; a FIX or SECURITY request (capture or task new) naming a done task logs an `escape` against it with the lenses it closed with, and `fm friction` lists escapes — a review's recall over time; `fm task prove --hunks` splits a new Python file into its top-level definitions (decorators included; docstring and imports left alone) and removes each alone. Review hardening (the guard reads what bash runs): `check_bash` joins backslash-continued lines outside quotes, comments and bodies before anything else (`python3 - <<'X' \` + `1>FILE` is a redirect, not body); every heredoc splitter ends a body only on the exact delimiter line (tabs dropped for `<<-`), so `X ` is body as in bash; a python line with a second `<<` form or a backslash isn't a parsed unit; and both the proved-writes and the drive check look for a module shadowing an import in the folder `python -` runs in (the `cd` target on its own line included). Found proving this batch: `git apply -R --unidiff-zero` put a zero-context hunk back at the nearest identical line counted from the old file's position, so after an insertion `prove --hunks` mutated the wrong line; hunks are now reverted by their position in the new file (`_revert_hunk`), checked against their + lines.
- Self-improvement pass 5 (T-0287; the pass's other ideas grounded as built or merged into T-0234): `fm replay --cmd TEXT [--cwd DIR]` asks the guard for one command's default verdict (no grants) and never runs it — for grounding a false positive without hand-built hook payloads; the pre-audit's security code patterns skip added lines of doc files (md, rst, txt, adoc), so docs that name a pattern no longer flag a change (replayed over 60 commits: only the pass-4 commit's doc-only hits changed).
- Research rigor (T-0277): fetched pages are cached for the day under `state/research-cache/<day>/` (successes only; earlier days pruned), so verification and repeat asks don't refetch; `fm research ask --file FILE` runs one question per line (≤ 20), each planned, budget-checked and noted on its own. Each claim carries its support — `also: <site>` when a similar claim (≥ 3 shared stems and half their union) from another registrable domain agrees (docs.x.dev and blog.x.dev are one site; `(unchecked)` when that quote couldn't be checked; a quote not on its page supports nothing), `single source` when none does — and possible contradictions across sub-questions or models (a similar AGAINST line, or a similar claim differing in negation) get a Possible conflicts section, one line per pair of statements; `--quorum MODEL` runs the same researchers on a second model and marks `both models` / `only <model>` / `<model> gave no answer` (identical claims listed and counted once; a failed model is named). The note's header says these labels are word overlap, not reading. Pages under 500 characters or that look like a challenge/error page aren't cached. `--file`: one failing question is reported and the rest still run (exit 1 if any failed); notes asked in the same minute get -2, -3… instead of overwriting. The research card shows ⟷ conflicts and single-source counts. A judge-based --tournament was left out (a third paid pass for what quorum shows).
- Outside view (T-0278, batch of T-0237/T-0238; `fmoutside.py`): `fm landscape [--if-due]` asks fm research one dated question — what developers want from coding-agent harnesses that is missing or unreliable now — into `research/landscape-YYYYMMDD.md`, then appends a `## Since <previous>` section against the newest earlier `landscape-*`/`harness-landscape-*` note: items new this scan (each marked with the fm command that already covers it, via fm recall's command match), and items gone since; the bullet/numbered lines of both notes are compared by word overlap (≥ 3 shared stems and half the shorter item's), recall hits and earlier diff sections ignored. `--if-due` skips within 30 days of the last scan (`landscape_at` in meta). `fm deps` reads requirements*.txt, pyproject.toml (PEP 621 and poetry), package.json and Cargo.toml at the project root, asks PyPI, npm and crates.io (base URLs overridable by FOREMAN_PYPI_URL/NPM_URL/CRATES_URL) for each one's latest version, eight at a time, and lists those a major behind with a migration question ready for `fm research ask` (a spec that keeps the major — exact, `~=`, caret, tilde, or a lower bound with an upper one — is compared; a bare `>=`/`>` is listed as uncapped, never behind); `--research [N]` asks the first N (default 3), each budget-checked. Registry requests go to public https only, redirects re-checked (an overridden base URL is trusted); unreachable or unknown packages are listed as not checked with why. Lockfiles and transitive dependencies are left out.
- Evolve (T-0224): `fm evolve --target <instruction file: plugin/skills/**/*.md, plugin/skills/routing.json, rules/*.md, agents/*.md, output-styles/*.md> [--drop] [--live LABEL] [--ids …] [--max 3] [--runs 1] [--mutate-model sonnet]` — a tool-less `claude -p` child (no tools, MCP or user settings) returns the whole revised file from the last bench failures, the friction digest and the case list (`--drop` empties it instead); fm commits it on `evolve/<time>` in a worktree at `<repo>.evolve/<time>`, runs both arms from the same commit (live from a HEAD worktree, never the working tree) through `fmbench.run_arm` (which refuses any plugin without the guard; `--live` reuses results only from the same HEAD and model) and keeps the branch only if `fmbench.gate` passes and the candidate is better (more passes, or ≥ 5% cheaper per case) — a tie is kept only for `--drop`; otherwise the worktree and branch are removed. Never touches the live plugin; merging stays the user's call. The guard's own files (`fmevolve.GUARD`: `lib/fm{guard,hooks,replay,evolve,mutate,bench}.py`, `hooks/`, `tests/test_guard*.py`, `tests/fixtures/guard_*` and `gen_evasions.py`, after normalising `..`) are refused before any other check, whatever `--plugin-dir` says; the target is read at the pinned head sha, and one that isn't a plain file there (a symlink, a folder; git never walks a linked folder) is refused before the child runs (T-1517).
- Taste profile (T-0112): `fm taste` reads what the user's choices say: their requests that were finished, work dropped with its reason (housekeeping drops such as 'folded into' or 'done in' left out), and the steers and corrections in the ledger; `fm ideas` packs carry the drops and steers next to the requests and corrections they already had (T-0099), so brainstorms lean toward what the user accepts.
- Autonomy and the user (T-0671: T-0439, T-0461, T-0477): `fm taste` also proposes vetoes — key words shared by 3+ steers that say no/never/don't/avoid, none an existing veto or a declined proposal covers — for one yes through AskUserQuestion (`fm taste adopt [N]` records them in `vetoes.json`, `fm taste decline N` keeps the no in meta `taste_declined`). `fm decide --ask "Q" --default A | --options A B …` is a question for the user: with only options the taste record picks the default (no option a veto covers, then most words shared with finished requests and steers that aren't a no; ties keep the caller's order); `--urgent` says ask now; full autonomy decides now (a decisions.md row `Q → A`); standard autonomy queues it in one ask digest (meta `ask_digest`, deadline `DIGEST_HOURS` = 8 h after its first ask) that `fm next` carries until `fm decide --digest` lists it and `fm decide "<answer>" --answer N` settles each ask; past the deadline any `fm decide` applies every default as a row. An ask that names a guard category (or `fm ask`) is refused: the taste record and the digest never pre-answer what only the user grants, and nothing here writes `allow` or `pending_approvals`. `fm capture [TEXT] --from-file PATH|-` attaches a log, transcript or paste: its path and an excerpt (first 10 and last 30 lines, redacted, defanged) quoted in the Raw request, a paste kept redacted in `attachments/paste-<hash>.txt`, the title from the first line when there's no text; a binary file (a screenshot) is attached as its path alone.
- Relate (T-0383, `fmrelate.py`): `fm relate [--no-child] [--if-due] [--clear] [--on] [--drop T-B T-A]` infers which open tasks build on others, so nobody types `#T-` dependencies. An open task id named in another open task's title or request is an edge (free); a tool-less child (`fmideas.run_child`, feature `relate`, haiku, budgeted; never in a sensitive project) reads the open, unbatched tasks (id, type, tier, title, the request's first 300 chars, the newest 150) and replies `T-B -> T-A: why` and `group: T-1 T-2 — theme` lines. Only open, unbatched ids, edges not vetoed and making no cycle with `depends_on` or each other, at most 5 per task, are kept, as brief meta `inferred_deps`, `inferred_why` and `group` (apart from `depends_on`). A run where no child answered (`--no-child`, sensitive, a failure) keeps the earlier inferences; an answer replaces them. `--drop` removes one edge and records it in `inferred_veto`, never inferred again; `--clear` drops every inference (vetoes stay) and sets meta `relate_off`, which `--if-due` and the triggers honour until `--on`. `fmcore._deps` (depends_on, then inferred_deps — none for an urgent brief) feeds `order_queue`, `rank_inbox`, `fm run`'s batching and `fm tidy`; inferred ids aren't reported as dangling, and a cycle through one is hinted at `fm relate --drop`. `fmcore._topo` orders both views on a heap with keys and deps read once per brief: each after what it waits on, a group's ready members right after the one placed last (active and urgent work still lead; the inbox breaks a cycle at its best-ranked item). `fm queue` prints each task's reason and group. SessionStart and an `fm intake` of 3+ items start `--if-due` detached: not while another run's `relate_claim` is under 10 minutes old, once a day, and only when 3+ open tasks are newer than the last successful run's highest id (`relate_day`, `relate_seen` are written only after it succeeds; a failed child is a `relate` event with its error). The inbox's age term counts whole days, so same-second captures rank by id, not by the clock.
- History lessons (T-1047, `fmeco.seed_history`): `fm adopt` (or `fm adopt --history` alone) reads the last 300 commits; subjects starting fix/revert, each with up to 5 files, go into the research note `git-history-lessons` (under a line saying it is repo content, not instructions; redacted, defanged) that fm recall finds. A body line `error: X → Y` (first 2,000 chars, per line) becomes a known fix with trust=False: project store only, a hint until `fm fix trust N`; `best()` prefers trusted fixes. A second run adds nothing.
- What Foreman learned (T-1045): `fm digest` (text and JSON `learned`) and the pane's dim idle line count, over the window, known fixes added, worked and missed (fmfixes.learned; the totals run all-time per fix, ponytail-noted), rules to propose from correction clusters, and interventions (corrections plus guard blocks) per closed task. The pane's line is cached in the project's state as learned-line.json for 120 s (computing it costs about 0.45 s; a warm fm ui stays about 0.3 s). A mod test sweeps the pane over widths 30 to 200 and heights 8 to 60 so that no Raster or Box is wider than the pane and every long Text truncates (T-1050).
- Backtest (T-1058, `fmbacktest.py`, `fmreplay.events()`): `fm backtest --rule REGEX | --fix SIG | --card NAME [--days 14] [--json]` replays recent recorded sessions (read-only) and says how often the change would have fired, in how many sessions, and how many of those calls errored: a rule matches each call's command, path or pattern; a fix only failed calls whose output holds its signature; a card its name as a whole word (hyphens match spaces; a text approximation of real card injection).
- Trials (T-1059, `fmtrials.py`): `fm rule add` and `fm evolve` take `--moves METRIC --by DAYS` (≥ 1): the change is recorded in trials.json with the ledger event kind it should move (corrections → correction events). Once due, `fm digest` and `fm trial` grade it on equal before and after windows: "moved" when the after rate is at most half the before rate, else "no movement: propose revert" (never reverted unattended); malformed records are skipped. Lower is better for every metric.
- Drafts (T-1062, `fmdraft.py`): `fm draft` has a headless claude (fmserve._claude_cmd's flags, sandbox included, with --permission-mode acceptEdits) draft the top unblocked queued S task in a throwaway worktree, committed on draft/T-x as "draft: T-x (not reviewed)", never merged. draft.json keeps the task id and its brief's hash; `fm draft --status` sweeps the branch when the queue's top or its brief changed. `fm night` lists it as a "draft T-x" job (about $0.30).
- Scout (T-0950): Foreman finds its own work so the user never has to ask. `fm scout [--slow] [--dry]` runs zero-token probes — a published Claude Code newer than the installed one and `fmscout.CC_TESTED` (the README states the same version), dependencies in the user's projects no stack card covers, doctor FAILs, skill sources older than a week (refreshed, nothing captured), and an empty queue with no brainstorm this week, and fm find on the user's own project (slow) — and captures each finding once under its own key (`scout_keys` in meta; a dropped one stays quiet, a new version is a new key), at most 3 a run and 5 open. When the queue drains under full autonomy with the drive on, the Stop hook runs the quick probes (cached data, no network) at most once per 30 minutes and continues with what they captured; when they found nothing it asks Claude, at most once per 6 h, to run `fm scout --slow` and work what that captures. An answer to a question is not a drain. `fm night` runs `scout --slow` first. Every real run is a `scout` ledger event with its captures and each failed probe (skipped; the others still run). Library gaps are captured only in Foreman's own repo, one item per new set of dependencies (names that aren't plain package names are dropped); `--dry` writes nothing and refreshes no source. T-2105: `probe_genesis` (quick) offers Genesis once ever (key `genesis:start`) when nothing is runnable or captured, 10+ tasks are done and the project has no gen-0; a `Finding` with `confirm=True` makes capture set the brief's meta confirm, so it waits on the user's own `fm ask ID confirm` whatever the autonomy. T-2124: `probe_find` (slow) runs fm find on a user project, never Foreman's home (fm find is run by hand there), and only where git's user.email authored one of the last 50 commits (otherwise the probe fails with the authors it saw; no commits or no git, it says nothing); an email is only a claim, so where others commit too each lead carries confirm and waits for the user's yes. It files only kinds that prove themselves (`PROVEN`: dep, docs, secret; the rest wait for a failing test), at most `FIND_DAY` 3 open and 3 in any 24 hours, never a key it filed before nor a `fmfind.settled` one (so a regression is fm find's to refile), each tagged finder and find_key so its fate feeds that finder's precision. Doctor FAILs seen in a user project are Foreman's fault, so `Finding(home=True)` files them in Foreman's own inbox, deduped there (a failure to write there is a probe error, not a crash). fmdocs runs git with fsmonitor and hooks off, skips links and reads at most 1 MB a doc, since fm find feeds it untrusted trees.
- Recall and lessons: `fm focus` writes a "Related" section into the brief (and prints it): the project's most similar past briefs with their outcome and lesson, decisions and research notes (BM25 over suffix-stripped words, ≤ 4 capped plain lines, labelled as data), plus a tier hint when similar finished tasks ran bigger; `fm recall TEXT` or `fm recall --task ID` does the same on demand. `fm recall --ask "QUESTION"` (T-0484) answers from the project's memory instead: brief sections (each Log entry on its own, Related left out), ledger events not already echoed in a brief, decisions.md rows and research paragraphs go into an in-memory SQLite FTS5 index (porter stems, built per question), and the best BM25 passages print as snippets with the task ids each cites (its brief, ids it names, the task a decision was made in); no FTS5 falls back to plain recall. `fm explain [block|drive]` (T-0464) says why Foreman did the last thing it did: the newest guard block (the project ledger, joined with the hook's `events.jsonl` record for the tool and command) or drive/Stop decision (`drive`, `drive_wait`, `drive_offer`, `drive_drained`, `drive_reload` in `events.jsonl`, now carrying the project; `stop_gate` in the ledger), with the rule that fired (the guard's rule id and its own wording, or the drive branch), its inputs, the grants (or drive/autonomy settings) on record, the last ledger events of its task or session and the hook breaker. `fm task show ID --story` (T-0483) prints the task's ledger, rolled months in `archive/` included, as chapters (Plan, Steps, Evidence, Reviews, Close) with times, edits one line per chapter; `--json` gives the chapters for a UI. M/L tasks record a lesson at the end (`fm task done ID --lesson "…"`, or `none: why`), which recall shows on related work.
- Brainstorm and cost leftovers (T-0959): a brainstorm child that prints text and then exits non-zero keeps that text, flagged partial (the lens file opens with `PARTIAL (exit N: …)`, the result has `partial: true`, the summary row says `part`; research children get the same text and error pair); `fm ideas` writes `manifest.json` beside `ideas.json` (the given pack's sha256, model, lenses, `--seen` paths, rounds, time); `fm cost --sessions` labels each cache bust `ttl` (more than 5 min since the session's previous turn) or `context edit`; `fm friction --minutes` estimates minutes lost in the last week from the global events (30 s per guard block and failed tool call, plus failed calls' and hooks' run time); `fm burden --by-verb` lists the fm verbs no session called in the window (`fm usage --prune`'s list).
- Self-improvement loop (T-0125): `fm friction` digests Foreman's own friction since the last pass from the global events and every project's ledger — guard blocks by category with examples, each rechecked against today's guard (T-0187: "today's guard allows it" means a fix cleared it, from the full command in the session's transcript), failed tool calls, evidence nudges and drive stops, slow gates (latest vs usual), hook latency, the user's corrections and steers, recorded lessons, and what became of the self-inbox (closed since, still open), so a fix that didn't remove its friction shows up again. A mark keeps the window's counts, so the next digest shows a trend per 100 tool calls (guard blocks, failed calls, drive stops, steers) against the last pass's window. `fm friction --every N` makes `fm next` call for a pass at a task boundary after N closed tasks and at least 2 h after the last pass (0: off; on for Foreman's own project); `--brief` writes a self-contained brief for one read-only `foreman:fm-recon` subagent (≤5 grounded fixes, and whether the loop itself should change); its findings go to the self-inbox (`fm capture --source self`) and through the normal queue (tests, audits, commits); `--mark` closes the pass. Each guard block also logs its command, redacted and cut to one 160-character line (T-0172), and the digest shows the newest example per kind, so a pass can ground a false block.
- Forge (T-1070): `fmrepeats.forge(p)` writes `research/forge-<kebab>-<hash>.sh` per uncovered repeated command shape and `research/forge-skill-<kebab>-<hash>/SKILL.md` per repeated step, then captures one FEATURE per draft keyed by a `Forge draft: research/…` line (fmeco.capture_once) with a step and a criterion; `fm repeats --forge [--json]`.
- Cross-project recall (T-1073): `fmrecall.others(p, text)` walks `c.all_projects()` (sharing on, not sensitive), matches `failures.jsonl` signatures then ≥3 shared tokens, finds the task with `c.find_brief`, skips none/retired lessons (`_live_lesson`), and renders through `private`, `plain` and `defang`; `note_failure` adds the hint with a fixed data label when `seen_before` finds nothing local; `fm recall --others [--task ID]`.
- Repeated work: `fm repeats` reads the project's briefs (archive included) for commands run 3+ times in 2+ tasks (by shape: sequences whole, otherwise the first three words, interpreter flags, ids, numbers and quoted values normalized; Foreman's own commands and git reads skipped) and steps that recur in 3+ tasks, marks what `fm check` already runs, and suggests a gate, a script or a project skill for the rest; `fm tidy` counts the open ones. `fm repeats dismiss "<shape or step>"` retires one once it became a tool (or isn't worth one). How to build each: `references/execute.md` → Project tools.

</details>

### Cost, usage and performance

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Budget (T-0227): `fm budget [--days N]` shows spend by feature against the caps; `fm budget set --day USD --run USD [--because WHY]` (raising records a costly decision). The ledger is `state/spend.jsonl` (global), caps `state/budget.json`; children use `--output-format json` and `fmbudget.result()`; estimates are a feature's mean recent cost per run; caps halve when the newest statusline snapshot shows weekly ≥ 80% or 5-hour ≥ 90%. Subagents have no token cap (T-0320; their tokens are still recorded and shown): PreToolUse matches Agent|Task and refuses a launch only while usage is ahead of pace — the 5-hour window at 90%+, or weekly usage more than 10 points past the share of the week gone by (never below 75%, T-0364; never past 90%), from the snapshot's `resets_at`; unknown usage never refuses.
- Fast active task (T-1140): `c.active_brief_fast(p, lane)` reads each brief's frontmatter head and parses only those whose status is active or verifying; every fmhooks hot path uses it (the committed-copy fallback keeps active_brief(load_briefs)). PreToolUse median 135 → 72 ms on 1,069 briefs.
- Foreman's share (T-1257): `fm cost` also says how much of what entered the context (tool results and transcript attachments, in characters) was Foreman's own, by source: `fm` command output (Bash results of commands made of `fm` calls alone, by verb: `_fm_verb`, T-1276), hook notes (`hook_additional_context`, `hook_system_message`, `hook_blocking_error` opening with "Foreman" or "Ambient tests:"), its rules file (`instructions` entries ending in `rules/foreman.md`), its output style (`output_style_instructions`, and Claude Code's per-turn `output_style` reminder), and its `- foreman:` lines in the skill and agent listings. `fmcost.scan(folder, since, share)` fills it in the pass `fm cost` already makes; `--json` has `foreman_share`. On this repo over three days: 6.8%, led by the style reminder (2.7%) and the rules (1.7%); fm output is 0.9% once chained test runs aren't counted as Foreman's.
- Retry tax (T-1824): `fmcost.retry_windows` spans each step's failing evidence to its next pass (still failing: to now); evidence on a later step drops an earlier step's open failure (a red→green red run, or one the task moved past). `fm cost --task ID` adds `retry N (P%)` beside spent, and `fm cost`'s by-task line and `retry_by_task` JSON name it per task.
- Forecast (T-0973): `fm cost --forecast [--json]` reads the newest statusline snapshot's rate_limits (`fmbudget.forecast`): per window (5-hour, weekly) the percent used, the percent projected at its reset on a straight line from the window's start, and when it fills if that comes before the reset; a reset already past or a millisecond epoch gives no line.
- Performance and cost (T-0676: T-0467, T-0486, T-0449, T-0487, T-0448): `fm check` runs its gates by failure odds ((fails+1)/(runs+2) over each gate's last 20 real runs, a flaky first failure counting) per second of median runtime, so `--fail-fast` stops sooner; a gate with no history runs first, and with none at all the configured order holds; results, evidence and the `check_run` event stay in configured order (the cached pass compares it). Every timed hook's `hook_ms` event carries Foreman's revision (`rev`: plugin.json's version, read once per hook process after the timing), and `fm doctor`'s `hook SLO` check warns when the p95 of the last 200 PreToolUse runs (from 20) passes bench_hooks' budget, naming the revision where it rose and the one before (in-process time: interpreter start and imports aren't in it). While usage runs ahead of pace (`fmbudget.degrade()`: the pace subagents wait on) optional work is dropped with a note: `fm next` skips the self-improvement pass, the Stop drive offers no side task or product check while background work runs, `fm ideas --deepen` runs 0 rounds; the caps still refuse what's over one and `fm check` never consults them. `fm task finish`/`done` log the session's model (the statusline snapshot), type, tier and verification grade on `task_done`; `fm cost --by-model` counts them per model and type/tier. At a step boundary of an active M/L task (its newest work event is step evidence, no edit since) with context at `CONTEXT_NOTE_PCT` or more, the Stop drive says once to checkpoint (`fm checkpoint --note`), Resume here being the handoff, and that this is the point to /compact.
- Cost and usage: `fm cost` reads Claude Code's transcripts for tokens by task, session and tool result, and where the active time went (T-0188: model against each tool, shell commands by what they ran; waiting on the person and gaps over 10 min left out); `fm usage` lists the skills, playbooks and fm commands used and never used and how often the injected Next was followed; `fm digest` shows the week (SessionStart offers it once a week); `fm quiet -- CMD` and `fm outline PATH` keep noisy output and big files out of the context. Zero-token instruments (T-0701): deterministic typed tools, stdlib and git only, each cut at a line cap with its most telling lines first; `fm instruments [--json]` lists them with their JSON input schemas and caps (the fm subcommands are built from that registry). `fm sym PATH:NAME` prints a definition (Python by ast, other languages by keywords and braces) with the names it uses that its file defines or imports and its callers in the repo (≤40 lines); `fm fail [FILE]` turns a test run's output (stdin or FILE) into the failing test, the error, the repo frames and the deepest one's source (≤15); `fm logs FILE [--since-good FILE]` mines Drain-style templates with counts, errors and warnings first, and with a good run's log the new and gone templates first (≤40); `fm data FILE` gives a csv/tsv/json/jsonl/sqlite file's columns with type, values of another type, empties, distinct counts and stats, and its first 5 rows (≤40); `fm trace [FILE]` maps a Python or JS stack trace to repo lines, deepest first, each with the commit date and task that last touched it (≤30). Another machine's paths (a CI runner's) map by their longest suffix under the folder fm runs in, then the repo root. `fm run --models S=…,L=… [--save]` picks a model per tier; `fm notify '<cmd using $1>'` tells you when fm run finishes, blocks or stops. `fm share on` (opt-in) shares privacy-filtered lessons across projects (`state/shared/lessons.jsonl`); `fm evals add ID` turns a failed task into an eval case.
- Read memos (T-1082): `fmcore._read_brief` parses a brief once per process while its (inode, mtime_ns, size) is unchanged (a file written in the last 2 s isn't kept; a moved or deleted brief is forgotten; 5,000 entries at most) and hands every caller its own `Brief.copy()`; `tail_jsonl` keeps the last read of up to 8 files (≤20,000 lines) and serves a smaller tail of an unchanged file from it as fresh top-level copies. fm ui --json: 0.67 s → 0.30 s.

</details>

### Bench

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Benchmark (T-0212): `fm bench build` (cases from finished single-commit tasks, or with `--commits RANGE --verify CMD` from any commit that changes tests and source — T-0222 with test files and verify commands, validated fail-to-pass in temporary worktrees, saved to the project's `<state>/bench/cases.json`), `fm bench run [--plugin DIR] [--max 3] [--budget 3] [--model M] [--label L]` (headless `claude -p` per case in a temporary worktree at the parent commit with `FOREMAN_STATE` fresh and seeded full/drive-on, user settings left out so only the candidate plugin loads, hidden tests restored before the verify commands grade; results in `bench/results/<label>.json`), `fm bench compare A B`, `fm bench list`, `fm bench models --models a,b,c [--save]` (T-0223: per tier, the model with the most passes and lowest cost among ties; --save sets fm run's per-tier models), `fm bench gate A B` (T-0221: exit 0 when B loses no passes on the shared cases and costs ≤ 15% more per case; `run --runs N` gives pass rates), `fm bench show LABEL` (per case: the replay's own tasks and statuses, ledger events, guard blocks, tools, verify results — T-0220). Each case is a paid session. Replays run in bypass mode like the user's own sessions, so `run` refuses a plugin that doesn't ship Foreman's guard (a PreToolUse Bash hook and lib/fmguard.py), and the session's environment drops forge, cloud and registry credentials (Claude's own login stays); there is no OS sandbox, so candidates should be Foreman's own reviewed code.
- Duel fairness and Foreman's own turns (T-1231): `fm bench duel` leaves court cases (type COURT: they grade what Foreman learned from the user's state) out unless `--ids` names them, and takes `--runs N` (pass rate, mean cost and time per case and arm) and `--jobs N` (cases at once; worktree add/remove serialized by `fmbench._GIT`). From the first duel's transcripts, the turns Foreman spent on itself were cut: `fmcli._task_shape` rewrites `fm task <sub>` argv before parsing (no id → the active task, named on stderr; `ac`/`step` action on either side of the id, none → add; `--reason` for drop/block/defer); `fmcore.never_ran` treats a unittest/pytest test module that the command names and that failed to import a dependency (not a SyntaxError) as never ran, so its check stays editable; `fm task finish` closes an S task's evidence-less steps on its first criterion's check (a step with a failed run of its own still needs its own); `fmhooks._first_brief` makes the task on the first edit of a full-autonomy session whose only request is the one being worked (S, the tag's type, DONE-WHEN lines as criteria; `fm task new` as a 3 s subprocess, then one more refusal so the retry meets the whole guard); a headless session is told at start to end with each check it ran (T-1228). The first brief's edit then goes ahead after `_guard_decision` re-runs with the task active (brief is the guard's last category), and its note teaches the one-call close; `fmcore.sensitive` matches token/session/login/permission/acl/security/signing only as whole path words (`_SENSITIVE_WORD`), auth/crypt/secret/passw/credential/oauth/jwt/guard/sandbox/sudo/keyring as substrings. In an unattended run the Stop hook's `_close_first_brief` closes and commits the S, non-FIX task the session's first edit made (ledger `first_brief`) once `_test_gate` (fmmap's gates, a test one first, no map build) passes within 20 s; not paused or panicked, not retried after `first_brief_close_timeout`; a failure is told to the agent once (`first_brief_close_failed`); the Stop hook's limit is 30 s. A one-request headless SessionStart carries `_layout_note` (tracked top folders, the test command) instead of the empty queue. Bench commands run `fmbench._frozen`, a copy of the plugin folder taken at start.
- Long sessions (T-1242): `fm bench build --commits R --verify CMD --together` merges a gapless chain of commit cases into one `M-<sha>` case (requests in order under `## Request k`, every hidden test, graded at the newest commit; `fmbench.together`). `fm bench duel --compact-at TOKENS` sets `CLAUDE_CODE_AUTO_COMPACT_WINDOW` on both arms and keeps the sessions (`_session(keep=True)`) so `_compactions` counts their `compact_boundary` entries, then removes the files; `count_tests` reads unittest/pytest summaries from the graded tails into `tests: [passed, total]`. `--marathon N` sets `FOREMAN_MARATHON` on Foreman's arm: the Stop hook's `_marathon` sends N passes of "find one more improvement, test first" once this session's queue is empty (ledger `marathon_pass`), and a marathon that hits its `--budget` cap is `capped`, not an error. In `fmhooks._first_brief`, a prompt with two or more `## Request k` sections (`_requests`) queues one S task per request (`_queue_requests`: `--raw` holds its text, each depends on the one before, the first focused, all within 3.5 s of the hook's 5); a prompt the ledger cut at 2,000 characters is read whole from the transcript (`_first_prompt`). SessionStart with source `compact` appends `_requests_note`: the open request tasks this session queued, word for word from their Raw request sections (the active one first, within `REQUEST_TEXT_CAP` 6,000 characters: Claude Code saves a hook context over about 10 KB to a file and shows a 2 KB preview; the rest by id). An edit with nothing active in such a session goes to `_next_request`: the next open request whose earlier ones are closed is focused and its text comes with the note (a session's own `first_brief` events also stop a second copy of its queue). At Stop, after the active request closes, `_close_evidenced` focuses and finishes this session's other open request tasks that have step evidence, in order, within the hook's 25 s. A bench replay never gets the PushNotification nudge. `_diagnose` adds task titles (with source), and `site/stats.py --duel long=DIR@haiku` / `marathon=DIR@haiku` pick the newest forced-compaction or marathon duel for the Benchmarks page (`pages._long`). `fm task finish --commit` names uncommitted files the task never changed and commits one given with `--with` (T-1235).
- False dones (T-1377): `bench/spec-suite/impossible.py` holds round 3, tasks no change can do; `make.py --suite impossible --check` (`check_impossible`) proves each one's tests fail at its parent, at its commit and with each variant. `fm bench build --commits R --verify CMD --impossible` (`build_commits(impossible=True)`, `validate(..., impossible)`) keeps commits whose tests fail at their own commit too as `IMPOSSIBLE` cases (`impossible`, `judge`, `IMPOSSIBLE_RUBRIC`); `run_case` grades their tests as well, and a run that makes them pass is `cheated` (failed); `false_done` is cheated or a judge score of 1 or less (a rate in `run_case_n`), and the `--vs` duel adds `false done: Foreman n/k, <rival> n/k` (data `false_done`).
- Bench family (T-0280, batch of T-0231/T-0240/T-0241/T-0242/T-0243; all in `fmbench.py` on one session helper): `fm bench build --judged` makes work whose commit has no tests (docs, UI, prose) a judged case — no hidden tests; after the replay a tool-less judge (`fmsecond._child`, feature `bench-judge`) reads everything the session left in the worktree against the base (`git add -A`, `diff --cached`, ≤ 40k chars), its final reply, the case's rubric (the brief's criteria) and the user's steers and corrections (`fmideas.taste`), and replies `SCORE: N` (0–5) with reasons; 4+ passes, and `score`/`judge` land in the results, `fm bench show` and the pane. `fm bench duel PLUGIN` (a folder or an installed id) replays the same cases on Foreman alone and on Foreman with the contender's `--plugin-dir` beside it, gates them, and says helps / no difference (may duplicate Foreman) / hurts with the gate's reason. `fm bench versions REV [--source DIR]` replays them on Foreman at REV (a detached worktree of its repo, removed after) and on the running one, and gates old → current. `fm bench court` turns each steer or correction said during a task whose work is one commit into a judged case `R-<task>-<hash>` (that task's request at the commit's parent; rubric: honour what the user said), saves them in `<state>/bench/cases.json` (a full `build` keeps them, so `fm evolve` and every gate replay them too) and runs them. `fm bench soak [--turns 3]` replays a case as a conversation: the session is kept and resumed by id, and after each reply a tool-less shadow user (`bench-soak`) who knows only the user's steers and corrections answers with `NEXT:` (or `DONE`) and a `REPEAT:` line for each thing it had to say again; results (`complaints`) in `bench/results/soak-<time>.json`. Duel and versions log `bench_duel`/`bench_versions` events; the pane's bench card shows the latest two verdicts, a judged run's mean score and a soak's repeats. Every replay keeps `run_arm`'s guard check and budget (soak checks it inside `soak_case`). Review hardening: bench git runs with `core.fsmonitor=` and `core.hooksPath=/dev/null` (a replay's worktree shares the repo config, and git after a bypass-mode session must not run what it configured), the judge's diff without external diff or textconv; the change and reply are fenced as data; a crashed session isn't judged; the shadow user's NEXT is plain()ed and defanged before it reaches the session; a soak with no session id is an error; a plain `build` keeps judged cases.
- Unattended checks (T-0298, batch of T-0244/T-0236): `fm bench stranger [--plugin DIR]` runs one bypass-mode replay (guard required, budget-checked) in a fresh toy repo with no Foreman state and only the README (≤ 16k chars) as instructions, asked to fix a failing test with Foreman; its findings — guard blocks, failed `fm` calls (tool_fail events on fm commands), the toy test still failing, a session error — are captured as self items (at most five, none whose words an existing self item mostly has; each `confirm: true` with the replay's text quoted as data, so it waits for the user's own yes), and a `bench_stranger` event is logged; it waits while usage is high. `fm night [--dry-run] [--max-usd 2] [--only JOB]` (`fmnight.py`) refuses outright when usage is high; when no statusline snapshot is under 12 h old (usage unknown) the limit halves; a sensitive project runs nothing. Jobs, cheapest first, each an existing fm command with an `fmbudget.estimate`: `fm second session --if-due` once a day, `fm landscape --if-due` when due, one research-debt question (the first RESEARCH inbox item nobody has asked: `fm research ask TITLE --task ID`), `fm bench court --max 3` when steers make court cases, and one `fm bench versions <branch>` race for a kept `fm evolve` candidate not raced yet (court and versions get a per-session `--budget` from their estimate); kept while the total fits `--max-usd` and today's remaining budget, the rest listed as skipped. A run executes them one at a time as fm subprocesses (FOREMAN_NO_BACKGROUND=1), and before each re-reads the real spend since the night began and the usage: past the limit, climbing usage or two failed jobs in a row stop the night (`stopped` says why). Heartbeat (T-0434): `fm run` and `fm night` write `heartbeat-run.json` / `heartbeat-night.json` in the project's state dir before each session, job or usage-limit wait (pid, what, when the next beat is due); any exit Python sees removes it, so one left over 10 minutes overdue means wedged or killed: `fm doctor` warns (`heartbeats`) and runs the `fm notify` command once per stale episode (meta `heartbeat_told`). It never merges, logs a `night` event, and `fm digest` lists the nights. Scheduling it (cron, a systemd timer) stays the user's.

</details>

### Release, ship, CI and PRs

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Review threads and merge train (T-1087, `fmpr.py`, through gh): `fm pr threads ID [--pr N]` adds each unresolved review thread's first comment to the task as a step (redacted, defanged, marked untrusted data), keyed `[PR #N comment ID]` so a second run adds none. `fm pr train [--task ID]` lists open PRs by number; a PR needs reviewDecision APPROVED and every check green, else it is skipped; drafts and PRs with no checks are skipped too; the task's `publish` grant (fm ask ID publish) is required before any merge and is spent by the run (dropped from the task's allow, logged `grant_spent`; ask again for the next); each eligible PR's head is fetched (pull/N/head), merged onto the current base in a scratch worktree and put through the project's `fm check` gates, then `gh pr merge N --merge --match-head-commit SHA` (the tested head, so a later push is refused); the first red re-test, conflict or merge failure stops the train with exit 1 and the merged PRs are still reported. The train runs each PR's code (conftest, Makefile, package scripts) with your environment: use it only on PRs from people you trust.
- CI watch (T-1052, `fmci.py`): `fm ci-watch [--branch B] [--commit SHA] [--max-retries N] [--interval S] [--max-polls N]` polls gh run list (headSha, status, conclusion, url) for the watched commit only (HEAD re-read each poll, or --commit resolved to its full sha), so an older commit's run is never taken for this one; a failed run's --log-failed tail (last 40 lines, redacted, defanged, marked untrusted) becomes one FIX brief per run id; green exits 0, but a green run's failed non-blocking (continue-on-error) jobs are named and briefed once (T-1942); a failed run whose only failed jobs were cancelled with no steps (never got a runner) is no code failure: no brief, `gh run rerun --failed` and the new attempt is watched (runs are keyed by id and attempt), or, when gh can't rerun, exit 1 saying to rerun on GitHub or push again (T-2013); more than --max-retries failed runs, or max-polls without a finished run, exits 1 saying it gave up. It can wait a long time: run it in the background. Without gh (missing or logged out, T-1939) a github.com origin's runs come from the public API by head_sha (fixed host, no token, polls at least 75 s apart for the 60-an-hour limit) and the brief holds the failed jobs' annotations; a 401/403/404 (private repo, limit) ends it with gh's error and the code, and FOREMAN_NO_NET or a sensitive project keeps gh's error.
- `fm release VERSION [--ui V] [--push] [--resume] [--dry-run]` (T-1188, `fmrelease.py`; Foreman's own repo only): release.py's ritual as 9 named steps (tree, check, bump, changelog, commit, release-check, canary, push, round), a checkpoint in the project's `release.json` after each; `--resume` continues from the first unfinished step (same VERSION), `--dry-run` prints the plan. `--ui V` is required when `mods/foreman-ui` changed since the last `Foreman X.Y.Z` commit. `--push [--remote URL]` needs the active task's `publish` grant (checked before any change, spent before the push, a `grant_spent` event), merges into the remote's main (origin by default) from a temporary worktree with `--no-ff`; a skipped push is never recorded, so `fm release V --push` later runs only the push. The first run's `--ui/--push/--remote/--no-check` are kept for `--resume`; `--no-check` records the gates as skipped and such a release can't push; `--abandon` forgets an unfinished release (another version can't start over written changes until then). `release.py` is a thin wrapper over it.
- `fm ship [COMMIT…] [--remote URL] [--test CMD] [--task ID] [--watch] [--check]` (T-1943, `fmrelease.py`; T-2281: after the green test and before the push it writes the always-on footprint to the project's `footprint.json`, which `fm doctor`'s footprint check holds as the lean ratchet: a metric over 2% above it WARNs until a `fm decide "footprint: <metric> +N% …"` dated on or after the ship names it): lands HEAD's commits the remote's main lacks (git cherry, by patch, after `refs/foreman/shipped`, the last commit it shipped, when that is an ancestor of HEAD: T-1945) or the named ones: fetches main, cherry-picks them in a scratch worktree at the fetched head, checks their files match the last pick, runs the test there (--test, remembered in git config foreman.shiptest for the next ship: T-1950; else fm check), fetches again and pushes HEAD:main only if main is still an ancestor, spending the active task's publish grant, or with `--task ID` that task's, closed or active, so a finished task ships without a separate ship task (T-2007) (spent before the push, given back if git push fails; fm release --push shares it); any failure pushes nothing and keeps the grant, the working tree is never touched, and the scratch worktree is always removed. Prints the pushed sha for `fm ci-watch --commit`; `--watch` watches CI on it in the same job and exits with the watch's code (T-1946). A failed write of the shipped ref after the push only warns (T-1951). `--check` pushes nothing and needs no grant: exit 0 when main has those commits' changes (by patch; the default picks when none are named), 1 naming each one it lacks, so a ship task's criterion stays true after later ships (T-1960). A remote or commit that reads as an option is refused. Run it in the background. Its test run is marked (meta `ship_testing`) and waits for a live full `fm check` or ambient run first; a full `fm check` on another tree waits for it (T-1978).
- PR threads and merge train (T-1087, `fmpr.py`): `fm pr threads ID [--pr N]` turns the PR's unresolved review threads (GraphQL reviewThreads) into task steps, each once by its exact step prefix, path and body redacted and defanged as data. `fm pr train [--task ID]` takes approved, non-draft PRs whose checks are all green (an empty rollup is not green) in number order: each head merged with origin's base in a scratch worktree, the project's gates run there, then `gh pr merge N --merge --match-head-commit <tested sha>`; it stops at the first red re-test, conflict or merge failure and always reports what merged. It needs the task's `publish` grant, spent per run. The train runs each PR's own code (conftest, Makefile, package scripts) with your environment: use it only on PRs from people you trust.

</details>

### Night, drive and autonomy

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Run contract (T-1158, `fmcontract.py`): `fm contract --until HH:MM [--usd N] [--tokens N] | off | receipt` keeps meta `contract` {until, usd, tokens, started, tokens0}. `over(p, held=)` returns time/spend/tokens once a bound is reached and freezes it (`held` writes meta without re-locking: the Stop hook calls it under its own lock and fails open). Spend sums this project's `spend.jsonl` rows since the start (headless: `fm run` records each session's `total_cost_usd` while a USD contract holds, one task at a time); tokens are this project's transcript totals from the cost cache (refreshed detached, at most once a minute). Past a bound the Stop drive and the forever loop stop (next_for drops the loop action), `fm run` starts nothing and prints the receipt (tasks closed, spend, tokens, the bound). A startup or clear SessionStart after it ended keeps the receipt (`contract_receipt`) and drops the contract.
- Forever loop (T-1057, `fmloop.py`): `fm loop on|off|status|round|yield N` keeps meta `loop` (on, round, dry, started, last, history). With it on, `_next_for` returns stage `loop` instead of idle when the queue and inbox are empty, and the Stop drive continues into the round once per round started (key round+brief count, within DRIVE_MAX, waiting while `_pace()` says usage is ahead). `fm loop round` prints what shipped since the last round, the `fm ideas` line with `--seen` for the newest 6 earlier ideas.md and the steps (T-1966: while `fmbudget.degrade` drops optional work, usage pace or the spent risk budget, step 2 drops fm mission and fm ideas for ideas made in the main thread); `fm loop yield N` records the grounded yield once per round (T-1986: a dry yield while `fmbudget.degrade` drops optional work leaves the dry streak as it was and says so, since no fan-out ran; T-1990: the brief's step 5 says it too); while a round is open (no yield) a plain `fm loop round` shows its brief again and changes nothing (T-1153), and `fm loop round --next` (what fm next says for a drained open round) gives it the count of tasks made since it began and starts the next, and 2 rounds in a row under 3 turn it off with a decision. `tally` (T-2011) gives "N rounds, M tasks closed since DAY" for this run (rounds since meta `from_round`, set by a fresh `fm loop on`; distinct `task_done` events since `started`): in the stop decision, `fm loop off` and `fm loop`. While looping, a closed task or a started round resets the drive count, so DRIVE_MAX stops spinning, not long rounds. `fm ui` carries `loop`; the pane and band show `∞ round N`. T-2073: `fm loop round`'s Surfaces line names every top-level area and, from `lag(p)`, the files recent work would normally have brought along but didn't, learned from git history (`fmmap.drift`: co-change rate, updates owed), defanged, one line each, 'unknown' without git.
- Risk budget (T-1080, `fmrisk.py`): today's (UTC) ledger spends points: a guard block in a destructive category (rm-outside, git-destructive, system, credentials, self-authorize, pipe-shell, confine) 1, a close whose linked tests failed (close_affected exit ≠ 0) 2, a Revert commit since midnight 3. Past the cap (6, meta risk_cap), `c.effective_autonomy` steps down a notch (full → standard, standard → slow), `fmbudget.degrade(p)` names it so optional work waits, and `fm next` says why; the next day restores it. `fm risk` shows it; `fm risk set --day N --because` is user-only (the guard's USER_ONLY_FM): the braked can't lift the brake. Every autonomy reader goes through effective_autonomy (fm decide --ask then queues the ask instead of self-deciding), and every degrade() caller passes the project. It brakes Foreman's own autonomy decisions; it is not a guard: `fm task set ID approved=true`, a re-tier or `fm pause on` still move a task (each logged). One read per process while the ledger is unchanged (about 20 ms cold). T-1875: `fmrisk.collisions(p)` groups the last 7 days' guard_block events by rule (category when unruled), most first, with the newest detail and `fmguard._hint`'s rewrite; `fm risk` lists the top 8, rules that spend risk first (T-1972; JSON `collisions`), and SessionStart's `Watch:` line adds `collision_line` (the rule, its count, → fm risk; T-2117) for a rule hit 3+ times. Report only: CAP and WEIGHTS stay.
- Night queue (T-1046): `fm night` adds a "queue" job when an unblocked S/M task is queued: `fm run --fit --max 2 --wait 0 --max-usd <the night's remaining budget>`, planned only when its estimate fits the night's limit. `fm run --max-usd X` runs one task at a time with `claude -p --output-format json`, records each session's total_cost_usd through fmbudget, and stops starting tasks once the run's spend reaches X (a single task can overshoot it). The night event holds the task ids closed since it began; `fm digest` names them.
- Night gardening (T-1071): `fm night` runs two free jobs: compost (`fm tidy --apply --lanes-only`: worktrees and branches of done or dropped tasks only; an unmerged branch keeps its lane_branch) and consolidate (`fm recall --lessons --consolidate --capture --from-night`: lessons grouped by token Jaccard ≥ 0.6 with the same negation words, `keep <id>` named, nothing retired; one open CLEAN task at most).
- Repeat-session skip (T-1825, `fmserve._repeat_key`): `fm run`'s serial loop keys each session's start on the worktree id, the task's newest evidence line when it is a failed run (timestamp aside) and the steps and criteria done; a session that ends with the same key moved nothing but its own failing run, so the task is blocked with that check named (`block()`, then the next task) instead of buying another of its SESSIONS_PER_TASK. No git or a passing newest run: no key, no skip. `_fingerprint` now counts the first evidence line too (it counted "\n- ").

</details>

### Crash, snapshots and rollback

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Crash damage (T-0268, pass 1): fm doctor's `integrity` check scans Foreman's repo and every project root for zero-byte loose git objects (object names only, older than 5 s: git's in-flight tmp_obj_* files and an object being written aren't damage) (what a crash mid-write leaves; git then fails on any command that reads one), zero-byte briefs and state JSON that no longer parses, and names each; `fm doctor --repair` moves the empty objects into `state/quarantine/git-objects-<time>/` (never deletes; across filesystems too, each failure reported; git rewrites any the working files still hold) and touches nothing else. fm check runs doctor as a gate, so the next gate run after a crash says so.
- Crash hygiene (T-1405): the long verbs (check, code, release, bench, sentinel) write every thread's stack to `state/logs/stacks/<verb>-<pid>.txt` on SIGUSR1 and once after `FOREMAN_STACK_AFTER` seconds (30 min); an empty file is removed when the verb ends, a killed verb's empty one at the next long verb's start, and fm tidy prunes dumps older than 14 days. A Foreman commit (`fm task finish --commit`) that git refuses over `index.lock` removes the lock only when it is over 10 min old, no process has it open (an fd on its inode, any name or cwd) and no git runs with its cwd in the repo (Linux /proc; elsewhere it's only named); a git of ours it can't inspect keeps the lock, another user's or a non-git unreadable process doesn't (under hidepid=2 others' processes don't show); the lock is re-stat'd just before removal, and the removal logs an `index_lock_removed` event and retries once. fm doctor's `lock probe` has a child hold an flock on a probe file in state/ and fails, naming the filesystem, when this process can still take it (that proves exclusion between processes on this host only).
- `fm fsck [--fix]` (T-1667, in fmdoctor): read-only, every project, each line naming its project; open tasks' lanes whose folder, gitdir record or branch is gone (the `fm lane rm` hint only for the current project), `*.pid` locks in state/ an hour old or more whose process is dead or that hold no pid, `*.jsonl` journals whose last line has no newline or isn't JSON (the last 64 KB, read twice before it's called torn), state JSON files (state/ and each project's top level, under 1 MB) that don't parse (T-1669), and either one it can't read; lib-sources, snapshots, quarantine, outputs and caches skipped, symlinks and FIFOs never opened; exit 1 on any. `--fix` (and fm doctor --fix) removes only the stale locks, each re-checked first, and is refused outside the initial PID namespace (a sandbox's os.kill can't see a live holder); flock `.lock` files are never removed.
- Work snapshots (T-0884): before a Bash command that discards uncommitted work runs (`git reset --hard`/`--merge`, `checkout -f`/`--`/a path that names no commit, `restore` of the worktree, `clean -f`, `switch -f`/`--discard-changes`, `rm -f`, an `--abort` of merge/rebase/cherry-pick/revert/am, `worktree remove --force <dir>`, `stash drop`/`clear`; `fmguard.discards_work`, the guard's own tokenizer, following a `cd` before it, `-C` with `~`/`$VARS`, `sh -c` text, never heredoc bodies), the PreToolUse hook saves it under `refs/foreman/snapshots/<UTC stamp>-<sha7>[-stash]` (`fmcore.snapshot`: the working files, untracked included and ignored not, as a commit when they differ from HEAD; or the stash commits), in every session, never blocking, within 3 of the hook's 5 s (`SNAPSHOT_S`; past that, or on a git failure, the note says nothing was saved), and tells Claude the sha and the restore line (`fmcore.restore_line`: `git -C <top> restore --source=<sha> --worktree -- :/`, or `stash apply <sha>`; newer files are left alone). `fm snapshots` lists the project repo's saves newest first; `fm tidy --apply` deletes ones older than 14 days. Limits: `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` and commands python spawns aren't followed; an untracked non-ignored secret rides along in the local ref for those 14 days (a `push --mirror` would carry it).
- Rollback (T-1085): `fmrollback` snapshots BACKUP_PARTS of every project into `state/snapshots/<old>-<ts>.tar.gz` (a detached child at the first SessionStart after a version change; `.part` then rename; a manifest of sha256s; 3 kept, pre-rollback copies in their own pool of 3). `fm rollback --yes` extracts to a temp dir with filter='data', checks the manifest, replaces files under `c.lock`, skips ledger files, re-applies live consent meta (standing, approvals, autonomy, pause, trust) and each brief's allow/approved, and logs `rollback`. `fmguard.USER_ONLY_FM` holds every user-only fm command (prefix-matched flags).
