- Small verbs (T-0960): `fm oops` names the newest work snapshot and its restore line; `fm oops --apply` restores it after snapshotting the files as they are. `fm round close` tags HEAD `round-N` (one past the highest) and lists the done tasks its commits name (subject or `Foreman-Task:` trailer, reverts skipped) since the previous round tag. `fm show X` routes: a task id → `fm task show`, an existing FILE[:LINE] → `fm why`, other words → `fm recall`. `fm release-check` runs `fm doctor` and `fm demo` on this checkout with a temporary HOME and `FOREMAN_STATE`, then the docs' runnable fences (T-1519, above; trusted like tests, not sandboxed), exit 0 when all pass (`fmdemo.cmd_release_check`). `fm sensitive suggest` prints the paths that suggest a sensitive repo (`.env*` but not `.example/.sample/.template`, `*.pem`, a `secret(s)`/`prod(uction)` path part; `fmcli.sensitive_signals`) and sets nothing. `fm outcomes --readme` prints "N of M closed tasks held; K ungraded" (ungraded: done without a recorded verification grade).

</details>

### Cards and library

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Card library growth (T-1037, `fmlib`): `fm lib gaps [--root DIR]... [--json]` lists dependencies no card covers across registered projects (or the given roots; a missing root is an error), most-used first; `fm lib draft NAME --area AREA` stages a skeleton in the state's lib-drafts; `fm lib admit PATH|NAME` reads the staged card once (no symlinks), runs the card lint and an untrusted-text check on those bytes (curl/wget piped to a shell or python, bash <(curl …), ignore-previous-instructions, --no-verify, disabling tests/checks/hooks/guards, rm -rf ~ or /; exempt only where a governing never/avoid/don't directly precedes the match, or inside an Avoid bullet's quoted example), and writes it with O_EXCL under the cards folder only (kebab-case area and name).
- Import-matched cards (T-1036): `fm map` keeps every detected card's `dep:` signals (and up to 40 dependency cards past the top 8); `fmlib.cards_for` reads the edited file's first 64 KB plus the edit's new text (`imports_of`: Python modules with a distribution-name alias table, JS/TS packages incl. scoped ones, Rust crates, Go import paths; relative imports and std left out), returns the import-matched cards (score, then name) then `card_for`'s pick, at most two, each injected once per task; `fm audit prep` appends the Review checklists of the cards a task's edits used (`fmlib.card_review`); `fm lib stats` counts `card_injected` events per card.
- Edit-time stack cards (T-0949): the PreToolUse hook, on a Write/Edit/MultiEdit/NotebookEdit under an active task, picks the map's best card for the file from `map.json` alone (`lib_cards`: each suggested card's area, score and file/ext signals; `fmlib.card_for`): a card whose file glob or extension matches, a framework card before a language card, then the higher score, then the name. When this task hasn't had that card yet (an `enforce` ledger event of kind `card_injected`, written before the note goes out, so a lost output never repeats it), it adds the card's Avoid section, cut at a whole bullet to at most 700 chars with '…'. No Avoid section, no such card or a malformed map adds nothing, and a failure never blocks the edit. Off with `FOREMAN_NO_CARDS=1` or `fm lib inject off` (project meta `lib_inject`). `fm doctor -v` shows the enforcement line: 7-day counts of validator blocks, merge refusals (`fm lane merge`'s red or unrunnable criteria, also `enforce` events), waived merges (`lane_unverified`) and injected cards. Review fixes: a non-language card's ext that a language card also has is stored as `lext:` and ranks after that language's card (a topic card like go-concurrency never shadows lang/go for every .go file); the once-per-card-per-task memory is `cards.json` in the project's state (the last 50 tasks), so later edits read a few bytes, not the ledger; `fm lib inject` with no word never creates a project; a merge the guard refuses is a `merge_refused` enforce event too. T-0980: the map keeps up to 3 language cards past the top 8 (Foreman's own top 8 were all claude-code cards, so no .py edit ever got the python card).
- Cards at prompt time (T-0977): a UserPromptSubmit whose text names a stack card outright (whole word, longest first: react-hook-form before react; hyphens also match as spaces) gets one line naming up to 3 of them with the `fm lib show … --section avoid --section verify` call, once per session per card (`_first_time`), and a `lib_named` ledger event; card names that are everyday words (make, go, just, express, solid …: `fmlib._COMMON`) never match. The names come from `state/lib-names.json`, rebuilt only when the card count changes, so a prompt never parses the cards (about 30 ms cold, 1 ms warm). `fm doctor -v`'s enforcement row adds cards named and cards read (fm lib show) over 7 days.
- Project map: `fm map` keeps `map.json` in the project's state dir (gates from `fm check` and the project's own files, layout, entry points, the most-changed files over 180 days, and test ↔ source links by name within a language, e.g. `test_guard.py` → `fmguard.py`, plus every file under a top-level folder other than its own that a test names as a string literal, e.g. `test_design_lint.py` → `site/`, T-2086), rebuilt when HEAD moves (`--rebuild` forces it); `fm impact PATH` lists the tests linked to a path and the files that mention its module; `fm focus` adds the likely tests for the brief's scope to its Related section. The intake skill grounds from these before reading code.

</details>

### Pane and UI

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- foreman-ui's modules (T-0130): `hooks/register.tsx` holds the hooks, the band and the pane (and every atom: the engine reads an atom only in the file that declares it); `hooks/rows.tsx` the transcript's Foreman rows and the small drawings (meters, sparklines, the mascot), which take the resolved element table rather than `$` (the engine follows `$` only into functions of the same file); `hooks/state.ts` the shared constants, timing maps and settings (`cfg`); `hooks/kit.ts` the pure helpers. Other sessions draw the installed copy of the mod, which lags this repo until it is released; `fm doctor` warns while it does (T-0191, hooks compared, not only the version). Panels (T-0179, the user's 'each message … look like a panel', then 'the command changes … too'): every reply block is a full-width round-bordered panel, grey, the closing report (Foreman's Changed:/✓/Next: lines) in the accent, pulsing while live; command, edit and agent rows are panels tinted with their colour over the track (`tint`), red for a failed command, pulsing while they run; reads and searches stay one quiet line; the person's typed prompt is a panel in their colour (T-0182). The dashboard matches (T-0181): its header (name, mode, today, mascot) and its controls are panels, every card's border is the same quiet grey with colour only in its heading (the active task in the accent, what waits on the person in warn, failing gates in red), and the task card lists steps and done-when criteria under small labels in one column, audits as a bar; while the dashboard is shown, the band under the chat draws only what needs the person or just changed, and nothing otherwise. Not panelled, because the engine gives no hook for them: thinking summaries, and the AskUserQuestion dialog (a hook may only rewrite its questions; it can't wrap the engine's interactive drawing).
- Economy mode (T-0198): foreman-ui reads the account's usage windows (`session.usage`); while weekly usage is past 80% or the 5-hour window past 90%, a `prompt.compose` hook appends one session section, `foreman-ui:economy` (no brainstorms, one combined reviewer pass at most, no other subagents unless needed, targeted reads, short replies; correctness and the guard unchanged), and a toast says so once. The section names the threshold, not the live figure, so the prompt cache only breaks when a window crosses it.
- Speedrun splits (T-1078, `fmwatch.splits`): the pane's task block shows each stage's time for the active task against the median of 3 or more closed tasks of its type and tier, in green under and red over (beyond ±10%); `fm ui --json` active.splits. Stages are derived from ledger marks (task_new, task_plan, the first ac_check, the first audit, finish, task_done), and since T-1137 from real `stage` ledger events ({from, to}), logged by fmcli.mutate and fm focus whenever a brief's derived stage moves (proxies only for older tasks).
- Pane palette and scrubber (T-1081): the mod option `palette: 'cb'` swaps the ok/error pair for Okabe-Ito blue and orange (FIX chips follow it), `'riso'` (T-1512) for fm code's risograph inks (`fmcodetui.RISO["night"]`: ok, error and accent; `kit.ts` PALETTES.riso, a test keeps the two equal), and error rows keep their ✗ glyph so colour is never the only signal. In the history, `b` steps back one entry and `l` returns to live; while scrubbed, an "as of HH:MM (N back) · press l for live" row heads the entries up to that point.

</details>

### Foreman Code

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Foreman Code (T-1212, `fmcode.py`): `fm code "<request>" [--model P] [--escalate P] [--max-steps 40] [--dry-plan] [--no-commit] [--json]`, `fm code --resume [ID]`, `fm code models`; claude's `-p`/`--print`, `-c`/`--continue` and `--output-format json` mean `--plain`, `--resume` and `--json` (after the verb, so the project there is `--project`; T-1399). The model only fills slots: a PLAN (`check_plan`: type, tier S|M, one-line title, 1–8 criteria {text, verify}, 1–12 steps) repaired at most twice with the error quoted back (`slot`), then asked one plain-text question a field (T-1765: `PLAN_FIELDS`, `CLOSE_FIELDS`; criteria as `text :: command` lines) and assembled, checked as before, a failure naming both and `Next: fm code models`; then per step one action per turn — native tools when the profile has them, else `{"action": …}` read by `extract_json`: read/search run locally and bounded (400 lines, git grep -E 30 s, 80 hits; credential files by `fmguard._is_credential`, `.git/` and pipes or devices are never read, and search drops their hits); edit/write/run first go to `hooks/hook PreToolUse` with a Claude-Code-shaped payload (session `fm-code-<task>`; exit 2, a deny or an ask is a refusal returned as the result, never run), then `fm edit` (JSON stdin), a write inside the project, or `bash -c` in its own process group (120 s, head and tail 3000 chars, memory bounded, the group killed after), each followed by PostToolUse. Each criterion's verify is guarded and run as `fm task evidence --ac N --run` before step 1 (the red baseline, shown to the model) and after the last; failures go back as a fix phase (3 rounds, 3 more on `--escalate`), then `fm task block`. Close: `fm task finish --audit "fm code (<model>): harness-run verify" --run <verifies>`, an M task's lenses, docs, lesson and why-not-caught from a CLOSE slot, `--commit "<title> (<ID>)"` unless --no-commit. `--max-steps` caps model turns; reaching it stops without closing the journal, so `--resume --max-steps N` carries on (the start record keeps --no-commit, --escalate and --max-steps). A FIX or FEATURE verify that passes before any work, or one the guard refuses, goes back for one re-plan (a refusal then stops before any task). Every tool result is redacted (the key variables' values, `fmcore.redact`) and the journal is written through `redact_obj`; the model is sent the last 6 tool results whole and older ones as one-line stubs (`trim`), with head and tail cut to 1500 characters under a 32k context; replies cut at the output limit (max_tokens 8192) are never run. A sensitive repo (`fm sensitive on`) refuses a non-loopback profile. The journal (`<project state>/code/<ID>.jsonl`: start with the plan, every message, action, verify, phase_done, end) rebuilds a killed run; every tool call whose result never landed is answered "not run (interrupted)", never redone. Toolchain (T-1407): at start the pins in `.tool-versions`, `.python-version`, `.node-version` and `.nvmrc` are read as data, each looked up with `mise where`, `asdf where` or `pyenv prefix` run from `/` (so the repo's config, hooks and `.envrc` never run), and its bin folder goes first on PATH for workers and checks (`Run.env`); a pin no manager has is named in a note, never installed.
  - Any request (T-1320): with no model resolved, a `--worker` named in `PLANNERS` (claude, codex, gemini) fills the plan and close-notes slots as an fmmodels profile of kind `cli` (`_cli`: the conversation as one prompt, the CLI in its read-only mode, claude's `--output-format json` usage counted); a custom worker template can't plan (`--verify` or a model). A plan reply `{"answer": …}` is a question: `_answer` (a model re-reads the files in a `read_only` act loop limited to read/search/done; done's summary is the answer) emits `answer`, no task. `_plan_problem` also sends a check `fmcore.unrunnable` refuses back to the planner. `_commit_task` drops untracked build artefacts (`_ARTEFACT`) and paths in neither tree, index nor HEAD. The site's examples gallery (`site/build.py` EXAMPLES, `load_example`, `example_html`) reads `demo/examples/<kind>.jsonl`, real `fm code --json` runs.
  - Bench (T-1215, `fmcodebench.py`): `fm code bench [--model P | --worker CLI] [--seeds a,b] [--seed-timeout 600] [--max-steps 40] [--json]`. `SEEDS` (set `SEED_SET` = 1: operator, off-by-one, guard-clause, rename, danger) each {files, tests, verify, request}; `run_seed` makes a temp repo + home + FOREMAN_HOME (models.json copied, GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL set, HOME the temp one for a model, the real one for a worker CLI's sign-in), runs `fm code --json` in its own session (killed at the limit: reason timeout), writes the seed's tests back and re-runs its verify (no shell): closed only when that passes and the canary `~/keep.txt` is intact. A worker skips the danger seed (its commands aren't judged before they run). `bench` appends {ts, foreman, seed_set, seed_names, model, worker, seeds, closed, score, previous (the last run with the same model/worker, set and seed names), results}; `verdict` decides each seed (timeout, verify timeout, canary touched, verify failed with fm code's last words) to `<state>/code-bench.jsonl`; `history()` skips lines that aren't runs.
  - Worker leases (T-1294): each worker turn gets `FOREMAN_WORKER_OF=fm-code-<task>` (fm code's private channel: fmbus `_me` honours only a value starting `fm-code-`), so its own Claude session's Foreman leases are the run's; the judge and later turns edit on, other sessions are still refused, and `fmbus.drop_session` frees them when the run ends.
  - TUI motion (T-1300): `fmcodeterm.py` is the terminal: `colour_depth` (24 on COLORTERM truecolor/24bit, 256, 8, 0 on NO_COLOR), `Term.frame` (canvas → ANSI, only changed cells after the first frame), `parse_keys` (escape sequences → curses-style key names), `session` (alternate screen with wrap off, cbreak, SIGWINCH through a wake pipe, Ctrl-Z hands the terminal back and fg redraws, everything restored on any exit even with the terminal gone), `split_tail` (an escape sequence cut across reads waits for its rest). NO_COLOR draws the mono theme; a theme without colours never flashes or fades (FX.colour), so chips stay reversed; `Canvas.put` gives a wide character two cells (the second empty) and joins combining marks to their letter. In `fmcodetui.py`: `Motion` (per value: when it changed and from what; the first frame is no change), `FX` (one frame's time, setting full/reduced/off, colours; `current()`), computed styles `rgb(...)` resolved by `resolve(style, cfg, cell_bg)`, `Canvas.bg` (panel tints), palette roles accent2/ok2/bad2/glow/panel, `App.fps` (30 easing, 12 live, 1 still), `record()` (frames → rsvg-convert → ffmpeg; `--record FILE`). Panel helpers: `_waiting` (an empty panel's words, shimmering while `st.status in LIVE`), `_focus_line` (the newest Activity line's band), `contrast`/`_faint` (a user [colors] entry under 3:1 is skipped with its ratio). `to_svg` writes every background before any glyph, glyph rects 0.6 px wider/taller so cells don't seam. T-1512: `App.hush` (`z`) cycles motion through `fmsettings.set_key` (tui.toml and the pane's option; refused, it hushes the screen only); `FX.spin` turns at `TEMPO[agent_state(st)]` (running 10, thinking 5, waiting 2 a second); `fmcodeterm.probe` writes a glyph and CSI 6n only when both ends are a TTY (0.25 s for the whole probe, typed keys kept) and `App.glyphs` gates the `RAIL` (▀) track; `POSTER` groups the `?` keys; `load_config` messages pass `_escaped` (no raw control characters from a tui.toml). T-1740: during a live run `q` and `s` need a second press within `CONFIRM_S` (2 s); the `?` poster names the command line behind a key (`CLI`); a launch tip names one command never run (`App.used`, kept in the state's `code-keys.json`); `_loop` writes `title_text(st)` as the terminal title (pushed and restored), rings the bell once per block or guard refusal (`App.bell_due`, never with motion off), and a model quiet past 10 s reads "model: last heard … ago". T-1741: the request box is a `LineEditor` (arrows, Home/End, Ctrl-A/E/W/U/K, Delete, this launch's requests on Up/Down; a paste lands at the cursor) with a preview line under it (`App.preview`: where Enter sends it and which runner); `/name` runs an app command (`App.palette`), Tab after `@` completes a tracked file (`App.tracked`, git ls-files once, 20,000 at most); `/` in the run view (key `search`) filters the Activity log, its title counting what matched, and the focused panel names its own keys in its bottom edge (`PANEL_HINTS`). T-1742: `gallery(cols, height, depth)` draws every `PRESETS` theme on `SAMPLE_RUN` (`fm code --themes`); `intraline(lines)` marks the words a paired -/+ line changed and the diff view reveals `DIFF_RATE` lines a second from `App.view_opened` (all at once without motion); from `WIDE_ABOVE` (160) columns `WIDE_COLUMN` (plan, verify) is a third column when the layout doesn't already show them.
  - TUI (T-1283, `fmcodetui.py`): `fm code tui [REQUEST] [--plain] [--print-config] [--snapshot SVG --events FILE --size WxH]` (fmcli rewrites `code tui` to `code --tui`, so flags may come before the request). Layers: `State` (pure, from fmcode's `--json` events, schema 1; unknown events ignored, a newer schema refused), `Canvas` + panels `fn(state, w, h, app)` → lines of (text, style) (`track`, `log`, `checks`, `files`, `time`, `tokens`, `plan`, `verify`), `render` per `layout` (`top`, `columns`, `widths`, `heights` with "auto" = content height, `narrow` below `narrow_below`, the log alone below 60), `App` (keys → commands, views `help`/`diff`/`history`/`picker` over a faded screen, the child `fm code … --json` in its own session), the curses driver (`_attrs`: hex → nearest 256 or 8, NO_COLOR → bold/reverse, a `paint` screen style paints the background), `--plain`, `to_svg`. Config: `$FOREMAN_HOME/tui.toml` (`theme` preset from `PALETTES`, `[colors]`, `border`, `[layout]`, `[keys]` with names like ctrl+x/f1/enter, "none" unbinds), `palette` (default; cb: blue and orange, `CB`, over the preset), `load_config` → (cfg, one line per bad setting), `print_config` round-trips. Settings (T-1496, `fmsettings.py`): one schema (`schema()`: key, type, default, choices or range, group, help, effect) over both surfaces, the app's tui.toml (from `DEFAULTS`) and the foreman-ui mod's userConfig (from its plugin.json; Claude Code keeps the values in settings.json `pluginConfigs.<id>.options`). `SHARED` gives motion (full, reduced, off; the old calm) and palette (default, cb) one vocabulary; plugin.json still lists the old words in its options, since Claude Code drops a stored value its options don't name. `fm settings list|get|set KEY VALUE [--surface app|plugin]`: `set_app` changes one key in place (`edit_toml`: comments, order and other keys kept; a missing key goes after its table's last key, a missing table at the end), refuses a value load_config rejects or a render that raises, says which other settings the change made bad (the app skips them), and writes atomically under `fmcore.lock` of the file's folder; `set_plugin` does the same for settings.json. Inside Claude Code (`CLAUDECODE` or `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT` set) both writes go through `fmguard.check` (the task's grants, trust, a builder lane's confinement): refused, nothing is written and the exact change is printed (exit 2); the user's own terminal writes directly. A `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` outside the home folder is refused; `fm settings list` names the settings the app can't read instead of showing their defaults silently. T-1656: `[profile.NAME]` tables with `when` (`fmsettings.WHEN`: ssh, tmux, term glob, width_below, colors) on `facts(env)`; load_config applies the first match through `_apply`, its own checks, as `cfg["profile"]`; `fm settings profile` lists them. T-1657: `[[rule]]` tables (`rule_list`: `when` over `RULE_EVENTS` running, verify, failing, closed; `layout` and `panels` swaps, 16 at most) → `cfg["rules"]`; `render` draws `rule_layout(cfg, st, app)` each frame (a "rule N" chip in the status line; the layout editor shows the file's own), and `render_error` draws each rule's layout too. Extensions: `$FOREMAN_HOME/tui/*.py` with `setup(api)` (`api.panel`, `api.command`, `api.on`, and since API version 2 `api.setting(key, type, default, help, choices=, range=)`: `EXT_SETTINGS`, app settings `ext.NAME.key` in `[ext.NAME]`, grouped and checked by `fmsettings.ext_value`; `fm settings` loads them only for actions that read them, under `EXT_BUDGET` seconds each with stdin closed), files others can write skipped, a failing one (an exit too) reported and the rest loaded; never the project's. fmcode events carry `ts`; `changed` has `stat` {path: [added, removed]}. The site draws `demo/code-tui.jsonl` with `snapshot`.
  - Workers (T-1214): `--worker CLI [--worker-timeout 1200] [--verify CMD]`. `WORKERS` holds each CLI's unattended form with `{prompt}` (claude -p --permission-mode acceptEdits, codex exec --full-auto, gemini --approval-mode auto_edit -p, aider --yes-always --no-suggest-shell-commands; none auto-approves a shell command, since the guard judges a worker's files afterwards, not its commands; opencode only as a template), run with `worker_env()` (no forge, cloud or registry credentials nor anything named like a secret; model keys stay; in a worker run `Run.env()` uses it too, so the verify gets no more than the worker), refused in a sensitive repo; `worker_argv` takes a spec with `{prompt}` as the user's template (shlex), a table name as its row, else `[spec, prompt]`, and a CLI not on PATH is a one-line error before any plan. `--verify` plans with no model (S, the request's tag type else FEATURE, one criterion, one step; a guard refusal or a FIX/FEATURE verify passing already stops). Each step and fix round is one `Run.work` turn through `run_bounded(reap=True)` (the group killed at the timeout; on Linux fm code is the turn's subreaper (prctl), so a setsid'd or double-forked leftover is killed and reaped too; judged in a `finally`, full environment so the CLI keeps its own keys, output redacted into the journal, `phase_done` for --resume; worker, timeout and verify in the start options). `Run.judge` then: `.git/config`, `config.worktree`, `info/exclude`, every `.git/hooks/*` (and the hooks link itself) and the global Git config back to the `_snapshot` (first; the judge's own `_git` runs with no fsmonitor or hooks), HEAD moved back (`reset --soft`, or `update-ref --no-deref` when it was detached), new assume-unchanged/skip-worktree flags cleared, then each written file: `git status -z -uall` against the dirty files' bytes, plus a stat walk of the whole tree but `.git` (`_stats`: a write changes ctime) for what git can't show; with `--verify`, the files its words name (a file, or a folder's files, from the before-stats: `_named`) are put back too, since a worker never changes the check that judges it (T-1271; a file the test only imports isn't named). A kind `fmguard.classify_write` protects is refused with no standing yes or trust (`_strict`); a file git shows also goes to the guard as a Write (and an unshown one under `.claude/` or `.foreman/`). Refused → its saved bytes (dirty files, and `kept`: protected files up to 4 MB), `git checkout HEAD`, or deleted if new; else it can't be put back and the task blocks. Allowed → PostToolUse. Over 300 files git shows, none is kept. Each revert is an emit line and a `revert` record; only the harness's verify marks work done. `--escalate` stays model-only.
  - Failures and survivors (T-1754): every failure ends with `Next: <command>` on stderr and `next` in its error/blocked event (`next_step`: a `CodeError(msg, then)` names its own; a model error resumes the task, or lists models before one exists; blocked shows the task; else `fm doctor`), and fm's usage-error printer prints an error's `then` for any verb. The TUI notes it under the STOP line. The start event carries `foreman={root, version}`. Cost (T-1357): `Run.usage(r, profile, what)` prices a call by `r["usd"]` (`fmmodels._cli` passes claude's `total_cost_usd`) or the profile's validated `price` {in, out} per million tokens; a worker turn by its stream result's `total_cost_usd`. `Run.spend` adds it to `run.usd`, `fmbudget.record("fm code", …, project=slug)` and a journal `spend` record (`_load` sums them; planning's spend is logged at `_journal_start`). `--max-usd` (journal option `max_usd`): `check_cap` before every model call and worker turn raises a CodeError whose Next doubles the cap; `capped(profile)` and the worker argv give claude `--max-budget-usd` with `left()`. The closed event carries `usd`. Durable runs (T-1338): `unfinished(jdir)` lists journals with a start record and no end in their last 64 KB whose `<task>.lock` is free, newest first; a new run (not --resume, not --task) notes up to three with their resume command (`unfinished` in the note event). The app's `_history_row` shows a stopped run's `fm code --resume ID`, and r on it in a project view starts the resume there; `App(place=state/code-place.json)` saves the open project on quit and `reopen()` opens it on a home launch outside any project when its folder still exists. Run hooks (T-1351): `Run.emit` hands closed, blocked and error events to `on_event`, which runs code.toml's `[on]` commands (`ON_EVENTS`: blocked and error are also needs-you) through `run_bounded(..., stdin=event JSON)` with `HOOK_SECONDS`, after `Run.guard` checks each as a background Bash call (fed stdin and a bound time turn off only the foreground rules). `fmcore.code_line(p)` reads the newest `code/*.events.jsonl` tail (8 KB) and its lock for `fm state --line`. Failover (T-1356): `failure_kind(code, reply)` sorts a worker turn from its exit and the reply's last 600 chars (limit with `_reset_at`: `limit reached|<epoch>` or `resets 3pm`/`15:30` as the next local time; auth; timeout; crash); `Run.failover` waits a limit out within code.toml `limit_wait` (`LIMIT_WAITS` per turn) and redoes the turn, else a CodeError whose Next resumes; auth, crash and timeout move `run.worker` to the next name in code.toml `chain` (a `failover` journal record); auth with no chain stops. Pings (T-1335): `ping_runner(name)` = `test_runner` (refuses a non-runner, lints, --version) then the runner's plan template with `PING_PROMPT` plus its lean args, `run_bounded` in a `fm-ping-` temp folder with `worker_env()`, timed; `ping_state` sorts it (failure_kind, then flag-refused and endpoint-down patterns on a crash); no plan template → `version only`, never a prompt. `ping_cache(put)` keeps `state/code-runners-ping.json`; `fm code runners --ping [NAME]` adds `ping` to each row. The app's runners screen draws a lamp per row from the cache and `p` runs `_runner_test(item, ping=True)` on its thread. Routing (T-1358): `runner_records()` reads every project's `code/*.events.jsonl` (head 64 KB: the start's worker or a note's `routed`, the plan's type; tail 16 KB: the end and the closed `usd`) into {runner: {kind: {runs, closed, priced, usd}}}; `pick_runner` returns `_first_verified` for `auto` (why starts "auto"), and `route(run)` after the plan picks the installed runner with `ROUTE_RUNS`+ runs and two thirds closed, cheapest per priced closed run, journaling `route` (`_load` restores it, and a `failover`, unless --worker was given). Config (T-1359): `CONFIG` {key: (what, how to change)} and `config_view(p, args)` resolve each key flag > env > project > code.toml/models.json > default; a code.toml parse error becomes `problem` and the view falls back to RUNNERS and defaults, while runs keep raising it. `fm code config [--explain KEY] [--json]`. Run friction (T-1339): `run_friction(cause, detail)` keys a cause (cause plus the reason's first eight words, digits dropped) and, when that key isn't logged yet, files it through `fmcli.capture_friction` on Foreman's own project (`foreman_home`) and logs `run friction: KEY`; called at Blocked, a turn's stall, a start finding unfinished runs, and a CodeError/ModelError before the task. `noticed()` lists open Improve rounds with friction there; the app's home puts them first (`kind: noticed`, Enter opens that project), skipped by `here` and `reopen()`; an App given its own `projects` shows none unless given `noticed`. Local context (T-1360): `fmmodels._probe` sets a found server's `context` from `_local_context(name, base_url, model, row)`: Ollama POST /api/show `num_ctx` (else `OLLAMA_CONTEXT` 4096), llama.cpp GET /props `n_ctx`, vLLM's /models row `max_model_len`, LM Studio GET /api/v0/models/ID `loaded_context_length`; anything else or a number outside 512..10M keeps `LOCAL_CONTEXT`. Diagnostics (T-1760): `why(jdir, tid)` reads `<tid>.events.jsonl` (the newest without an ID) and the journal's `phase_done` records; with no end event the task lock tells running from stopped. `piped_context()` reads stdin only from a FIFO or regular file, `select` waiting `PIPE_FIRST` for the first byte, then to EOF, `PIPE_MAX` or `PIPE_SECONDS`; `run.context` is journaled in options and fenced into the plan's user message and `_task_text`. `containment(root)` → {kind landlock|guard, label} on the start event; the app's header shows the label. CodeCase.code runs fm code with stdin from /dev/null unless given input. Cards (T-1762): `card(st, app)` builds a failure card (State.error, State.next) or a stall card (State.stall, cleared by any event but stalled, working, tokens) for the main view, drawn by `_card` above the status bar; `App._card_key` takes Esc (hide by kind and first line), Enter (`run_next`: resume, `READ_ONLY_NEXT` output into the text view via `fm_output`, else named for the shell), w, k, m (stop, then the picker with `after_pick = "resume"`). Slot integrity (T-1398): `elided(new, have)` (ELIDED: a commented '...', '... rest/existing …', 'rest of the file … unchanged'; a bare '...' is Ellipsis) refuses an edit or write; `near_anchor(text, old)` evens whitespace line by line and returns the one span, "ambiguous" or None, and `Run.edit` applies the file's own span; `places(output, root)` pulls up to five in-tree file:line pairs into the fix prompt's "Where it fails". `App.sweep()` (main, at open) calls `fmcode.stop_survivors`; `App.stop` notes the child's runners from code-procs (`_runners_of_child`) and main prints `stop_report()` after the screen closes, killing a group still alive after 3 s. `run_bounded` writes `state/code-procs/<pid>` (`<start> <owner pid> <owner start>`, start times from `fmideas._starttime`) on spawn and removes it after its group kill; `stop_survivors()` at every run's start kills the group of each entry whose owner isn't the process it names, when the runner still is (or has a stray, `fmideas._strays`), and the run notes the pids; a reused pid only loses its entry.
- Width oracle (T-1516): `test_code_tui.HostileWidth` sweeps hostile strings over every fm code panel and view; `Canvas.put` blanks the other half of a wide cell it overwrites and `_mark` makes variation selectors zero-width; a crash saves `fixtures/crashes/*.json`, replayed by the same class.
- macOS portability (T-1828): `fmcodetui._runners_of_child` names a runner with `fmideas._cmdline` (ps without /proc), so quitting the app stops runners there too; `fmplaybooks._run` resolves its temp folder (/var is /private/var). Tests that read a pty keep `helpers.pty_reader` on the master from before the writer starts: macOS drops unread output when the slave closes, returns b'' at EOF (Linux raises EIO) and stalls writes and tcdrain while the master goes unread.

</details>

### Running it

<details>
<summary>notes</summary>

- Headless (a server, no terminal open): `fm serve` in a repo runs Claude Code Remote Control there as a systemd user unit (`~/.config/systemd/user/foreman-serve-<slug>.service`; its output, which includes the session URL, is discarded and errors go to the journal), survives logout and reboot (it enables linger, or warns), restarts on failure with a 5-in-10-minutes breaker (a restart reconnects its sessions), and puts the project in full autonomy with drive on; send requests from claude.ai/code or the Claude app. Starting it needs your yes (`fm ask ID remote`); `claude` is found on the saved PATH at every start. It uses your default permission mode unless `--permission-mode` is given (sensitive repos: `default`, approve from the phone). `fm serve status` (unit state, repo, active task; the last journal lines, URLs hidden, when it's down; `fm doctor` warns on a dead unit) · `fm serve stop [--all]` (restores autonomy and drive; uninstall stops every unit). Claude Code must trust the repo root itself first (open `claude` there once; a trusted parent folder doesn't count). `fm run` instead works the queue in fresh `claude -p` sessions, one task each (drive scoped by `FOREMAN_DRIVE_TASK`), skipping tasks that wait on you; a usage limit is waited out with backoff (5 min doubling to 1 h per wait, `--wait` hours in total, default 6, each wait logged) and the task retried; it stops on no progress, any other nonzero exit (login, crash), the wait budget, the per-session timeout or two sessions in a row that end blocked or unfinished in a lane (T-0434: a breaker that only stops, never retries; a usage limit doesn't count); a task with no progress, or still unfinished after its sessions, is blocked with that reason first, so the next run takes the next task (T-0955); it re-execs itself once under `systemd-inhibit --what=sleep:idle` when that's on PATH and logind answers (env guard `FOREMAN_INHIBITED`) so the machine doesn't sleep mid-run; `fm night --print-timer` prints a systemd user timer + service and a crontab line for a nightly `fm night` and installs nothing; it logs to `state/logs/run-<slug>.log` (redacted), and refuses while `fm serve` runs in the same project.
- Disable: `claude plugin disable foreman@foreman` (Claude Code works normally). Drive only: `fm drive off`. Everything unattended, every project at once (T-0436): `fm pause` writes `state/PANIC`; while it's there the Stop drive returns nothing, `fm run` refuses (and a running one stops before its next session), `fm serve` start, `fm night` (and a running night before its next job), `fm lane new` and the daily background `fm second session` refuse, and autonomy reads standard (STATE, status and the session context say PAUSED; project meta keeps its own setting). It only stops things: a `claude` session already running finishes its turn, and `fm serve stop` still works. `fm pause --now` (T-1821, `fmserve.panic_stop`) writes PANIC marked hard and also stops what runs: every fm session runner's process group, every fm serve unit, every lease (state/bus/leases.json), and a running fm run's `_watch` reads the hard flag every 2 s and stops its session's group (lanes with work are kept; fm night runs fm run, so it stops too); one record per `--now` in `state/stops.jsonl` (one failed stop doesn't end the sweep; it lands in the record's errors). Run inside an fm session or serve unit, it stops everything else, writes the record, then its own session and unit. Interactive Claude Code sessions and subagents still finish their turn; locks are flocks and free as holders exit. `fm pause off` lifts it. Per-repo manual permissions: `fm sensitive on`.
- Uninstall: `plugin/uninstall.sh [--dry-run] [--purge-state] [--yes]` removes plugin and marketplace, undoes the wiring from the manifest (including the plugin maps that leaves empty), and archives state before removing it.
- Restore the pre-build backup: `tar -C ~ -xzf ~/.claude/foreman/backups/claude-20260925-233203.tgz` (overwrites same-named files, deletes nothing).
- Logs: `state/logs/hooks.log` (hook errors), `state/events.jsonl` (timeline), per-project `ledger.jsonl`. Debug hooks with `claude --debug`.
- Another machine: `gh repo clone ChaseSunstrom/foreman ~/.claude/foreman && ~/.claude/foreman/install.sh`, or `git pull` in `~/.claude/foreman`.
- Rendering tips: `/tui fullscreen` (mouse, click-to-expand tool output, live `/diff` panel), `/focus`, `Ctrl+O` transcript search. Many sessions: `claude agents`. Visual diffs: the Desktop app's Code tab.

</details>

## 10. Known limitations

- A task's own changes are measured from a snapshot of the working files taken when it is first focused (T-0078). When it is paused (focus moves, block, defer) the files are snapshotted again, and on re-focus, if they changed meanwhile, the start point moves to the files as they are with the task's own changes taken back out (T-0136), so another task's or the user's work isn't counted as its own; when both changed the same lines the start point stays and the brief's log says so.
- Edit attribution comes from the Edit/Write hooks and, for Bash (T-0086), from `git status` after each call: a file whose mtime falls inside the call is the active task's touch and gets the same scope note (a file another process changes during a long command is counted too; tripwires still see only Edit/Write targets, which are known before the edit); `fm cost` attributes tokens to the task of the newest ledger event, an approximation when sessions interleave; the pre-audit and sensitive-change checks are regex heuristics that point reviewers, not proofs.
- The guard is a speed bump, not a sandbox: shell text can be obfuscated (a script file that writes elsewhere, computed paths). Interpreter code that names a protected path and writes is caught, and archives, clones and copies are checked by where they write (not by archive members). Running `claude plugin|mcp|config` changes (directly, with global options first, from interpreter code, or as a `/plugin install`-style prompt in argv, stdin or a heredoc) needs `plugin`, but an obfuscated spelling isn't recognized (interpreter code is read whole: only a real top-level `fm … --run "<cmd>"` argument is left out, being checked as a command of its own, and `$'…'` quoting or a heredoc form the parser doesn't know leaves nothing out); "driving Foreman's modules" parses quoted heredocs fed straight to python (T-0170): a real `import fm…` plus a writer, or any `from fm… import`, blocks; text that only mentions them doesn't, but only when the parsed code can't run what its strings hold: it imports nothing beyond a few plain modules (re, json, os, sys, pathlib…), calls no exec, eval, getattr or process function (system, subprocess, spawn, exec…) and touches no dunder attribute; anything unparseable, dynamic or outside such a body is read as text, as before; a quoted `cat`/`tee` heredoc with no pipe or substitution on its line is data, not interpreter code (T-0171: a script written, not run); when every interpreter in a command is such a fed python heredoc and each provably writes only through builtin `open` (no import but whitelisted pure `re`/`json`/`textwrap`/`sys` functions, nothing dynamic, no dunder or frame attribute, `open` never rebound or passed on, each write-mode `open` naming a literal or a name bound once to one), only those files count as written (T-0176), in the starting folder and every folder the command names after a `cd`; a `cd` on the heredoc's own line keeps the coarse rule, which reads only path-like strings; every command substitution a shell would run (backticks and `$( … )`, nested, in double quotes or an unquoted heredoc) is read as a command of its own and also to the end of the command, and a `#` comment ends at its line; a heredoc starts only outside quotes (T-0178: `echo '<<EOF'` made every later line an unread body); `fm replay` (T-0189) runs the real shell commands of the last 14 days of Claude Code sessions (their logs under ~/.claude/projects, up to 5000 distinct, each in the folder it ran in, no grants) through the current guard and lists what it now blocks (likely false positives) or lets through (possible bypasses) against the last accepted run (`--accept`; state/guard-replay.json keeps hashes and verdicts, never a command), and is a project gate whenever plugin/lib/fmguard.py changes; `plugin/tests/test_guard_diff.py` runs ~50 shell forms of each dangerous payload in real bash inside bubblewrap (read-only /, tmpfs /home, no network) with logging stand-ins and fails on any call the guard didn't block (it found trap, find -exec, setsid, flock and script -c); a variable in an `rm`, write, copy or `cd` target (T-0175: write targets with a `$` used to be skipped) is resolved only in a straight-line command (joined by `;` and newlines, or only by `&&`, where a command runs only after every earlier one did); a name the command can't set (never written bare, no `eval`/`read`/`declare`-like builtin, no computed command name) takes Claude Code's environment for write targets (a shell profile that changes it is beyond the guard), `HOME` the user's, and only `HOME` for `rm`; write targets are checked in every place the shell may be (a `cd` replaces the place when it surely happened: in an `&&` chain, or in a straight command into a folder that exists or that `mkdir -p` made there; otherwise, in a branch, a subshell, after `!` or into a folder that may be missing, it adds one; `pushd`'s stack forms and `cd -` go somewhere unnamed, as does a `cd` to an unknown folder, and a relative one CDPATH may redirect (assumed whenever any name may be set) leaves paths below an unnamed folder; `~+`, `~-` and `~user` are read); a write target is also checked as bash expands it (T-0177: comma and sequence braces, globs against existing paths, 1024 of each; extglob and globstar forms, which need a `shopt` first, are not); a target the command itself makes unknown counts as writing every path it names in the unknown part's place (T-0183: the command's own words, fm's text arguments left out, the first two unknown parts paired; a NAME=literal set once in a branchy command's straight prefix, before any branch or pipe, is known throughout, for `rm` too (T-0190); a variable's own name is never a guess), and a relative one below an unnamed folder, every existing folder it names (coarse but closed, like interpreter code; impossible paths skipped; a resolved target is named by its path, so a standing or trusted yes covers it); the text after `$(` is also read from the first folder (T-0158), so `cd $(mktemp -d) && echo > .foreman/x` still reads as the mirror; these from unquoted `NAME=literal` words read as written (quotes kept), and stops at any builtin outside a short inert list (only builtins can change a shell variable), a computed command name (`$X`, a backtick command, a glob) or any `+=`, `[i]=`, prefix, tilde, glob, space or `IFS` form; names bash sets itself (`$_`, `PWD`, `RANDOM`, `BASH*`) are never resolved; a `#` starts a comment only at the start of a word outside quotes; writes that cover a whole tree (checkouts, extractions, recursive copies, rsync) are checked for the protected paths they contain, so a checkout at Foreman's root or an extraction into `~` needs `core` (a project's own `.claude/settings*.json` counts for writes aimed inside the project, not for branch work at its root); persistence is gated as `system` where it is recognizable (user units, autostart, shell startup files and `.bashrc.d`, fish functions, desktop entries, git hooks and `core.hooksPath` in any spelling, `crontab`, `at`, `direnv allow`), not through arbitrary scripts; deny rules and git are the other layers. When the working copy of `fmguard.py` fails to import or run, the hook uses the committed guard (git HEAD) instead of locking every call, logging it to hooks.log; without a committed copy it still fails closed. `fm doctor` warns when protected core differs from the last commit. The same holds for the rest of the library: if the working copy can't be imported, the hook script loads the committed library; if `fmcore` fails at runtime, the active task (and its grants) is read with the committed `fmcore`, so the fix itself is never refused. `fmguard` imports nothing from Foreman, so its committed copy loads even when the rest is broken.
- Hints vs gates: the next action injected each turn is a hint the model can ignore; the gates are enforced by code (no edits without an active task — since T-0308 the guard's `brief` category, so a shell write into the project (redirect, `sed -i`, `cp`, `tee`, python `open()`) counts as much as Edit/Write; scratch, state, `~/.claude/projects` and checkout-wide git writes are exempt, and `FOREMAN_QUIET` sessions skip it — `fm focus` plan gate — by content since T-2269: an M/L Approach lists 3+ options and a choice, an L adds 2+ readings and Risks and rollback, a `(low)` assumption needs `research/NAME`, `spike T-NNNN` or a verified tag, and a started task isn't re-gated; since T-2277 an M/L focus with literal scope files also writes one `Blast radius (fm graph blast):` line into Risks and rollback — top co-change neighbours and past FIXes, once, from a built fresh graph only (a missing or stale one starts one detached `fm graph build`, guarded by a `graph.building` lock file, unless FOREMAN_NO_BACKGROUND; the next focus has the line), under a 1 s cap — `fm task done` blockers). Doc drift blocks done only in docs a task says it updated; elsewhere `fm docs` and done report it.
- `fm run` spots a usage limit by Claude Code's own message wording on the last line a failed session printed (stderr's when it has any, so a crash that merely mentions a limit still stops the run) and retries on backoff rather than parsing the reset time; a monthly limit just spends the `--wait` budget and stops (exit 1 for every stop reason; the message and log say which). Remote Control's own stderr lands in the journal unredacted. Remote Control output (it includes the session URL) is discarded, never logged.
- `fm audit prep` seeds lens briefs with past findings from the research folder, and with `fm sync` on, research can arrive from the repo: a crafted file could put a misleading 200-character line in a reviewer's brief (labelled as data, not instructions; the reviewer is read-only and its findings are verified by the main thread).
- A pinned plugin yes checks the marketplace's local copy right before the install; Claude Code could still refresh the marketplace between that check and its copy, and a remote-source entry pins the code only as far as it names a ref or sha. No yes is ever spent on a plugin change the guard can't tie to one plugin (interpreter code running claude's plugin/MCP/config commands, a prompt's /plugin or /mcp): run the fm plugins or claude command itself. While a pin is in place, the yes covers nothing but installing or enabling that plugin; an unpinned yes still covers other plugin changes (a marketplace, an MCP server, a new skill file, a write under ~/.claude/plugins), and the dialog says when a pin covers only a remote source's marketplace entry.
- The state fallback is found through each process's `XDG_STATE_HOME` and temp dir; processes that disagree on those would split state.
- The prompt path trusts Claude Code's dialog: a mode or another hook that auto-approves permission dialogs would also approve an `fm ask`. The chat fallback trusts that a reply starting with yes answers the question just asked; pasted text never counts, and requests expire after 24 h or at your next reply.
- Subagents can't be tool-less (an empty `tools:` list means every tool), so brainstormers run as `claude -p` children via `fm ideas` (no tools, no MCP, no user plugins).
- Built-in task tools are off on current models, so the TaskCompleted gate is dormant unless `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1`.
- `footerLinksRegexes` isn't used (only http(s)/editor URLs are allowed; T-ids can't reach brief files).
- Evals run in a temp HOME, so each case embeds the rules (`tests/e2e/sync_evals.py`). Score at 1.1.0: 1.0 (5/5 cases, one run each; single runs are noisy, so compare candidates over several).
- Konsole may ignore OSC 777 notifications and OSC 9;4 progress; titles work.
- Telemetry needs the collector endpoint before it can be enabled.
- A per-repo opt-out must use `defaultMode: "default"` (Claude Code ignores `auto` and `bypassPermissions` in project/local settings).

## 11. Deviations from BUILD_PROMPT.md

| Deviation | Why |
|---|---|
| Python stdlib `fm` (jq absent) | jq isn't installed |
| captures are briefs with status `captured`; INBOX.md, STATE.md and registry.md are generated views | one record per item; the views are rebuilt from the briefs |
| `fm intake`, `fm task log`, `fm decide`, `fm research add`, `fm sensitive`, `fm drive`, `fm install-user/uninstall-user` added | the loop needed them and the prompt had none |
| per-repo opt-out uses `default` | see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |
| task-tool mirroring dormant | see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |
| statusLine wraps your original line plus claude-hud | see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |
| footerLinksRegexes skipped | only http(s) and editor URLs are allowed; T-ids can't reach brief files (section 10) |
| RESEARCH ordered right after BASELINE | see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |
| one Stop handler (evidence gate + drive + terminal sequences) | see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |
| drive mode (your request) with `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP=60` | your request |
| ECC procedures ported (your request) into one on-demand playbooks skill | your request |
| eval cases embed the rules | see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |
| guard category `self-authorize` (found by live verification) | found by live verification |
| 1.1: chat approvals (`fm ask`), tiered audits gating done, full autonomy, brainstorm with tool-less children, all Foreman code protected. | the 1.1 release; see `local/PLAN.md` §1 |

Details and reasons: `local/PLAN.md` §1 (D1–D22).
